\section{Preliminaries and the preservation levels}
\label{sec:prelim}
\label{app:framework}

\textbf{Federated learning.} $N$ clients each hold a local dataset $\mathcal{D}_i \sim P_i$; the server learns $w^* = \arg\min_w \sum_i \frac{|\mathcal{D}_i|}{|\mathcal{D}|} F_i(w)$. In FedAvg~\citep{mcmahan2017communication}, each round the server broadcasts $w^t$, selected clients run local SGD, and the server aggregates $w^{t+1} = w^t + \frac{1}{|S_t|}\sum_{i \in S_t} \Delta_i^t$.

\textbf{Poisoning and robust aggregation.} An adversary controlling a fraction $f$ of clients can flip labels~\citep{chen2017targeted}, inject backdoor triggers~\citep{bagdasaryan2020backdoor,xie2019dba}, or scale malicious updates to dominate aggregation~\citep{bagdasaryan2020backdoor}. The server may replace FedAvg with a Byzantine-robust rule: Krum~\citep{blanchard2017machine}, Trimmed Mean, Coordinate-wise Median, Norm Clipping~\citep{sun2019can}, or RFA (geometric median)~\citep{pillutla2022robust}, each imposing an accuracy/robustness trade-off.

\textbf{Game theory.} A normal-form game is $G = (\mathcal{N}, \{\mathcal{S}_i\}, \{u_i\})$; an equilibrium is \emph{pure} when every player's strategy puts all its mass on one action and \emph{mixed} otherwise. \textbf{No claim of this paper is game-theoretic}: this is needed only to read the residual scale check of Appendix~\ref{app:scale}, and the type-space and Bayes-Nash machinery an earlier version used is not needed here.

\section{The screen, the identification boundary, and the proofs}
\label{sec:composability_criterion}
\label{app:conditions}

The paper's central practical question is \emph{which} defense pairs compose well under deterministic joint application. Specifically, the criterion screens for \emph{mechanism-preserving composition}: when one constituent defense is already individually effective (C1), can the other defense be added without breaking that suppression? This is the operationally relevant question (the criterion is not a general characterization of all composable pairs, but a sufficient condition for \emph{mechanism preservation} among pairs where at least one component has known effectiveness), never a sufficient condition for low ASR. We answer it first, with a checkable criterion, and treat the alternative, temporal mixing, as outside the scope of this paper. \textbf{But the practical question is not what the paper establishes}: what it establishes is that preservation of the statistic the screen checks is not causally tied to the security the screen predicts, so the criterion below should be read as the application §\ref{sec:condition_scoring} scopes it to (an ordering over an evaluation budget) and not as a deployment recommendation.

Observe that each robust aggregation defense $d$ operates through an \emph{admission signal} $\sigma(u)$ applied to client update $u$: it accepts, clips, or down-weights $u$ based on whether $\sigma(u)$ exceeds a threshold. Crucially, the admission signal varies by defense family:
\begin{itemize}[noitemsep,topsep=2pt]
\item \textbf{Weighting defenses} (reputation, FoolsGold): $\sigma(u) = $ distance from consensus ($\ell_2$) or pairwise similarity.
\item \textbf{Clipping defenses} (NormClip): $\sigma(u) = \|u\|_2$.
\item \textbf{Rank-based defenses} (TrimmedMean, CoordMedian): $\sigma_k(u) = $ coordinate-wise rank of $u_k$.
\item \textbf{Geometric defenses} (RFA): $\sigma(u) = $ distance from geometric median.
\end{itemize}
When two defenses are applied sequentially (d1 transforms the updates, d2 aggregates the result), d1's transformation may \emph{corrupt} d2's signal or vice versa.

\begin{criterion}[Signal-Preservation Composability: an evaluation prioritizer, never a security screen (L2)]
\label{thm:composability}
Let $d_1$ and $d_2$ be defense mechanisms. The following sufficient conditions guarantee \textbf{preservation of $d_2$'s suppression mechanism under composition, conditional on the standalone suppression C1 asserts}. They are \textbf{not} an independent guarantee of low composition ASR: Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}, which proves them for one class, bounds no ASR. Stated once so the scope cannot be misread:
\begin{center}\small\vspace{-3pt}
C1 $\wedge$ C2 $\wedge$ C3 $\;\Rightarrow\;$ $d_2$'s suppression \emph{mechanism} survives composition, levels (P1)--(P3) of Def.~\ref{def:levels_full}.\\[1pt]
C1 $\wedge$ C2 $\wedge$ C3 $\;\nRightarrow\;$ low composition ASR.
\vspace{-3pt}\end{center}
\noindent The conditions are of \textbf{two different kinds}, and we group them accordingly, because conflating an empirical screen with a mechanistic property is the main source of confusion about what the criterion claims.

\smallskip
\noindent\textbf{Stage 0: applicability.} Before either kind of condition is informative, the question the criterion asks has to be live.
\begin{description}[noitemsep,topsep=2pt]
\item[(C0) The downstream mechanism is load-bearing.] $\mathrm{ASR}(d_1,a) \geq 0.5$ for at least one committed attack $a$. C2 and C3 concern preservation of \emph{$d_2$'s} admission signal, so they say nothing when $d_1$ alone already suppresses everything: the composition inherits that suppression, destroying $d_2$'s signal costs nothing, and a correct prediction would be correct for a reason the criterion did not supply. The asymmetry is deliberate: a strong \emph{$d_2$} is the intended case, since asking whether upstream processing breaks an effective downstream defense is the framework's whole question, whereas a strong $d_1$ makes the question moot. C0 was \textbf{formulated post-hoc} and its provenance is in Remark~\ref{rem:c0}.
\end{description}

\smallskip
\noindent\textbf{The empirical screening condition.} This one is measured rather than read off definitions, and it is the gate the identification boundary is about.
\begin{description}[noitemsep,topsep=2pt]
\item[(C1) Standalone suppression.] Some constituent suppresses the committed attack on its own: $\min_{d\in\{d_1,d_2\}}\mathrm{ASR}(d,a) < 0.5$, quantified \emph{per attack}. With C0 this forces the suppressing constituent to be $d_2$ \emph{on the attack $a^*$ that witnesses C0}, and there only (Lemma~\ref{lem:testability}), which is exactly what makes the gate a descendant of the quantity the screen is trying to estimate (Proposition~\ref{prop:identification_full}). \textbf{On a committed attack that $d_1$ happens to suppress, the constituent doing the work can be $d_1$ instead}, because C0 is existential over attacks while C1 is universal over them; both of our certified pairs are of that kind, and §\ref{app:validation_full} reports what that costs.
\end{description}

\smallskip
\noindent\textbf{Mechanism-preservation conditions.} These are what Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} proves for the weighting$\to$aggregator class, and they are determined by $d_1$'s and $d_2$'s definitions alone, no experiment required. \textbf{What we recommend is an \emph{evaluation-prioritization criterion}, C1$\wedge$C2, because that is the whole of what our evidence supports.} We do call it a \emph{screen} throughout, in the ordinary sense of a cheap test run before an expensive one. \textbf{We disclaim predictive validity explicitly, here at first use}: the criterion orders candidate compositions for evaluation, and it does \emph{not} predict whether a composition is secure. The pooled two-channel model over $26$ cells is unconfirmed ($R^2{=}0.239$), and our own pre-registered replication \textbf{refuted} the cross-arm admission ordering the criterion would need in order to predict (§\ref{app:targeted_full}); recall is $40\%$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:emergent_full}), so the negative direction carries no information either. \textbf{It is never a security screen, and a PASS is an evaluation ordering rather than a deployment decision} (Table~\ref{tab:scope_levels}). C3 is retained as a \textbf{margin diagnostic} rather than a gate, because on our data it never binds on a pair C1$\wedge$C2 already admits and shows no incremental predictive value (§\ref{sec:condition_scoring}). \emph{The pre-registered labels of §\ref{sec:composability_validation} were frozen with C3 in the gate and are not relabelled}; the demotion is a recommendation going forward, supported by a post-hoc factorial ablation, and it changes no reported number: C1$\wedge$C2 selects exactly the same two in-distribution pairs.
\begin{description}[noitemsep,topsep=2pt]
\item[(C2) Signal preservation.] $d_1$'s per-client transformation $T_1$ does not destroy $d_2$'s admission signal: $\sigma_2(T_1(u_a)) \approx \sigma_2(u_a)$ for malicious updates $u_a$.
\item[(C3) Boundary preservation.] $d_1$'s transformation does not compress the adversarial--benign separation below $d_2$'s discrimination threshold. Formally: if $d_2$ suppresses $a$ by detecting $\tau_{d_2}(u_a) > \theta$ (triggering trimming/rejection), then $\tau_{d_2}(T_1(u_a)) > \theta$ must hold, i.e., $d_1$ must not map adversarial updates from above to below $d_2$'s rejection boundary.
\end{description}
When C1 holds but C2 fails (signal destruction), composition is no better than $d_2$ alone. When C1 and C2 hold but C3 fails (boundary compression), $d_2$ retains its admission signal direction but its threshold no longer fires: the attack passes because $d_1$'s rescaling places adversarial magnitudes within $d_2$'s acceptance region.
\end{criterion}

Writing C2 as preservation of $d_2$'s admission signal elides a distinction our own experiments later force, so we name the levels before relying on the condition. They are the paper's main conceptual instrument, and every claim below is stated at one of them.

\begin{definition}[Preservation levels]
\label{def:levels_full}
For an upstream transform $T_1$ and a downstream defense $d_2$ reading admission signal $\sigma_2$:
\begin{enumerate}[noitemsep,topsep=2pt,label=(P\arabic*)]
\item \textbf{Value invariance:} $\sigma_2(T_1(U)) = \sigma_2(U)$.
\item \textbf{Ordering invariance:} $\sigma_2(T_1(U)) = \varphi(\sigma_2(U))$ for some $\varphi$ preserving the ordering $\sigma_2$ induces on clients. \emph{This is the level C2 is stated at} (made explicit in \eqref{eq:general_form}); the distinction from (P1) matters, because exact values are sufficient but never necessary.
\item \textbf{Decision invariance:} the client or coordinate $d_2$ actually selects is unchanged.
\item \textbf{Adversarial contribution (admission) invariance:} the \emph{adversarial} input $d_2$ admits into its aggregate is unchanged. \emph{Admission} is the term we use throughout for whether adversarial input survives $d_2$, and it is a \textbf{level, not one measurable quantity}: for a selector it is the indicator that an adversarial update is selected at all; for a weighted averager, the adversarial share of the weight mass; for a coordinate-wise order statistic, the adversarial share of selected coordinates, which is why Table~\ref{tab:channels_full}'s influence column is aggregator-specific by necessity. The committed pre-registrations say ``admission'', so that word is retained in every frozen rule name and no scored quantity is relabelled.
\item \textbf{Attack-suppression preservation:} ASR stays below threshold.
\end{enumerate}
\textbf{(P1)$\Rightarrow$(P2)$\Rightarrow$(P3) hold, and hold by definition}: equal values induce the same ordering, and a rule reading only that ordering cannot decide differently. \textbf{These three are logical implications under the definitions above, not empirical claims about how defenses behave}, and nothing is asserted by proving them: their content is that they stop at (P3). \textbf{Below (P3) the chain breaks, and it breaks in both directions.} Both breaks are measured. \emph{Holding} decision \emph{and} admission invariance does not deliver suppression: \texttt{cos\_krum}'s selection is bit-identical at every dose and its admitted adversarial mass is $0.000$ throughout, yet its ASR falls $0.173$ by the first rung alone through the magnitude channel, at $\rho{=}2.72$, where clean accuracy moves only $0.599 \to 0.567$ and our accuracy gate passes (paired $t{=}{-}1.976$, one-sided $p{=}0.044$, falling in $5/8$ seeds). Across the whole ladder it falls $0.425$, but that endpoint sits below our own accuracy floor, so \textbf{the gate-passing rung is the witness we rest on} (§\ref{app:targeted_full}). \emph{Losing} decision invariance does not cost suppression: Krum's selection flips in up to $80\%$ of rounds under an intervention that pins the adversarial mass, and its ASR stays inside the pre-registered practical-equivalence margin ($|\Delta|{=}0.026 < 0.15$) (§\ref{app:targeted_full}). A criterion stated at (P1)--(P2) therefore constrains security in \emph{neither} direction. \textbf{(P4) is where the security content sits, but we do not claim it is sufficient for (P5), and it is a \emph{level} rather than one universal scalar security quantity.} It is instantiated per aggregator by the measure that aggregator's own rule exposes, Krum's and \texttt{cos\_krum}'s selected-client indicator, \texttt{coord\_median}'s share of selected coordinates, reputation's adversarial weight share (\texttt{experiments/build\_channel\_table.py}), so \textbf{(P4) names what to measure, not a single number comparable across defenses}. It is the first level that refers to \emph{adversarial influence on the aggregate} rather than to the downstream defense's internal decision, and \texttt{cos\_krum} is our own witness that (P4)$\;\not\Rightarrow\;$(P5): admission identically $0.000$ at every rung while suppression moves. \textbf{So the hierarchy answers ``which level does a security claim have to be stated at?'' and not ``which level predicts ASR?''}: no level in it is offered as a predictor.
\end{definition}
\begin{remark}[Which level C2 asserts, and which one is load-bearing]
\label{rem:levels}
At $\rho = 54.6$ the \emph{uncontrolled} dose ladder of §\ref{app:ladder}, whose adversary is free to attenuate, changes Krum's \emph{decision} in $86.7\%$ of rounds while changing its \emph{admission} of adversarial input in $13.3\%$, and Krum's suppression does not deteriorate at all. \textbf{Those two figures are the uncontrolled arm's and no other's}: Mode~S, which pins the adversary at the same rung, reads $0.733$ and $0.000$ instead (Table~\ref{tab:channels}, \texttt{dose|krum|2.0|*} against \texttt{doseS|krum|2.0|*} in \texttt{results/admission\_\allowbreak measurement.json}), which is why Table~\ref{tab:channels} can say admission never moves in any round of any arm it reports. A selection that flips from one benign client to another disturbs the statistic maximally and the mechanism not at all. \textbf{Terminology, once and for all: ``\emph{mechanism}'' always means the downstream defense's \emph{discriminative operation} (what separates adversarial from benign updates) and never its security behaviour, since that implication is precisely what this paper refutes.} We reserve ``mechanism preservation'' for the formal property Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} establishes, saying \emph{statistic} or \emph{decision} preservation where only the weaker property holds. C2 above is the (P2) condition (which is what makes it checkable from definitions alone) but the load-bearing reading is (P4). \textbf{This is the paper's central tension: our theorem operates at (P1)--(P3), while the experiments indicate security behaviour is decided at (P4)}; §\ref{app:targeted_full} puts the two against each other in an experiment where they predict opposite outcomes, and Figure~\ref{fig:story} is that argument in one picture.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[C0's provenance: an applicability condition formulated post-hoc]
\label{rem:c0}
C0 sits in Criterion~\ref{thm:composability} as a stage-0 applicability condition because Lemma~\ref{lem:testability} is stated over C0$\wedge$C1, but \textbf{it does not have the same evidential status as C1--C3}, and we record that here rather than let its promotion obscure it. It is \textbf{vacuous on the 7-defense menu of §\ref{sec:composability_validation}}, where the strongest single defense reaches only $0.519$, and we formulated it only after the prospective suite of §\ref{sec:prospective} introduced a defense for which it binds. It is a lesson from prospective evaluation, applied there retrospectively; it is \emph{not} part of the criterion validated on the 42 pairs, and none of the accuracy figures in §\ref{sec:composability_validation} depend on it.
\end{remark}

\paragraph{A scope condition on the criterion itself, not a limitation of our runs.} C1 and every prediction built on it are defined against a \emph{committed} adversary, one whose attack is fixed before the composition is chosen. An adversary who observes the composition can re-target whichever channel the screen certifies, so \textbf{what the screen bounds is \emph{inherited} robustness under a fixed attack, never robustness against an adaptive one}. Our single whitebox composition-aware stress test is consistent with that reading ($0.064 \to 0.157$ seed-matched at $n{=}30$ on the pixel arm the attack is built from, $2.4\times$; §\ref{sec:fg_rfa_flagship}).

\begin{table}[htbp]
\centering\small
\caption{\textbf{Scope of claims: three kinds, and the one place each scope condition is stated.} \textbf{Claims do not transfer upward}: the formal guarantee does not certify the general framing, and the empirical figures certify neither level above them. This table is the conservation record for every caveat in the paper; §\ref{sec:limitations} restates the same conditions individually as (L1)--(L6).}
\label{tab:scope_levels}
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{0.115\linewidth}p{0.315\linewidth}p{0.505\linewidth}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{kind} & \textbf{content} & \textbf{status, and what it does \emph{not} license} \\
\midrule
\textbf{General} & An evaluation gated on the outcome it predicts cannot identify the mechanism's contribution, and admissible designs are within-defense; an inherited-suppression gate cannot certify emergent synergy (Prop.~\ref{prop:identification_full}, Prop.~\ref{prop:emergent_full}). & \textbf{Proved, and not specific} to FL, to backdoors, or to our conditions. Licenses no claim that any composition \emph{is} secure, and none that mechanism preservation implies suppression. Recall is bounded, not achieved: the bound is $80\%$ from $|\mathcal{E}|/|\mathcal{S}|{=}1/5$, and $40\%$ is what we measure (L2). \\
\addlinespace
\textbf{Formal} & Mechanism preservation as \emph{invariance} under \textbf{positive per-client rescalings} $T(u_i)=c_iu_i$, $c_i>0$, into specific rank and geometric aggregators, with explicit margins (Thm.~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}, Lem.~\ref{lem:annihilation}, Cor.~\ref{cor:aggregate_mass}, Prop.~\ref{prop:invariance}, Prop.~\ref{prop:rank_invariance}). & \textbf{Proved on that class only}: a \emph{laboratory} for composition, not a model of FL defenses in general. A $d_1$ that alters participation, carries cross-round state, mixes clients, or aggregates first admits no such $T$ and is \textbf{covered by nothing here}. \textbf{Bounds no ASR.} C0 was formulated post-hoc and applied retrospectively to arms frozen before it (Rem.~\ref{rem:c0}, L4). Regime~A's condition is not operationally checkable, $r^\star$ not being a stable per-attack constant (L5). Cor.~\ref{cor:aggregate_mass}'s bound goes vacuous as $m \to 1$, which heterogeneity realizes. \\
\addlinespace
\textbf{Empirical} & §\ref{app:targeted_full}'s interventions and §\ref{sec:composability_validation}--§\ref{sec:prospective}'s 42-pair menu: CIFAR-10, CifarCNN/ResNet18, $N \in \{10,100\}$, $K \in \{5,10\}$, $f \in [0.05,0.6]$, Dirichlet $\alpha \in \{0.1,0.5\}$, \textbf{star topology}, three attack families; one arm on EMNIST-byclass/\texttt{simple\_cnn}, one on CIFAR-100/\texttt{cifar\_cnn} and one on CIFAR-10/\texttt{resnet18} varying architecture alone. & \textbf{Measured} at $n{=}5$ seeds per cell ($n{=}8$ for \texttt{cos\_krum}, $n{=}3$ frozen and $n{=}5$ pooled post-hoc on EMNIST-byclass, $n{=}3$ on the \texttt{resnet18} arm, $n{=}30$ for the FG$\to$RFA characterization), all fixed before the runs, so intervals are wide and \textbf{no null is read as flatness} without a pre-registered practical-equivalence margin. ASR is thresholded at $0.5$, the level at which the trigger fires on the majority of triggered inputs; a cell below the $0.35$ clean-accuracy floor is \emph{uninterpretable}, not suppressed. Dataset transfer is tested on \emph{two} arms and no more: the flagship Mode-S arm on EMNIST-byclass/\texttt{simple\_cnn}, and the sign-reversal cell of Table~\ref{tab:sixcell} on CIFAR-100, where the reversal replicates, the confounded leg falling and the controlled leg rising (§\ref{app:sixcell}). Both are ``replicated on a further dataset'', \textbf{never} ``generalizes'' (L1). The EMNIST arm is a \emph{channel} replication and carries no screen: no single defense measured on EMNIST-byclass suppresses the committed pixel backdoor alone (minimum $0.609$; the menu measured there excludes \texttt{foolsgold}, \texttt{reputation} and the cosine mirrors), so C1's per-attack precondition fails on that arm and the strategy comparison is \textbf{out of scope over the measured menu} rather than merely untested (L1). \textbf{``Refuted''} always means a pre-registered \emph{directional} hypothesis was rejected against a rule frozen for it, \textbf{never} that a mathematical claim was disproved. The $\gamma{\approx}0.66$ boundary is architecture-conditional (L1); the strongest development-set figures are \emph{fit}, not prediction (L2). \\
\addlinespace
\textbf{Both instruments are oracles} & Mode~S and Mode~A build $c_i$ from adversary identity, as does $\Delta_c$ (§\ref{app:targeted_full}, supplement §\ref{sec:rho_vs_deltac}). & They \textbf{identify a causal channel and are not screens}: no deployed defense knows which clients are adversarial, and estimating (P4) without that knowledge is the open problem of §\ref{sec:conclusion}. Admission is \emph{a direction to measure}, not a validated predictor: the pooled two-channel model is unconfirmed over $26$ cells ($R^2{=}0.239$) and its cross-arm ordering was refuted by our own replication (L6). C1 and everything built on it assume a \textbf{committed} adversary, so what is bounded is \emph{inherited} robustness under a fixed attack, \textbf{never adaptive robustness}. \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\subsection{The testability boundary}
\label{app:boundary}

C0 and C1 together constrain what any validation of this criterion can look like. We did not anticipate this; it turns out to explain the central weakness of our own prospective suite (§\ref{sec:prospective}, L4), and (once stated in general form) to \emph{derive} the class of within-defense designs in which C2's contribution is identifiable (§\ref{app:ladder}). Its content is narrower than ``a general identification boundary'' and worth stating precisely: \textbf{under inherited-effect gating}, cross-defense contrasts cannot identify the mechanism's contribution, because the gate conditions on a descendant of both the mechanism and the outcome. What we offer as the transferable contribution is not that negative but \textbf{the admissible design it derives}, set out below and run in §\ref{app:targeted_full}.

\begin{lemma}[Testability boundary]
\label{lem:testability}
Let $(d_1 \to d_2)$ satisfy C0 and C1, and let $a^*$ be a committed attack witnessing C0, so $\mathrm{ASR}(d_1,a^*) \geq 0.5$. Then $\mathrm{ASR}(d_2,a^*) < 0.5$: the downstream defense suppresses $a^*$ \emph{on its own}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
C1 at $a^*$ gives $\min_{d\in\{d_1,d_2\}}\mathrm{ASR}(d,a^*) < 0.5$. By C0 the minimum is not attained at $d_1$, hence it is attained at $d_2$.
\end{proof}

Nothing in the argument is specific to federated learning, to backdoors, or to our defense menu. Lemma~\ref{lem:testability} is \textbf{the enabling observation for the FL instance}; the substantive claim is the general one, and we state it as such.

\begin{proposition}[Why constituent-effect contrasts cannot identify composition effects under inherited-effect gating; Proposition~\ref{prop:identification} of the body, restated with proof]
\label{prop:identification_full}
Let a screen certify $(d_1 \to d_2)$ against attack $a$ only if both
(i) an \textbf{eligibility gate} $E(d_1,d_2,a) := \min_{d \in \{d_1,d_2\}} M(d,a) < \theta$ holds, where $M$ and $\theta$ are the \emph{same} outcome metric and threshold the screen predicts for the composition, and
(ii) a \textbf{mechanism condition} $P(d_1,d_2)$ holds, determined by the two defenses' definitions alone.
Suppose the screen is applied only where the downstream mechanism is load-bearing, $M(d_1,a) \ge \theta$. Consider estimating $P$'s contribution to $M(d_1 \to d_2, a)$ from contrasts across a defense menu $\mathcal{D}$. Then every certified pair satisfies $M(d_2,a) < \theta$, so of the four cells of $\{P,\neg P\} \times \{M(d_2,a) < \theta,\ M(d_2,a) \ge \theta\}$ the certified set occupies at most two, and the cell needed to separate $P$ from $d_2$'s standalone effectiveness is empty by the gate, for every $\mathcal{D}$. No contrast varying $d_2$ identifies $P$'s contribution. Identification requires holding $d_2$, $a$ and therefore $E$ fixed while intervening on the upstream transform: \textbf{the admissible designs are within-defense.}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The load-bearing condition excludes $d_1$ from attaining the minimum in (i), so certification forces $M(d_2,a) < \theta$; both cells with $M(d_2,a) \ge \theta$ are therefore unpopulated whatever $\mathcal{D}$ is, and $P$ is never observed against a downstream defense that fails on its own. Any estimator of $P$'s contribution from such contrasts is unidentified.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[The design that restores identification]
\label{cor:within_design}
Fix $d_2$ and $a$. Let $\mathcal{T}$ be a set of upstream transforms with $\mathrm{id} \in \mathcal{T}$, let the experimenter assign $T \in \mathcal{T}$ by a rule that is a function of the design alone, and let $Y := M(T \to d_2, a)$ be run and recorded for every assigned $T$, the control $T{=}\mathrm{id}$ included. Then for every $t \in \mathcal{T}$ the contrast
\[
\tau(t) \;:=\; \mathbb{E}\big[Y \mid \mathrm{do}(T{=}t)\big] - \mathbb{E}\big[Y \mid \mathrm{do}(T{=}\mathrm{id})\big]
\]
is identified, and equals the difference of the two arms' means. \textbf{Its scope is narrow, and stating it is half the point.} $\tau(t)$ is the total effect of the assigned transform at that one $(d_2,a)$: it separates into a statistic channel and an admission channel only when one of them is pinned by construction (§\ref{sec:metric_swap}), and it transfers to no other downstream defense, no other attack, and no adversary that adapts to $T$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Assignment is a function of the design, so $T$ is independent of the potential outcomes $\{Y(t)\}_{t \in \mathcal{T}}$, and every arm is run, so $\Pr(T{=}t) > 0$ on $\mathcal{T}$: ignorability and positivity hold by construction rather than by assumption, and $\mathbb{E}[Y \mid \mathrm{do}(T{=}t)] = \mathbb{E}[Y \mid T{=}t]$. The cell that Proposition~\ref{prop:identification_full} empties is emptied by conditioning on $E$, a descendant of $Y$; here nothing is conditioned on, and the control arm is run whatever its outcome. The scope clause is a restatement of $\mathrm{do}(T{=}t)$: the intervention is on the transform, not on one channel of its effect.
\end{proof}

\noindent This is the elementary converse of the proposition and we claim no depth for it. Its content is that the repair is \textbf{available and cheap, and is not what cross-defense comparison does}, including in our own first two suites: §\ref{sec:targeted} runs it, and Table~\ref{tab:comparability} is the CIFAR-10 cell where both designs exist, where they agree numerically at $\kappa{=}0$ and disagree in \emph{sign} at the top rung; §\ref{app:sixcell} reports a second such cell, on CIFAR-100, where the disagreement replicates.

\paragraph{A second boundary: what such a screen cannot find.} The gate that limits what a screen can be \emph{validated} on also limits what it can \emph{cover}, which converts the most obvious objection to our criterion, that its recall is poor, into a scope result.

\begin{proposition}[Inherited-suppression screens cannot certify emergent synergy]
\label{prop:emergent_full}
$(d_1 \to d_2)$'s suppression of $a$ is \textbf{emergent} if $M(d_1 \to d_2, a) < \theta$ while $\min_{d \in \{d_1,d_2\}} M(d,a) \ge \theta$. No emergent composition satisfies the eligibility gate of Proposition~\ref{prop:identification_full}. Hence, writing $\mathcal{S}$ for the compositions with $M < \theta$, $\mathcal{E} \subseteq \mathcal{S}$ for the emergent ones and $\mathcal{S} \setminus \mathcal{E}$ for the inherited ones, any such screen's recall obeys $\mathrm{recall} \le 1 - |\mathcal{E}|/|\mathcal{S}|$ on every menu and every attack.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Emergence is the negation of the gate, so each member of $\mathcal{E}$ is rejected while belonging to $\mathcal{S}$: a false negative. The bound is the resulting recall.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[The cap reaches $0$, so it is not a reassurance]
\label{cor:zero_recall}
If $\mathcal{E} = \mathcal{S}$, every screen carrying that eligibility gate has recall exactly $0$ on that menu, at every sample size: it certifies no low-$M$ composition at all. And $1 - |\mathcal{E}|/|\mathcal{S}|$ is a quantity the screen's own user cannot report, since $|\mathcal{E}|$ is measurable only by running the compositions the gate rejects.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Every member of $\mathcal{S}$ is then emergent, hence rejected by Proposition~\ref{prop:emergent_full}, so the certified set meets $\mathcal{S}$ in $\emptyset$ and recall is $0$; recall is also non-negative, so the bound is attained. For the second claim, deciding membership in $\mathcal{E}$ requires $M(d_1 \to d_2, a)$ for a pair with $\min_{d}M(d,a) \ge \theta$, and that is a pair the gate rejects.
\end{proof}

\noindent Our own menu witnesses $\mathcal{E} \neq \emptyset$: FoolsGold$\to$RFA reaches $0.093$ where FoolsGold alone reaches $0.732$ and RFA alone $0.845$ (§\ref{sec:fg_rfa_flagship}). One emergent pair among five low-ASR pairs puts the cap here at $80\%$ against a measured $40\%$, so emergence explains part of the gap; had this menu's low-ASR set been emergent throughout, the same criterion would have certified nothing. That is why we report recall as an operating point rather than as a residual (§\ref{app:frontier}).

\paragraph{Mechanism--effectiveness confounding: Proposition~\ref{prop:identification_full} in our instance.} The boundary has an uncomfortable consequence for validation, and it is worth spelling out concretely. In \emph{every} in-scope pair the criterion certifies, $d_2$ already suppresses the attack that $d_1$ fails. A certified composition's low ASR can therefore always be attributed to $d_2$'s standalone strength (C1) rather than to $d_1$ having preserved $d_2$'s mechanism (C2, C3): the two are confounded \textbf{by the conditions themselves}, not by an unlucky choice of defense menu. We name this \textbf{mechanism--effectiveness confounding} and offer it as a methodological observation about validating composition criteria of this shape, applicable beyond our own experiments.

In particular it rules out the experiment we previously named as decisive: a $d_2$ whose admission signal is invariant under positive rescaling but which is \emph{weak in isolation} cannot satisfy C1 in scope, so no such certified pair exists to test. The achievable substitute is \textbf{per-attack complementarity}: a $d_2$ that suppresses the attack $d_1$ fails while remaining weak on a \emph{different} committed attack, so its max-committed ASR stays high and it cannot suppress the composition single-handedly. Reputation is such a defense ($0.017$ on model-scaling, $0.842$ on the pixel backdoor), and §\ref{sec:metric_swap} builds the test on that observation.
\begin{proof}[Argument sketch]
(C1 sufficient) If at least one of $d_1$, $d_2$ individually suppresses $a$, composition inherits that suppression provided C2$\wedge$C3 preserve the signal chain. \textbf{That covers two routes and only one of them is this paper's subject.} If the suppressing constituent is $d_2$, the inheritance is exactly what C2$\wedge$C3 are for. If it is $d_1$, the suppression is inherited \emph{provided $d_2$ is not invariant to the transformation class $d_1$ suppresses through}; subject to that proviso $d_1$ sits upstream and nothing downstream undoes it, so C2$\wedge$C3 are not required on that attack and the preservation argument contributes nothing to it. \textbf{We had stated this route without the proviso, and it is not vacuous} (below). Both routes count toward a max-committed label, which is why the label can be right on an attack the mechanism has no purchase on (§\ref{app:validation_full}). C1 is sufficient, not necessary: two individually-weak defenses may suppress synergistically through complementary mechanisms (e.g., partial downweighting + geometric rejection), but predicting such synergy requires the quantitative margins below. The criterion is therefore \emph{conservative} in the direction this sketch argues for: it may reject working compositions. \textbf{It is not a guarantee in the other direction, and nothing here establishes one.} That no clean C1$\wedge$C2$\wedge$C3 PASS is a false positive is a \emph{measured} property of $42$ pairs (§\ref{sec:condition_scoring}), not a consequence of the argument above; the criterion has no established predictive validity ((L2), §\ref{sec:limitations}), and against an adversary that solves both constituents' constraints at once a certified pair's ASR rises $2.4\times$ (§\ref{sec:fg_rfa_flagship}).

(C2 role) Suppose $d_2$ individually suppresses $a$ via signal $\sigma_2(u_a) > \theta$. If $d_1$'s transformation $T_1$ destroys this signal ($\sigma_2(T_1(u_a)) \not\approx \sigma_2(u_a)$), $d_2$ cannot detect the attack. Example: NormClip as $d_1$ maps every client norm above $\tau$ to $\tau$. A $\sigma_2$ keyed on the update norm is then client-independent, and C2 fails outright. Reputation's consensus distance $\sigma_2(u) = \|u - \hat\mu\|$ does not collapse that way: at $\|u_i\| = \tau$ for every $i$, $\|u_i - \hat\mu\|^2 = \tau^2 + \|\hat\mu\|^2 - 2\langle u_i, \hat\mu\rangle$, so client dependence survives through the inner product. Clipping removes the radial part of the separation and no more. For a purely radial attack that is the whole of it, and model-scaling is one, since it multiplies the update by a constant that clipping undoes exactly; where the adversarial direction also differs, the angular part survives and C2 fails by margin compression rather than by annihilation. This is measured, not hypothetical: NormClip$\to$reputation leaves model-scaling ASR at $0.807 \pm 0.048$ and the pixel backdoor at $0.842 \pm 0.047$ over 5 seeds, against reputation alone at $0.017$ (per-seed values in §\ref{app:composition}). Quantitatively: C2 holds when relative signal distortion $\Delta_{\mathrm{signal}} := |\sigma_2(T_1(u_a)) - \sigma_2(u_a)|/\sigma_2(u_a) < \delta_2$ for a defense-specific tolerance $\delta_2$.

(C3 role) Even when $d_1$ preserves the \emph{direction} of $d_2$'s signal (C2 holds), it may compress magnitudes below $d_2$'s threshold. Example: FoolsGold as $d_1$ downweights adversarial clients by $\alpha \ll 1$. TrimmedMean's coordinate-wise rank signal still distinguishes adversarial coordinates (C2 holds), but after rescaling by $\alpha$, per-coordinate values $\alpha \cdot u_{a,k}$ fall within the trimming range: the coordinate-wise extreme that would trigger trimming is no longer extreme. Quantitatively: C3 holds when the margin ratio $\Delta_{\mathrm{margin}} := (\tau_{d_2}(T_1(u_a)) - \theta)/(\tau_{d_2}(u_a) - \theta) > 0$, i.e., adversarial scores remain above $d_2$'s rejection boundary after $d_1$'s transform.

(Sufficiency) If C1 holds, C2 holds ($\Delta_{\mathrm{signal}} < \delta_2$), and C3 holds ($\Delta_{\mathrm{margin}} > 0$), then $d_2$ detects and suppresses $u_a$ even after $d_1$'s transformation. For the weighting$\to$aggregator class, Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} instantiates these margins explicitly: $\rho < r^\star$ for the rank aggregators, and the transformed-point separation condition $\Delta'_{\mathrm{sep}} > 2(R'_B+\delta')$ for the geometric one, which Corollary~\ref{cor:checkable_margin} restates in a form that can be checked without running the composition. \qedhere
\end{proof}

\paragraph{The proviso on C1's $d_1$ route, and why this menu cannot test it.} Every $d_1$ we run acts as a positive per-client rescaling $T(u_i) = c_i u_i$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance}), so the proviso asks only whether $d_2$ reads that rescaling at all. Two of our own downstream choices do not, and they fail it to different depths. \emph{Partially:} \texttt{cos\_krum} scores a rescaling-invariant statistic, so its \emph{decision} ignores $d_1$'s downweighting entirely, which is measured rather than argued. Under one upstream RFA stage on live update stacks, Krum's selection moves in $0.533$ of rounds and \texttt{cos\_krum}'s in $\mathbf{0.000}$, at a nonzero aggregate displacement of $0.133$ (App.~\ref{app:oracle_free}). The transform acted and the decision did not move. But the emitted update is still the downweighted one, so on that $d_2$ the magnitude channel carries the inheritance the decision channel has stopped carrying. \emph{Completely:} FLTrust gates on the scale-invariant $\cos(u_i, g_{\mathrm{srv}})$ and then renormalizes every update to $\|g_{\mathrm{srv}}\|$, so a positive per-client rescaling cancels \emph{exactly} on both channels, which §\ref{sec:prospective} measures on the six pairs placing it downstream. \textbf{The proviso bounds the argument and corrects no measured row}, and Lemma~\ref{lem:testability} says why it cannot be otherwise here: the only $d_2$ in our menu that cancels both channels also suppresses both committed attacks alone ($0.048$), so in scope the $d_1$ route is never the \emph{sole} route to C1 and no certified pair isolates it. The five measured reputation$\to d_2$ pairs inherit reputation's model-scaling suppression at $0.0146$ to $0.0181$ against its own $0.017$ ($n{=}3$ each, seeds $42$--$44$, in \texttt{results/\allowbreak all\_\allowbreak compositions/\allowbreak summary.json}) precisely because none of those five downstream defenses renormalizes. Testing the proviso needs a $d_2$ that is rescaling-invariant on \emph{both} channels and weak in isolation, and this paper does not contain one.

\subsection{From the federated protocol to the abstract statement, term by term}
\label{app:mapping}

Proposition~\ref{prop:identification_full} is stated over an abstract screen precisely so that nothing in it rests on federated learning, and Lemma~\ref{lem:testability} is its instance under our criterion. That correspondence is what carries the result into this paper and out of it again, so we display it rather than assert it. Figure~\ref{fig:identification} in the main text is the same object drawn as a graph, including the design that repairs it; Table~\ref{tab:mapping} is the same object written as a substitution.

\label{tab:mapping}

Proposition~\ref{prop:identification_full} & Our federated instance & Stated at \\
\midrule
outcome metric $M(d,a)$ & $\mathrm{ASR}(d,a)$, the committed attack's success rate under defense $d$ & Criterion~\ref{thm:composability} \\
threshold $\theta$ & $0.5$, the level at which the trigger fires on the majority of triggered inputs & Table~\ref{tab:scope_levels} \\
load-bearing condition\newline $M(d_1,a) \ge \theta$ & (C0), $\mathrm{ASR}(d_1,a) \ge 0.5$ for some committed $a$ & Rem.~\ref{rem:c0} \\
eligibility gate $E$:\newline $\min_{d} M(d,a) < \theta$ & (C1), $\min_{d\in\{d_1,d_2\}}\mathrm{ASR}(d,a) < 0.5$, quantified per attack & Criterion~\ref{thm:composability} \\
mechanism condition $P(d_1,d_2)$ & (C2)$\wedge$(C3): $\sigma_2(T_1(u_a)) \approx \sigma_2(u_a)$, and the margin survives $T_1$ & Prop.~\ref{prop:invariance}, Thm.~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} \\
menu $\mathcal{D}$ & the pairs $(d_1 \to d_2)$ a suite draws from a defense library & §\ref{sec:composability_validation} \\
certification $M(d_1\!\to\! d_2,a) < \theta$ & the criterion's PASS label on a pair & §\ref{sec:prospective} \\
the empty cell of the $2\times2$ & $\neg P$ against a $d_2$ that fails alone: no admitted pair has one & Lem.~\ref{lem:testability} \\
holding $d_2$, $a$, $E$ fixed & Mode~S and Mode~A: same $d_2$, attack and seeds, intervene on $T$ & §\ref{app:targeted_full} \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

Two features of the substitution carry the whole argument, and both are legible in the table rather than argued for. \textbf{First, $E$ and the quantity the screen predicts are the same measurement.} C1 is an ASR comparison at $0.5$ and the PASS label is an ASR prediction at $0.5$, so the gate is not an unlucky covariate that a larger menu would decorrelate: it is a function of the outcome. \textbf{Second, $P$ is decided without running anything.} C2 and C3 are read off $d_1$'s and $d_2$'s definitions, which is what Prop.~\ref{prop:invariance} makes algebraic, so no version of the design recovers identification by measuring $P$ more carefully. The two together are what force the within-defense design of §\ref{app:targeted_full}: with $d_2$, $a$ and the seeds fixed, $E$ is fixed too, and the intervention on $T$ moves $P$ alone.

\noindent \textbf{What the mapping does not license.} It shows our criterion is \emph{an} instance, not that every composition screen is one. A screen whose gate reads something other than the outcome it predicts, a per-client norm statistic say, has a different $E$ and falls outside Proposition~\ref{prop:identification_full} entirely; the proposition is a statement about outcome-gated screens, ours included. Nor does the mapping transfer any \emph{empirical} claim: the middle column names measured quantities, and their scope is Table~\ref{tab:scope_levels}'s, not the proposition's.

\subsection{A restricted theorem: C2$\wedge$C3 for weighting$\to$rank and weighting$\to$geometric compositions}
\label{sec:restricted_theorem}
\label{app:proofs}

\noindent\textbf{What follows is a boundary theorem, and deliberately not a security theorem.} It bounds what an \emph{invariance} argument about composition can establish: preservation of the downstream defense's discriminative mechanism, levels (P1)--(P3) of Definition~\ref{def:levels_full}, and it says nothing whatever about suppression, which lives at (P4)--(P5). We state that in the same breath as the theorem because the whole point of the paper is the gap between the two: the theorem is the best case for statistic-level reasoning, proved with explicit margins, and §\ref{app:targeted_full} then shows that even exact invariance neither implies nor preserves suppression. Read it as a \emph{formal} claim in the sense of Table~\ref{tab:scope_levels}: proved, on one class, bounding no ASR.

The criterion above is an operational heuristic. For the class of compositions that matter most in practice, a per-client \emph{weighting} defense $d_1$ (reputation, FoolsGold) followed by a \emph{rank-based} or \emph{geometric} aggregator $d_2$ (TrimmedMean, CoordMedian, RFA), which contains the flagship FoolsGold$\to$RFA, C2 and C3 can be \emph{proven} rather than asserted, with an explicit tolerance on how aggressively $d_1$ may reweight. The key structural fact is that $d_2$'s admission score is scale-equivariant, so it commutes with $d_1$'s reweighting up to a bounded ratio; the composition fails only when $d_1$ compresses that ratio past a margin set by $d_2$'s separation.

\begin{theorem}[Bounded reweighting preserves the downstream discriminative mechanism]
\label{thm:bounded_reweight}
Consider a round with $n_b$ benign and $n_a$ adversarial updates under honest majority $n_b > n_a$. Let $d_1$ be a per-client reweighting $T_1(u_i) = w_i u_i$ with $w_i \geq 0$, and define the ratio over \emph{strictly positive} weights, $\rho := \max_i w_i / \min_{i:\,w_i>0} w_i$. Suppose $d_2$ suppresses attack $a$ through a client admission score $\sigma_2(\cdot\,;U)$ that is scale-equivariant, $\sigma_2(c\,u_i; c\,U) = c^{k}\sigma_2(u_i;U)$ for all $c>0$ and some degree $k\ge 0$. Assume $w_a > 0$ for adversarial clients; the $w_a = 0$ case is Lemma~\ref{lem:annihilation}. The theorem establishes preservation of $d_2$'s suppression \emph{mechanism}; it does not bound ASR. Then:
\begin{enumerate}[noitemsep,topsep=2pt]
\item \textbf{(Rank aggregators; C2$\wedge$C3 hold up to a margin.)} If $d_2\in\{\text{TrimmedMean},\text{CoordMedian}\}$, so $\sigma_2$ is a coordinate-wise rank, then $d_2$'s per-coordinate suppression of $a$ is preserved when two conditions hold. \textbf{(i)~Count majority:} $n_a < K/2$ for CoordMedian, and $n_a \leq \lfloor\beta K\rfloor$ for TrimmedMean with $\beta$-trimming. \textbf{(ii)~Signed ordering:} on each suppression-relevant coordinate $k$, the adversarial transformed value lies strictly beyond every benign one, $w_a u_{a,k} > \max_b w_b u_{b,k}$ (upper tail) or $w_a u_{a,k} < \min_b w_b u_{b,k}$ (lower tail). \emph{Sufficient magnitude condition for} (ii): if $\mathrm{sign}(u_{a,k})$ is common to all adversaries and $|u_{a,k}| > \rho\cdot\max_b|u_{b,k}|$, then (ii) holds; writing $r^\star := \min_k \min_a \min_b |u_{a,k}|/|u_{b,k}|$ for the coordinate-wise separation margin, which minimizes over adversaries as well so that the bound binds on the least extreme one, this is $\rho < r^\star$. Magnitude alone is \emph{not} sufficient without the sign assumption, and the reason is not that a large adversarial coordinate could sit between the benign values: since $\rho \ge 1$, the magnitude condition gives $|u_{a,k}| > \max_b|u_{b,k}| \ge \max(\max_b u_{b,k},\, -\min_b u_{b,k})$, which forces $u_{a,k} > \max_b u_{b,k}$ or $u_{a,k} < \min_b u_{b,k}$. What magnitude leaves open is \emph{which} of the two. Mixed adversarial signs split the adversaries across both tails, while (ii) is a one-tail condition and (i)'s count budget is written against a single tail; a common sign is what puts them all in one. With $n_a{=}1$ the sign assumption is therefore vacuous. At $\rho=1$ ranks are preserved exactly, recovering Proposition~\ref{prop:rank_invariance}.
\item \textbf{(Geometric median / RFA; C2$\wedge$C3 hold up to a margin.)} The composition hands RFA the \emph{transformed points} $u'_i := w_i u_i$ and RFA runs its standard \emph{unweighted} geometric median on them; it is not a weighted geometric median, and $w_i$ enters only by moving the points. All quantities must therefore be defined on the transformed set. Let $\mu'_B$ be the geometric median of the benign $u'_b$ alone, $\mu'$ that of all of $U'$, $R'_B := \max_b\|u'_b - \mu'_B\|$ the transformed benign radius, and $\delta' := \|\mu' - \mu'_B\|$ the displacement caused by including the adversarial points. Suppression is preserved when
\[
\Delta'_{\mathrm{sep}} := \min_{a,b}\|u'_a - u'_b\| \;>\; 2\,(R'_B + \delta'),
\]
under which every adversarial residual $\|u'_a - \mu'\|$ strictly exceeds every benign residual $\|u'_b - \mu'\|$, so RFA's inverse-residual weight on $a$ stays below the benign floor.
\item \textbf{(Norm-equalizing $d_1$; C2 fails or is compressed: the degenerate case.)} If instead $d_1$ maps every client norm to a common value (NormClip with $\tau$ below all client norms), the consequence depends on what $\sigma_2$ reads. A $\sigma_2$ keyed on the update norm becomes constant across clients, so it stops separating $a$ and C2 fails outright. A $\sigma_2$ keyed on consensus distance does \emph{not} become constant: at $\|u_i\|{=}\tau$ for all $i$, $\|u_i-\hat\mu\|^2 = \tau^2 + \|\hat\mu\|^2 - 2\langle u_i,\hat\mu\rangle$, so client dependence survives through the inner product. Equalization removes the \emph{radial} part of the separation and compresses the rest to its angular part, and C2 then fails once that residual drops below $d_2$'s threshold, which is a margin condition of the same form as parts (1) and (2) rather than an algebraic collapse. Nor is the weight ratio infinite: $w_i = \tau/\|u_i\|$ is finite for every non-zero update, so $\rho$ is a finite scalar and a $\rho$-margin formally applies. What fails is that $\rho$ is set by the realized norms, so no margin fixed \emph{before} the round bounds it; weight exactly zero is the genuinely unbounded case, and that is Lemma~\ref{lem:annihilation}.
\end{enumerate}
Parts (1) and (2) are sufficient conditions for $d_2$ to \emph{preserve the downstream suppression ordering}, not to exclude adversarial contributions outright. Neither is a sufficient condition for low ASR, and part (2) orders residuals per client only; it constrains the \emph{aggregate} adversarial coefficient solely through Corollary~\ref{cor:aggregate_mass}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
(1) TrimmedMean/CoordMedian act on coordinate $k$ through the rank of $w_i u_{i,k}$ among $\{w_j u_{j,k}\}_j$; $a$ is suppressed on $k$ because its transformed coordinate is extreme (trimmed, or not selected by the median). Given (ii), at most $n_a$ of the extreme order statistics are adversarial, so with (i) the selected value is benign. For the magnitude condition, $w_a>0$ gives $\rho \ge \max_b w_b/w_a$, so $|u_{a,k}| > \rho\max_b|u_{b,k}|$ implies $|w_a u_{a,k}| > w_a\rho\max_b|u_{b,k}| \ge (\max_b w_b)\max_b|u_{b,k}| \ge \max_b|w_b u_{b,k}|$; with a sign common to all adversaries this places $w_a u_{a,k}$ strictly beyond every benign value on that side. Scale-equivariance ($k{=}0$ for pure rank) makes the uniform case $\rho{=}1$ exact.
(2) All quantities are on the transformed points $u'_i = w_i u_i$, which is what RFA receives. Every benign residual obeys $\|u'_b - \mu'\| \le R'_B + \delta'$ by the triangle inequality. For adversarial $u'_a$, $\|u'_a - \mu'_B\| \ge \Delta'_{\mathrm{sep}} - R'_B$, hence $\|u'_a - \mu'\| \ge \Delta'_{\mathrm{sep}} - R'_B - \delta'$. Demanding that this exceed the benign bound gives $\Delta'_{\mathrm{sep}} - R'_B - \delta' > R'_B + \delta'$, i.e.\ the stated condition. No weighted-majority assumption is needed, and none would be appropriate: RFA weights every input point equally and the $w_i$ act only by relocating the points. Our implementation runs Weiszfeld for 50 iterations at tolerance $10^{-6}$, so the argument transfers to it.
(3) Clipping to a common $\tau$ sends $\|u_i\|\mapsto\tau$ for all $i$. For $\sigma_2(u_i)=\|u_i\|$ (NormClip-as-$d_2$) the score is then a client-independent constant, separation vanishes, and that is C2-failure outright. For the consensus distance, expand $\|u_i-\hat\mu\|^2 = \tau^2 + \|\hat\mu\|^2 - 2\langle u_i,\hat\mu\rangle$: the first two terms are common to all clients, so every trace of magnitude separation is gone, but the inner product is not, and it carries the angular separation between $u_i$ and the consensus direction. Equalization therefore compresses the separation onto its angular component instead of annihilating it, and C2 fails once that residual falls below $d_2$'s threshold $\theta$, a margin condition of the same shape as parts (1) and (2). The norm-keyed score is the special case in which the residual is identically zero. \qedhere
\end{proof}

\noindent\textbf{The condition as stated cannot be checked before running the composition, and that is fixable.} Part (2) is stated on the transformed points, and $\delta' = \|\mu'-\mu'_B\|$ is by definition the displacement that including the adversarial points causes: evaluating it requires executing the very composition the criterion is meant to screen. In a screening criterion that is a real defect. It is also removable, because the displacement is controlled by the benign spread and the client counts alone.

\begin{corollary}[Checkable separation margin]
\label{cor:checkable_margin}
In the setting of Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}(2), write $S'_B := \sum_b \|u'_b - \mu'_B\|$ and let $\bar R'_B := S'_B/n_b$ be the \emph{mean} transformed benign residual. Under honest majority $n_b > n_a$,
\[
\delta' \;\leq\; \frac{2 S'_B}{\,n_b - n_a\,} \;=\; C_A\,\bar R'_B, \qquad C_A := \frac{2 n_b}{\,n_b - n_a\,},
\]
and therefore the separation condition of Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}(2) holds whenever
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:checkable_margin}
\Delta'_{\mathrm{sep}} \;>\; 2 R'_B + 2\,C_A\,\bar R'_B .
\end{equation}
No quantity in \eqref{eq:checkable_margin} is computed from the composed aggregate.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
$\mu'$ minimizes $F(x) := \sum_i \|x - u'_i\|$, so $F(\mu') \leq F(\mu'_B)$. Write $t := \delta'$. By the triangle inequality each benign term obeys $\|\mu' - u'_b\| \geq t - \|u'_b - \mu'_B\|$ and each adversarial term obeys $\|\mu' - u'_a\| \geq \|\mu'_B - u'_a\| - t$, so $F(\mu') \geq n_b t - S'_B + \sum_a \|\mu'_B - u'_a\| - n_a t$. Since $F(\mu'_B) = S'_B + \sum_a \|\mu'_B - u'_a\|$, the inequality $F(\mu') \leq F(\mu'_B)$ gives $(n_b - n_a)\,t \leq 2 S'_B$, which is the displacement bound. Substituting it into $\Delta'_{\mathrm{sep}} > 2(R'_B + \delta')$ yields \eqref{eq:checkable_margin}. \qedhere
\end{proof}

\noindent\textbf{Honest majority is exactly what the geometric case needs, and it enters on the displacement, not on the weight ratio.} $C_A$ diverges as $n_a \to n_b$, so Corollary~\ref{cor:checkable_margin} is the precise point at which $n_b > n_a$ becomes indispensable, while the theorem itself needs no majority assumption on the \emph{weights}. An earlier version of this paper summarized part (2) as a weight-ratio bound $\rho < n_b/n_a$; that summary was wrong, and the correction is not cosmetic, because the majority condition belongs on $\delta'$ instead. \textbf{Measured at Dirichlet $\alpha{=}0.5$}: the displacement bound holds in all $17$ rounds with $w_a>0$, and is conservative there by a factor of at least $10.5$ (mean $215$), because the realized displacement is set by a force balance among the residuals rather than by the worst case a bound must cover. Condition~\eqref{eq:checkable_margin} is satisfied in $16$ of those $17$ rounds: $15/15$ at $n_a{=}1$, where $C_A = 2.67$ and the requirement at the measured mean $\bar R'_B/R'_B = 0.429$ is $\Delta'_{\mathrm{sep}} > 4.29\,R'_B$ against a measured $\Delta'_{\mathrm{sep}}/R'_B$ never below $5.88$; and $1/2$ at $n_a{=}2$, where $C_A = 6.00$ tightens the requirement past what one of the two rounds supplies. \textbf{The heterogeneity limit recorded for Corollary~\ref{cor:aggregate_mass} applies here too}: at $\alpha{=}0.1$ condition~\eqref{eq:checkable_margin} holds in only $5$ of $12$ positive-weight rounds. The checkable form certifies less exactly where the condition it replaces also stops binding, and it does not rescue the guarantee there.

\noindent\textbf{A further reduction to $\rho$ alone is vacuous on our runs, and we report it as vacuous.} The natural next ask is the analogue of part (1)'s $\rho < r^\star$: a threshold on $\rho$ computable from the \emph{pre-composition} round geometry, so that no pilot is needed at all. Such a threshold exists formally. Bounding $\Delta'_{\mathrm{sep}} \geq w_{\min}[\Delta_{\mathrm{sep}} - (\rho-1)N_A]$ and $R'_B \leq 2 w_{\min}[\rho R_B + (\rho-1)\|\mu_B\|]$, where $\Delta_{\mathrm{sep}}$, $R_B$ and $\mu_B$ are the untransformed analogues and $N_A := \max_a\|u_a\|$, makes $w_{\min}$ cancel and turns \eqref{eq:checkable_margin} into a linear inequality in $\rho$ with a critical value $\rho^\star$ depending only on $(\Delta_{\mathrm{sep}}, R_B, \|\mu_B\|, N_A, n_a, n_b)$. \textbf{It is empty on our data.} It requires $\Delta_{\mathrm{sep}} > 4(1{+}C_A)R_B$, and the measured $\Delta_{\mathrm{sep}}/[4(1{+}C_A)R_B]$ clears $1$ in exactly $1$ of the $17$ positive-weight rounds. Its maximum is $1.015$ at $\alpha{=}0.5$, attained in that $n_a{=}1$ round where $C_A = 2.67$ makes the requirement loosest, with mean $0.754$ over those $15$ rounds and a maximum of only $0.389$ over the two $n_a{=}2$ rounds; at $\alpha{=}0.1$ the maximum is $0.853$. Consequently $\rho^\star \leq 1$ in $16$ of $17$ rounds at $\alpha{=}0.5$ and in all $12$ at $\alpha{=}0.1$, against a measured $\rho$ of at least $1.034$ and $1.015$ in the two settings respectively; in the one round where $\rho^\star$ does exceed $1$ it reaches only $1.006$, against a measured $\rho$ of $1.196$ in that round. The reduced condition is therefore met in \emph{no} round, which is a stronger negative than a merely conservative threshold. The looseness is in the composition of the two bounds rather than in either one separately, and closing it would need a curvature assumption on the benign configuration that we are not prepared to assume. \textbf{We therefore claim no pilot-free screen for the geometric class}, and the calibration protocol below prices it accordingly.

\noindent\textbf{What part (2) does not assume.} Recorded explicitly, because the step invites readings it does not support: there is no honest-majority assumption on the \emph{weights}, no requirement that adversarial updates lie outside the benign convex hull, and the count majority $n_b > n_a$ is used in part (2) only through Corollary~\ref{cor:checkable_margin}, nowhere in the theorem's own proof.

Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}(2) orders residuals \emph{per client}: each adversarial point receives less inverse-residual weight than each benign point. That alone does not bound the adversary's \emph{total} share of the aggregate: several adversaries could each be down-weighted yet collectively hold substantial mass. The separation margin controls this, but only quantitatively:

\begin{corollary}[Bounded aggregate adversarial influence]
\label{cor:aggregate_mass}
Let $m := \Delta'_{\mathrm{sep}}/[2(R'_B+\delta')]$ be the realized separation margin, so Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}(2) reads $m>1$. Every benign residual is at most $D_b := R'_B+\delta'$ and every adversarial residual at least $D_a := \Delta'_{\mathrm{sep}}-R'_B-\delta' = (2m-1)D_b$. Bounding $\sum_j 1/d_j$ below by the benign terms alone and each adversarial term above by $1/D_a$,
\[
\Lambda_a \;=\; \sum_{a}\lambda_a \;\leq\; \frac{n_a/D_a}{n_b/D_b + n_a/D_a} \;=\; \frac{n_a}{n_b(2m-1)+n_a},
\]
so $\mu'$ is a convex combination whose adversarial share is at most $\Lambda_a$: writing $\mu' = (1-\Lambda_a)\bar u_B + \Lambda_a \bar u_A$ for its benign and adversarial parts, the displacement from the benign-only combination $\bar u_B$ is at most $\Lambda_a\|\bar u_A - \bar u_B\|$.
\end{corollary}

\noindent The bound is only as good as the margin, and \textbf{the separation condition alone is not enough}: as $m\to1$ it degrades to $n_a/(n_b+n_a)$, permitting the adversary $40\%$ of the mass at $n_a{=}2$, $n_b{=}3$. A margin comfortably above $1$ does the work. We measure $m \geq 2.82$ in every round with $w_a>0$ (mean $4.71$ over those rounds), capping the adversarial share at $5.1\%$ for $n_a{=}1$ and $12.6\%$ for $n_a{=}2$. The realized figure is far tighter than the bound: measuring $\Lambda_a$ directly, it never exceeds $1.8\%$ in any round, and the bound is satisfied in all 17 measured rounds with $w_a>0$. With Lemma~\ref{lem:annihilation} this completes the picture, and the mechanism survives for \emph{opposite} reasons: at $w_a{=}0$ the adversaries hold most of the Weiszfeld mass ($\Lambda_a=0.71$ measured) but carry no payload; at $w_a>0$ they carry payload but hold at most $12.6\%$.

\noindent\textbf{This is the one place in the paper where the theory predicts a number and the measurement lands inside it}, so we state the pair plainly: \textbf{bound $12.6\%$, realized $\le 1.8\%$}, in every measured round with $w_a>0$. \textbf{And it is also the one place where heterogeneity makes the guarantee measurably empty.} The bound is a function of the realized margin $m$ alone, and at Dirichlet $\alpha{=}0.1$ RFA's \textbf{all-round mean} margin \textbf{collapses from $m{=}3.24$ to $m{=}1.01$}, exactly the limit at which Corollary~\ref{cor:aggregate_mass} degrades to $n_a/(n_b{+}n_a)$ and certifies nothing, with two of three certified compositions crossing the ASR threshold there on the raw reading, one of three once our own accuracy floor is applied (§\ref{sec:oos_pass}(E)). So the theorem's explanatory value is genuine and measured in \emph{both} directions: it predicts a number that holds where heterogeneity is moderate, and its own quantity records where it stops holding. \textbf{That is not a robustness check; it is one of the paper's three central empirical findings} (§\ref{app:targeted_full}, §\ref{sec:conclusion}).

\paragraph{$\Lambda_a$ is level (P4) in measurable form, but (P4) is a \emph{level}, not a single metric.} Before the instantiation, one clarification the rest of the paper depends on: \textbf{(P4) is a conceptual level of the hierarchy and is instantiated defense-specifically}, because different aggregator families have different notions of what it means for adversarial input to enter the aggregate at all. A \emph{selector} admits or does not, so its (P4) is an indicator in $\{0,1\}$; a \emph{weighted averager} has an adversarial coefficient share in $[0,1]$; a \emph{coordinate-wise order statistic} has a fraction of selected coordinates that are adversarial. There is no single scalar all three compute, and this is why the influence column of Table~\ref{tab:channels_full} is \textbf{necessarily aggregator-specific} rather than a common measurement across rows: a fact about the level, not a shortcoming of the table. With that said: Definition~\ref{def:levels_full}'s levels are not equally meaningful. (P1)--(P3) are properties of a statistic and of a decision rule reading it; \textbf{(P4) is the first level whose content is the adversary's influence on the aggregate}, and for a geometric aggregator $\Lambda_a$ is exactly that level made measurable: Corollary~\ref{cor:aggregate_mass} bounds it, Lemma~\ref{lem:annihilation} drives it to a benign-only convex combination, and §\ref{app:targeted_full}'s instruments intervene on it directly. So the negative results do not leave the theory empty-handed: \textbf{the level the evidence points to is one the theory can bound, just not from a $(d_1,d_2)$ definition pair alone}, which is what a screen would need. That gap is the open problem we state in §\ref{sec:conclusion}. \textbf{Three quantities are distinguished throughout and never used interchangeably}: \emph{admitted adversarial mass}, whether an adversarial update enters $d_2$'s aggregate at all, binary for a selector and a coordinate fraction for an order statistic; the \emph{adversarial coefficient share}, the fraction of the upstream coefficient vector's total mass held by adversaries, which is what the instruments of §\ref{app:targeted_full} pin; and \emph{aggregate adversarial influence} $\Lambda_a$, the weight the adversary's direction actually carries in the aggregate. Unqualified, ``admission'' means the first. (Symbols are collected in Appendix~\ref{app:notation}.)

\begin{lemma}[Zero-weight annihilation]
\label{lem:annihilation}
Zero-weight clients are \emph{not} removed from the composition: $d_2$ receives them as zero vectors and still sees all $K$ points. Our implementation runs the $\varepsilon$-smoothed Weiszfeld iteration ($\varepsilon=10^{-8}$), whose fixed point is $\mu' = \sum_i \lambda_i u'_i$ with $\lambda_i \propto 1/\max(\|u'_i-\mu'\|,\varepsilon)$: a convex combination for \emph{every} configuration, including the singular case $\mu'\in U'$ that a zero-weight client at the origin can create, so no non-degeneracy assumption is required. If $w_a = 0$ for every adversarial client, then $u'_a = 0$ and, writing $\Lambda_a := \sum_{a}\lambda_a$, the adversarial terms vanish identically:
$\mu' = \sum_{b\text{ benign}}\lambda_b u'_b = (1-\Lambda_a)\,\bar u$ with $\bar u$ a convex combination of benign transformed updates. The aggregate is a \emph{contraction by $1-\Lambda_a\in(0,1)$ of a benign-only weighted average}: the attack direction never enters $\mu'$. The origin points' only effect is that contraction: a reduction in effective step size, i.e.\ a utility cost, not a security failure. The same holds for CoordMedian, where a zeroed coordinate carries no payload but shrinks the aggregate when selected.
\end{lemma}

\noindent\textbf{The iteration is truncated, and the lemma's conclusion does not depend on that.} The shipped code stops at \texttt{max\_iter}${}={}50$ or when successive iterates differ by less than \texttt{tol}${}={}10^{-6}$, in \texttt{fl\_core/federated.py} and, with the same two constants hardcoded, in the upstream-stage branch of \texttt{apply\_d1\_transform}. It returns the iterate \emph{preceding} the one that passes that test, which on a first-iteration exit is the plain mean. So $\lambda_i \propto 1/\max(\|u'_i-\mu'\|,\varepsilon)$ holds at the fixed point the lemma reasons about and not at the vector actually returned, whose weights are reciprocal distances to the \emph{previous} iterate, or uniform. \textbf{What the lemma needs survives truncation exactly}: every iterate has the form $\sum_i \lambda_i u'_i$ with $\lambda$ convex, and a zero-weight client contributes $\lambda_a \cdot 0 = 0$ whatever $\lambda_a$ is, so the adversarial terms vanish identically at every iterate and the contraction reading is unchanged. We record this because the lemma is stated about a fixed point and the implementation does not reach one.

\noindent\textbf{Why the positive-weight restriction matters.} Defining $\rho$ over all weights would make it \emph{infinite in every round}: FoolsGold's normalization subtracts the minimum weight, so 2 of 5 clients receive exactly zero (measured, $N{=}10$, $K{=}5$). A bound of the form $\rho < 4$ would then never be satisfiable, and Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}(2) would be vacuous on the very composition it is stated over. Restricting $\rho$ to strictly positive weights and handling $w_a{=}0$ separately via Lemma~\ref{lem:annihilation} is what makes the analysis apply to the algorithm we actually run. \textbf{Both channels are drawn in Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}}, which is the composed pipeline as a DAG rather than the evaluation design of Figure~\ref{fig:identification}: the statistic path C2 is stated over, and the zero-weight arc that reaches the aggregate without passing through $\sigma_2$ at all.

\begin{figure}[htbp]
\centering
% Round 69: the ninth review asked twice for "a formal DAG of the composed pipeline" and pointed at

Mode~S cuts that arc by pinning every adversary's coefficient at $c{=}1$, which is why it isolates C2's causal content and also why it needs adversary identity and is an instrument rather than a defense. \textbf{A statistic-level argument constrains the top path and leaves the bottom one free}, which is the whole of why (P1)--(P3) do not deliver (P5).}
\label{fig:pipeline}
\end{figure}

\noindent\textbf{Operational calibration protocol.} To evaluate the criterion for ($d_1{\to}d_2$): \textbf{C1} is available from standard single-defense baselines (zero cost). \textbf{C2/C3 for the theorem-backed class} (FG or reputation as $d_1$; RFA, CM, TM as $d_2$): for the \emph{rank} aggregators, Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}(1)'s bound $\rho < r^\star$ needs only $\rho$ and the coordinate-wise separation, both read off the C1 baseline runs, so this case really is free. \textbf{For the \emph{geometric} case an earlier version of this paper claimed the same, and that was false}: Corollary~\ref{cor:checkable_margin} removes the one quantity that would have required running the composition, but the benign spread that replaces it is measured on the transformed points, so RFA carries the same short-pilot cost as the other families and we no longer exempt it. \textbf{C2/C3 for other families}: one short pilot (5--10 rounds, ${\sim}10\%$ of full cost) measuring $\sigma_2(T_1(u_a))$ vs.\ $\sigma_2(u_a)$ suffices. The full 50-round experiment runs only for criterion-PASS candidates. $\delta_2 = 0.5$ separates all PASS from C2-FAIL cases on the development set; insensitive to choice: any $\delta_2 \in [0.3, 0.7]$ yields identical classifications on all 42 pairs.

\begin{proposition}[Rank-invariance of coordinate-wise aggregators]
\label{prop:rank_invariance}
Let $d_2$ be a coordinate-wise rank aggregator (TrimmedMean or CoordMedian) with admission statistic $\sigma_k(u) = \mathrm{rank}_k(u)$ among the received updates $\{u_1, \ldots, u_m\}$. Let $T_1(u_i) = \alpha \cdot u_i$ be a \emph{uniform} positive rescaling ($\alpha > 0$, same for all clients). Then:
\begin{enumerate}[noitemsep,topsep=2pt]
\item $\sigma_k(T_1(u_i)) = \sigma_k(u_i)$ for all $i, k$, i.e., C2 and C3 hold trivially.
\item Under \emph{heterogeneous} rescaling $T_1(u_i) = \alpha_i \cdot u_i$ with $\alpha_i \neq \alpha_j$, rank preservation fails when rescaling reorders coordinates across clients: $\exists\, k$ s.t.\ $\mathrm{rank}_k(\alpha_i u_{i,k}) \neq \mathrm{rank}_k(u_{i,k})$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(1) Rank is invariant to positive scalar multiplication: $u_{i,k} > u_{j,k} \iff \alpha u_{i,k} > \alpha u_{j,k}$ for $\alpha > 0$. (2) Counterexample: let $u_{1,k} = 3$, $u_{2,k} = 2$, with $\alpha_1 = 0.1$, $\alpha_2 = 1$. Then $\alpha_1 u_{1,k} = 0.3 < \alpha_2 u_{2,k} = 2$, reversing the rank.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}
\label{cor:cm_robust}
CoordMedian (pure rank selection) is robust to C3 boundary compression under any uniform rescaling but vulnerable under heterogeneous client-specific rescaling (e.g., FoolsGold or reputation weights). This predicts that compositions X$\to$CoordMedian will show \emph{intermediate} ASR when $d_1$ applies heterogeneous weights: consistent with observed NC$\to$cm (0.433) and rfa$\to$cm (0.485), both near the 0.5 threshold.
\end{corollary}

Proposition~\ref{prop:rank_invariance} concerns rank under \emph{uniform} rescaling. The sharper question is which admission signals survive \emph{heterogeneous} per-client rescaling, since that is what every per-client defense in our menu applies: NormClip ($c_i = \min(1,\tau/\|u_i\|)$), reputation ($c_i \propto e^{-d_i/s}$) and RFA-reweighting ($c_i \propto 1/\max(\|u_i-\mu'\|,\varepsilon)$) are all of the form $T(u_i) = c_i u_i$ with $c_i > 0$ strictly, each positive by construction. FoolsGold is of the same form with $c_i \ge 0$ but attains $c_i = 0$ in \emph{every} round (its shift-and-clamp gives the least-trusted client weight exactly zero, 2 of 5 clients here), so it falls outside the strict hypothesis below and is governed by Lemma~\ref{lem:annihilation} instead: the same distinction we draw for FLTrust's ReLU zeros in §\ref{sec:prospective}. The strictly-positive case has a sharp answer, and it partitions the menu:

\begin{proposition}[Invariance classes under positive per-client rescaling]
\label{prop:invariance}
Let $T(u_i) = c_i u_i$ with $c_i > 0$, \textbf{strictly}: the form taken by NormClip ($c_i = \min(1, \tau/\|u_i\|)$), Reputation ($c_i \propto e^{-d_i/s}$) and RFA-reweighting ($c_i \propto 1/\max(\|u_i - \mu'\|, \varepsilon)$), each positive by construction. \textbf{FoolsGold is deliberately not in that list}: its shift-and-clamp gives the least-trusted client weight exactly $0$ in \emph{every} round (2 of 5 clients here), so a zero coefficient is not a \emph{positive} rescaling and FoolsGold-as-$d_1$ falls outside the hypothesis, governed by Lemma~\ref{lem:annihilation} rather than by (a)--(c). Then:
\begin{enumerate}[noitemsep,topsep=2pt,label=(\alph*)]
\item \textbf{Cosine similarity is exactly invariant:} $\cos(c_iu_i, c_ju_j) = \cos(u_i,u_j)$. FoolsGold's weight vector is a function of the pairwise-cosine matrix alone, so $w(T(U)) = w(U)$ identically, and \textbf{C2 holds for every $\cdot{\to}$FoolsGold composition} (the $\cdot$ standing for any upstream $d_1$ throughout).
\item \textbf{Coordinate ordering is not invariant} in general; it survives under the bounded-ratio condition of Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}(1).
\item \textbf{Consensus distance and residual magnitude are not invariant.} Heterogeneous $c_i$ can invert their ordering, so $\cdot{\to}$reputation compositions fail C2, and $\cdot{\to}$RFA needs the separation condition of Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}(2).
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(a) $c_iu_i/\|c_iu_i\| = u_i/\|u_i\|$ for $c_i>0$, so every pairwise cosine, each client's normalized maximum similarity, and hence every FoolsGold weight is unchanged. (c) Counterexample for consensus distance: with $u_1{=}(1,0)$, $u_2{=}(0,1)$, $u_3{=}(3,3)$ the coordinate-wise median is $(1,1)$ and $u_3$ is farthest; under $c=(1,1,0.1)$ the median becomes $(0.3,0.3)$ and $u_3$ becomes the \emph{closest} point, reversing the ordering reputation depends on.
\end{proof}

\noindent This corrects a natural but wrong intuition. Norm clipping does \emph{not} blind FoolsGold: it cannot, by (a), though it does blind reputation, whose signal is consensus distance rather than similarity. We verified (a) in the shipped implementation: FoolsGold's weight vector changes by at most $3\times10^{-8}$ (float32 rounding) under NormClip, across 1000 synthetic trials and live FL rounds in which 1--2 of 5 clients were genuinely clipped (raw norms up to $24.5$ against $\tau{=}5$). Note this also means Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}(3)'s norm-equalizing degenerate case requires $\tau$ below \emph{all} client norms; at our $\tau{=}5$ only 1--2 of 5 clients clip per round, so norms are not equalized and that case does not describe our runs.


\subsection{The framework in general form}
\label{sec:general_form}

Positive rescaling is the instantiation that matters for current defenses, but the structure is more general, and stating it separates what is essential to the framework from what is an artifact of today's defense menu. Write $d_2$'s admission mechanism as a statistic $S(U)$ of the round's updates together with a decision rule that consumes only the \emph{ordering} $S$ induces over clients, which are extreme, which are far from consensus, which are down-weighted. Let $T$ be $d_1$'s per-client transform. Then $(d_1\to d_2)$ is \textbf{mechanism-preserving} exactly when
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:general_form}
S(T(U)) \;=\; \varphi\bigl(S(U)\bigr) \quad\text{for some } \varphi \text{ preserving the ordering } d_2 \text{ acts on,}
\end{equation}
which is C2; C3 additionally asks that $\varphi$ not compress the quantitative gap below $d_2$'s operating threshold. Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance} is the case $T(u_i)=c_iu_i$: for $S=$ cosine similarity, $\varphi=\mathrm{id}$, so \eqref{eq:general_form} holds unconditionally; for $S=$ coordinate value, $\varphi$ is a per-client positive scaling, order-preserving only under the bounded-ratio condition $\rho < r^\star$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}(1); for $S=$ consensus distance or residual magnitude, no $\varphi$ exists, because the reference point (median, geometric median) moves with $T$, so $S(T(U))$ is not a function of $S(U)$ at all. Three consequences worth stating. First, this is why the conditions are checkable without running an experiment: one reads $S$ off $d_2$'s definition and $T$ off $d_1$'s, and the question becomes algebraic. Second, it identifies what a genuinely new instantiation would require: an $S$ and a non-rescaling $T$, e.g.\ a $d_1$ that projects updates onto a subspace or applies a coordinate-wise mask, for which \eqref{eq:general_form} would need to be re-derived rather than inherited. Third, it marks the framework's real boundary: a $d_1$ whose transform is not per-client (one that mixes updates across clients, or carries cross-round state) admits no $T$ of this form, and nothing here applies to it.

\section{The intervention evidence: the confounded ladder, and the instrument it motivated}
\label{app:ladder}

\textbf{The controlled upstream transform.} We introduce a synthetic $d_1$ whose only parameter is the \emph{dispersion} of a positive per-client rescaling. For the $K$ participants of a round, at position $j$ in a $(\text{seed},\text{round})$-keyed pseudorandom permutation,
\begin{equation}
c_j \;\propto\; \exp\!\Big(\kappa \cdot \Big(\tfrac{2j}{K-1} - 1\Big)\Big), \qquad \text{normalized so } \textstyle\frac{1}{K}\sum_j c_j = 1 .
\label{eq:dose}
\end{equation}\begin{table}[h]

The statistic each $d_2$ reads is, in order, summed pairwise Euclidean distance, distance to the client median, pairwise cosine, and coordinate ordering. \emph{Standalone} is $d_2$'s own ASR (clean accuracy) on that attack, the C1 input, held fixed within every arm. \emph{Measured disturbance} is the fraction of live rounds on which the rung changes that arm's decision (for \texttt{coord\_median}, the mean fraction of \emph{coordinates} whose median-attaining client changes) measured before the freeze on the same raw updates, with no ASR computed (\texttt{results/dose\_disturbance.json}). Shapes and decision rules are frozen at commit \texttt{e711a95}.}
\label{tab:dose_design}
