\textbf{The abscissa is measured, not nominal.} What matters to C2 is not $\rho$ but what the rescaling does to the statistic $d_2$ actually reads, so before freezing any prediction we pushed live FL updates through the shipped transform and recorded each arm's decision at every $\kappa$ on the \emph{same} raw updates, computing no ASR. That establishes three things before any outcome exists (Table~\ref{tab:dose_design}): \emph{there is a dose}: disturbance is exactly $0$ at the identity rung and rises monotonically with $\kappa$ in every non-invariant arm; \emph{the class-(a) invariance claim holds in the shipped code}: \texttt{cos\_krum}'s selection changes in $\mathbf{0}$ of $120$ cells at $\rho$ up to $54.6$; and \emph{class (b) is graded rather than binary}, at roughly half the class-(c) disturbance ($0.487$ vs.\ $0.867$/$0.933$ at $\kappa{=}2.0$), which is what arm 4's prediction turns on.

\textbf{Frozen predictions.} The \emph{primary} test is pooled: across all 16 $(\text{arm},\kappa)$ cells, the rise in ASR over that arm's own $\kappa{=}0$ rung is predicted to be positively rank-correlated with the measured disturbance rate (Spearman, one-sided, $\alpha = 0.05$). Its predictor is a \emph{measured property of the statistic}, not the identity of the defense and not its standalone effectiveness, both held fixed within every arm. The \emph{secondary} predictions are the per-arm shapes of Table~\ref{tab:dose_design}, tested by a one-sided Jonckheere--Terpstra trend test across the four ordered rungs at $n{=}5$, with arm 3's ``flat'' scored as an \emph{equivalence} claim at a $0.15$ margin fixed and justified in advance (under a quarter of the $+0.629$ within-defense gap for Krum (the $n{=}3$ value on record at the freeze; the later top-up puts it at $+0.716$, so the margin is if anything conservative) and $0.300+0.15 = 0.450 < 0.5$, so ``within the margin'' and ``still suppressing'' coincide for this arm) and arm 4's class-(b) signature being the \emph{shallower slope}.

\textbf{One prediction we deliberately do not make.} Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}(1)'s ordering-survival condition $\rho < r^\star$ would give arm 4 a crossing rung fixed by a measured $r^\star$, but \textbf{no frozen prediction here uses $r^\star$}: we measured it and it is \textbf{not a stable per-attack constant}, ranging $0.00$--$12.59$ on model-scaling (median $0.57$) and $0.61$--$1.63$ on the pixel backdoor (median $0.92$) across the 12 rounds that sampled an adversary, so an illustrative value such as $r^\star{=}10$ is a stipulation rather than a measurement; §\ref{sec:restricted_theorem} states the condition symbolically and stipulates no value for it. This is a limitation of our own theorem that the dose-response measurement surfaced, reported as one (L5) rather than quietly left unchecked.

\subsection{Outcome of the confounded design: one arm confirmed, two refuted, one indeterminate, and the pooled test fails}
\label{sec:dose_outcome}

\noindent \emph{What did the outcome-gated ladder actually return, arm by arm?}
All 80 runs completed (16.5 h wall clock). Table~\ref{tab:dose_outcome} is the whole ladder; Figure~\ref{fig:dose_ladder} plots it on the nominal dose axis against the frozen shapes and Figure~\ref{fig:dose_measured} on the independently measured disturbance axis with the real-transform anchors, scored by \texttt{analyze\_dose\_response.py} against the rules frozen at \texttt{e711a95}, every number recomputed per seed from \texttt{results/dose\_response/}. The \textbf{primary} pooled test does not confirm: across the 16 cells, the rise in ASR over each arm's own $\kappa{=}0$ rung correlates with the measured disturbance at Spearman $\rho_s = +0.351$, one-sided $p = 0.091$, so the frozen criterion ($\rho_s > 0$ at $p < 0.05$) is not met and \textbf{we report the primary prediction as refuted}. Of the four frozen shapes one is confirmed, two are refuted, and one is indeterminate; \textbf{in three of the four arms ASR \emph{falls} as the dose rises}. This is the round's headline result and it is a negative one. It is also the outcome the pre-registration anticipated and named in advance: ``pooled correlation absent while individual arms move: the mechanism is defense-specific and measured disturbance is not a transferable predictor.''

Stars and crosses are the two \emph{real} upstream transforms measured on these same $(d_2, \text{attack})$ cells (NormClip, RFA), placed at their own measured disturbance rate: at matched disturbance they sit $+0.77$ and $+0.79$ ASR \emph{above} the synthetic curve, while their realized weight ratios (mean $3.61$ and $11.93$, maximum $4.90$ and $17.38$) fall \emph{inside} the ladder's span. The class-(a) arm's four rungs coincide at disturbance exactly $0$ ($0/120$ selections changed, maximum relative score drift $1.1\times10^{-4}$) and its ASR falls anyway: Lemma~\ref{lem:annihilation}'s magnitude channel, not C2 disturbance.}

\textbf{Arm 2 confirms: the ladder yields one prospective confirmation, while the pooled prediction is rejected.} Reputation under model-scaling rises monotonically from $0.017$ at the identity rung to $0.487$ at $\rho = 7.39$ and $0.779$ at $\rho = 54.6$ (Jonckheere--Terpstra $z = +4.64$, one-sided $p = 1.7\times10^{-6}$; permutation $p < 5\times10^{-5}$), while clean accuracy moves only $0.777 \to 0.701$, so what is lost is suppression, not the model. Same $d_2$, same attack, same standalone baseline at every rung: nothing about effectiveness varies, and $55\times$ dispersion in a \emph{positive per-client rescaling} (a transform the criterion treats as benign for invariant statistics) carries a defense from $0.017$ across the $0.5$ threshold. Two qualifications keep this in proportion. It validates the \emph{destruction} half of C2's mechanism (a non-invariant statistic, actually disturbed, loses suppression); the \emph{preservation} half (invariance $\Rightarrow$ suppression survives) is arm 3, which came out indeterminate. And even within this arm the measured disturbance is not what orders the curve: it saturates at $0.867$ by $\kappa{=}0.5$, where ASR is still $0.022$, and the jump to $0.487$ happens at the next rung at the same measured disturbance, so the \emph{dose} moves ASR here while the flip rate does not.

\textbf{Ecological validity: the real transforms do not lie on the synthetic curve.} Pre-registered caveat (iv) required this check, and it fails in an informative way. RFA$\to$Krum sits at ASR $0.779$ with measured disturbance $0.667$, where the synthetic ladder's nearest rung ($0.600$) gives $0.011$: a residual of $+0.767$. NormClip$\to$Reputation sits at $0.806$ with disturbance $0.333$, against $0.017$ at the identity rung: $+0.789$. Two anchors do land: RFA$\to$Reputation, $0.895$ at disturbance $1.000$ against $0.779$ at $0.933$ ($+0.116$), and both \texttt{cos\_krum} anchors, which coincide with its curve ($+0.000$ and $-0.044$) because that statistic is invariant under all three transforms. This is not a range mismatch: read off the same live rounds, NormClip realizes $\rho$ of mean $3.61$ (max $4.90$) and RFA mean $11.93$ (max $17.38$), both inside the ladder's $\{1.00, 2.72, 7.39, 54.60\}$ span. The conclusion is about our abscissa: \textbf{the rate at which a transform flips a defense's decision is not a sufficient statistic for what that transform does to the defense}: two transforms at matched flip rate differ by up to $0.77$ in ASR. \textbf{And the axis they \emph{are} unmatched on is now measured, not conjectured.} Adversary-correlated dispersion $\Delta_c := \mathbb{E}[c_i \mid i \in A] - \mathbb{E}[c_i \mid i \notin A]$ (§\ref{sec:composability_criterion}) separates the transforms carrying those anchors from the ladder: RFA reaches $\Delta_c = -1.21$ and NormClip $-0.72$, against $-0.34$ at the ladder's most dispersed rung: $2$--$3.6\times$ more extremely targeted at $\rho$ that is $3.7$ and $13\times$ \emph{smaller}, so \textbf{matched on the statistic, badly unmatched on the targeting, and $\rho$ does not order $\Delta_c$}. \textbf{This rescues no predictor}: $\Delta_c$'s magnitude does not order suppression either; rfa$\to$krum still fails at $0.779$ at $\Delta_c = -1.21$, while \texttt{reputation}, targeted harder still on both attacks, has rep$\to$coord\_median suppressing, and FoolsGold's $\Delta_c$ is \emph{positive} ($+0.663$). These figures are \textbf{post-hoc and exploratory}, computed with no ASR and rescoring nothing, and are tabulated in the supplement (§\ref{app:delta_c}). Arm 4 has no real-transform anchor at all, since \texttt{coord\_median} was not on the metric-swap menu, so its ecological validity is untested.

\textbf{What we now claim less of.} C2's causal content is established prospectively for one statistic (distance to the client median) and refuted for another (summed pairwise distance) at $\rho$ up to $54.6$; the pooled claim that measured disturbance predicts loss of suppression \emph{across} defenses is refuted, and we no longer make it. Within-defense disturbance remains the only design the testability boundary admits, and §\ref{app:boundary}'s general statement stands untouched by this outcome; it is a statement about admissible designs, and this is the admissible design, run and reported as it landed. What changes is the empirical status of C2: one confirmed arm, not a validated screening mechanism.

\subsection{The targeted intervention: isolating C2 from the attenuation channel}
\label{app:targeted_full}
\label{app:channel_table}

\noindent \emph{With the adversary's share pinned, how much of each arm's rise survives?}
\textbf{Reading \texttt{cos\_krum}'s entry in Table~\ref{tab:channels}.} Its $-0.425$ is the full-ladder endpoint, which lies below our accuracy floor; \textbf{the witness we rest on is instead its first, gate-passing rung} ($0.173$, §\ref{sec:targeted_rest}). The rank discordance between $\Delta\Lambda_a$ and $\Delta$ASR across the two moving arms is §\ref{sec:targeted_rest}'s refutation of our own predicted ordering. This section gives the emitter and the per-artifact provenance for every cell of that table.

\textbf{Round counts and per-row provenance, relocated here from the table's caption.} \emph{Admission} here is Table~\ref{tab:channels}'s $\Delta$~adm., the \emph{support} of the admitted adversarial mass, which that caption keeps distinct from the mass itself ($\Delta\Lambda_a$); it never moves in any measured round of any arm: $60$ rounds per CIFAR-10 \texttt{cifar\_cnn} arm, $56$ on the EMNIST-byclass replication and $72$ on the ResNet18 replication, $368$ in all, including the $48$ per arm where reputation and \texttt{coord\_median} carry adversarial mass at baseline, so the constancy is not an artifact of arms that never admit an adversary at all. The decision/admission dissociation is accordingly not one aggregator's property: it holds for a selector, a cosine-invariant selector, a coordinate-wise order statistic and a weighted averager, which is what the table's \emph{Kind} column records. Row~5 replicates row~1 on a second dataset and a differently configured CNN, with its channels measured \emph{before} its ASR existed; row~6 is the pre-registered score-only control, row~1 with the magnitude channel closed, so its decision, admission and influence columns are row~1's own; row~7 replicates row~1 with \emph{architecture} varying alone, its channels also measured before its ASR existed (§\ref{app:resnet18_dose}). \textbf{Row~7 is printed last rather than beside row~5, which is where it belongs by subject}: four sites in this paper index the score-only control as row~6, and the emitter orders the rows so that adding a seventh cannot silently falsify them. The table is \emph{not} an out-of-sample test. \textbf{Row~5 is the frozen $n{=}3$ in every column, its $\Delta$~ASR included}, because its three channel columns were measured at that seed count and were not re-measured: the same cell's $\Delta$~ASR at $n{=}5$ is $-0.013$, reported with its provenance in disclosure~\emph{(i)} below, and substituting it here would put one $n{=}5$ cell in a row of $n{=}3$ ones.

\label{app:score_only}

\textbf{What Mode~S does to the ladder's confirmation.} Reputation was the ladder's one confirmed arm (§\ref{sec:dose_outcome}); Mode~S shows most of that confirmation was attenuation. With the adversary's share held fixed, reputation's rise at $\rho{=}54.6$ shrinks from the ladder's $+0.762$ to $+0.178$ (one-sided Welch $t{=}{-}5.22$, $p{=}0.0017$ that it is smaller), and what remains tracks a small \emph{admission} change ($0.000 \to 0.020$) rather than the large decision change ($0.000 \to 1.000$), so H-admission is confirmed here too (Table~\ref{tab:targeted}). The confound is thus \emph{explained}, not explained away: a genuine, much smaller, admission-driven effect survives it. The class-(a) control cuts the other way and refutes us: \texttt{cos\_krum}'s selection is bit-identical at every rung (decision and admission both $0.000$), yet ASR is $0.584$ at the identity (it never suppresses) and \emph{falls} $0.425$ across the ladder ($p_{\downarrow}{=}0.0008$; the $\kappa{=}2$ rung is below the accuracy gate). Exact invariance of the statistic neither implies suppression nor holds it fixed; the magnitude channel moves it regardless. We report this as a refutation of Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance}'s practical content, not a confirmation of its algebra.

\textbf{Mode A: the two regimes, and where they hold.} The payload dose gives Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} a single falsifiable shape: ASR single-peaked at $r{=}1$, since an over-attenuated adversary carries no payload (Regime~B) and an over-amplified one is discarded by a rank aggregator (Regime~A). It is \textbf{confirmed for \texttt{coord\_median}}, the rank aggregator the regimes are stated for: ASR rises $0.124 \to 0.315 \to 0.443$ into $r{=}1$ and falls $0.443 \to 0.296 \to 0.098$ past it, both sides significant ($p_{\downarrow}{=}3.0{\times}10^{-4}$ amplified, $6.4{\times}10^{-4}$ attenuated) at accuracy $\ge 0.74$ throughout (Figure~\ref{fig:modeA}). It is \textbf{refuted for reputation}, which is monotone: ASR is maximal at the most attenuated rung ($0.894$ at $r{=}0.135$) and near zero once $r \ge 1$, because an attenuated adversary reads as \emph{consistent} to a reputation score and stops being down-weighted. Reputation is not a rank aggregator, so the two-regime shape was never licensed for it; the split is the theorem behaving exactly where it applies and not where it does not.

Mode~A is the $\Delta_c$-\emph{swept} companion to Mode~S's $\Delta_c$-pinned design (Proposition~\ref{prop:rho_deltac}); Mode~S itself is Figure~\ref{fig:modeS_dose}. \paragraph{A pre-registered replication on a second downstream defense, and what it refutes.} One arm cannot establish which channel matters, and the two Mode-S arms above move together on both candidate predictors: reputation is higher on \emph{both} decision and admission change and also had the larger rise, so nothing in that pair separates them. \texttt{coord\_median} under the pixel backdoor is \emph{rank-discordant} and therefore does: of the interpretable Mode-S arms it has the \textbf{smallest decision change} ($0.482$) and the \textbf{largest admission change} ($0.033$), so the decision reading predicts its rise is the \emph{smallest} of the three and the admission reading predicts the \emph{largest}. It is also a structurally different defense, a coordinate-wise order statistic (Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance}(b)) against Krum's selector (class (c)), and it suppresses the pixel backdoor at usable accuracy, so there is suppression to lose. Thresholds were frozen against the two published rises (confirm if $\Delta > +0.178$, refute if $\Delta < +0.150$, indeterminate between) at commit \texttt{06d9bce} before \texttt{results/dose\_replication/} existed, with the identity rung imported bit-identical and $15$ new runs spent (\texttt{experiments/pre\_registration\_dose\_replication.md}).

\textbf{The outcome is $\Delta = +0.098$ at $n{=}5$: the admission \emph{ordering} is refuted.} The arm with the largest admission change rose \emph{less} than an arm with a smaller one, so \textbf{admission magnitude does not order outcomes across arms}. Neither does the decision reading, which predicted the \emph{smallest} rise of the three when $+0.098$ is the middle one, so this arm refutes a rank prediction from \emph{each} candidate channel. The pooled model agrees: re-fit with these cells it is still not confirmed ($R^2{=}0.239$; the admission coefficient's one-sided $p$ moves $0.055 \to 0.041$, but the adversarial-share coefficient is negative, so the pre-specified conjunction fails). \textbf{What does replicate is the negative}: a decision change of $0.482$ costs no suppression by the frozen margin, at mean accuracy $0.698$, so the accuracy gate does not bind and the flagship result now holds on two downstream defenses in \emph{different} invariance classes. Two qualifications, the first pre-specified in the freeze and the second post-hoc: this cell's identity ASR is $0.443$, so equivalence is to the \emph{identity rung} and not to a low absolute ASR (an ``every rung ${<}0.5$'' criterion has no headroom here and was deliberately not used); and the rise is positive in $5/5$ seeds (paired $t{=}3.39$, one-sided $p{=}0.014$, \emph{not} pre-registered), so on this class the cost of disturbing the decision is \textbf{small and consistent rather than zero}. All numbers are recomputed per seed by \texttt{analyze\_dose\_replication.py}, which imports its rung builder and trend test from the frozen suite's scorer.

\paragraph{Two admissible estimands, opposite conclusions on the same cell.} This is the paper's central empirical result. On this identical cell (matched aggregate displacement, decision change and clean accuracy, Table~\ref{tab:comparability}), \textbf{the confounded design does not lose power, it points the other way}: at $n{=}20$ the outcome-gated ladder moves $-0.273$ ($0.478 \to 0.205$) where the instrument moves $+0.125$ ($0.478 \to 0.603$), the two paired $95\%$ intervals $[-0.334,-0.213]$ and $[+0.095,+0.155]$ disjoint and both excluding zero. Closing the attenuation channel does not sharpen a weak positive effect, it \textbf{inverts it}. \textbf{The two designs are not the same intervention}, and Table~\ref{tab:comparability} states the one non-match: $\Lambda_a$ moves in both ($0.081$ against $0.033$, both $n{=}5$), so neither column is a clean statistic-only intervention and no $p$-value attaches to the difference between them. What the contrast establishes is that the choice between two admissible estimands sets the inferred direction, so a criterion calibrated against the confounded contrast would have been calibrated against the confound, and a cross-defense contrast would have read the fall as evidence about the statistic. That is why the identification boundary of §\ref{app:boundary} is not a technicality about power.

\paragraph{What the two arms jointly establish, stated formally.} The arms above and the class-(a) control (\texttt{cos\_krum}) are between them a \emph{constructive} answer to the question C2 was written to settle. Both halves are witnessed by runs already reported, so the following formalizes measurements rather than adding a claim.

% Round 71, review P2: this statement is now the body's Prop.~\ref{prop:nonidentifiability} at the head
% of §\ref{sec:targeted}, and this copy is the restatement carrying the proof -- the same body/appendix
% pair Prop.~\ref{prop:identification} and Prop.~\ref{prop:identification_full} already form, with the
% same title convention. The two paragraphs below ("What the proposition does not say" and the corollary)
% stay HERE with the proof rather than travelling: each is a scope clause on a proved statement, and the
% body copy carries its own one-sentence scope clause so no disclosure depends on the reader reaching
% this page.
\begin{proposition}[Statistic-level non-identifiability; Proposition~\ref{prop:nonidentifiability} of the body, restated with proof]
\label{prop:nonidentifiability_full}
Within the positive per-client rescaling class there exist transform families $T_1,T_2$ and downstream defenses $d_2$ such that
(a) $T_1$ and $T_2$ induce arbitrarily different statistics and \emph{decisions} at $d_2$ while inducing the \emph{same} admitted adversarial mass and the same suppression; and
(b) $T_1$ and $T_2$ induce the \emph{same} admitted adversarial mass (indeed the same statistic and the same decision) while inducing \emph{different} suppression.
Consequently no predicate of the downstream statistic alone determines the admitted adversarial mass, and no predicate of the admitted mass alone determines suppression.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Both halves are exhibited by runs reported in this section. (a) Mode~S into Krum under model-scaling, $\kappa{=}0$ against $\kappa{=}2$: $\rho$ moves from $1$ to $54.6$ and Krum's decision changes in up to $0.80$ of rounds, while the support of the admitted adversarial mass changes in none of the $60$ measured rounds (a floor on this arm: no adversary is admitted at any rung), the adversarial coefficient share is constant at $0.266667$, and suppression shows \textbf{no evidence of a practically meaningful change under the pre-registered $\pm0.15$ practical-equivalence margin} ($|\Delta|{=}0.026$). (b) \texttt{cos\_krum} under the pixel backdoor: by Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance}(a) its statistic is invariant, and its decision and admission are measured bit-identical ($0.000$) at every rung, yet ASR falls $0.173$ by the first rung alone, at $\rho{=}2.72$, where the accuracy gate still passes ($0.599 \to 0.567$; paired $t{=}{-}1.976$, one-sided $p{=}0.044$, falling in $5/8$ seeds).
\end{proof}

% Round 61, page gate: this sentence was the closing line of §5's design-comparison paragraph, and it
% is the ONLY statement in the corpus that a preserved statistic is not thereby uninformative. It is
% moved here rather than dropped, beside the proposition whose reading it guards, and §5 keeps the
% pointer it already carried. One home, and the home is the claim's own neighbourhood.
\noindent \textbf{What the proposition does not say.} A preserved statistic is not thereby uninformative; it is not \emph{sufficient}, remaining evidence about what $d_2$ reads and none about what it suppresses.

\noindent \textbf{The corollary is why the title says \emph{beyond} statistic preservation}: no metric defined \emph{solely} on preservation of the downstream statistic can identify attack-suppression equivalence, because (a) exhibits statistic disturbance without suppression change and (b) exhibits suppression change without statistic disturbance. \textbf{Scope, stated immediately}: this is a non-existence claim \emph{within the positive-rescaling class on these defenses and attacks}, established by exhibited counterexample: \textbf{not} a claim that no metric of any kind can order security, and not a claim about transforms outside the class.

\begin{proposition}[$\rho$ and $\Delta_c$ are independently controllable]
\label{prop:rho_deltac}
Within the same class, the statistic-level dispersion $\rho = \max_i c_i/\min_i c_i$ and the adversary-correlated dispersion $\Delta_c = \mathbb{E}[c_i \mid i \in A] - \mathbb{E}[c_i \mid i \notin A]$ are not functionally related: there is a family with $\Delta_c \equiv 0$ exactly while $\rho$ ranges over $[1, 54.6]$, and a family with the statistic's dispersion held fixed while $\Delta_c$ ranges over $[-1.138, +2.469]$. Hence neither quantity determines the other.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
By construction, with the realized values tabulated in the supplement (§\ref{app:delta_c}) and computed with no ASR involved. Mode~S pins every adversarial coefficient at $c{=}1$ and spreads only the benign clients, giving $\Delta_c \equiv 0$ at every $\rho$ up to $54.6$; Mode~A holds the benign weights uniform and sweeps only the adversary-to-benign ratio $r$, moving $\Delta_c$ over $[-1.138,+2.469]$ at fixed statistic dispersion.
\end{proof}

\noindent This is why the two instruments were the right ones, and it makes the supplement's §\ref{sec:rho_vs_deltac} warning precise: \textbf{a transform reported by $\rho$ alone leaves $\Delta_c$ entirely free}, so $\rho$ cannot be a sufficient statistic for what the transform does to a defense. It does \emph{not} follow that $\Delta_c$ is a predictor: §\ref{sec:dose_outcome} reports that its magnitude fails to order suppression either.

\paragraph{The dissociation is not Krum-specific: all four aggregators, all four channels.} Table~\ref{tab:channels_full} collects the endpoint change ($\kappa{=}0$ against $\kappa{=}2$) in each measured channel under Mode~S for every downstream defense in the paper. \textbf{Recomputed, not transcribed}: every cell is emitted by \texttt{build\_channel\_table.py}, which only reads frozen artifacts under \texttt{results/}, \texttt{admission\_\allowbreak measurement.json}, \texttt{femnist\_\allowbreak admission.json} and \texttt{resnet18\_\allowbreak admission.json} for the channel columns, the \texttt{targeted\_dose/}, \texttt{dose\_\allowbreak replication/}, \texttt{dose\_femnist/} and \texttt{dose\_\allowbreak resnet18/} summaries for the ASR column; it also asserts that the identity rung displaces nothing ($0.00\mathrm{e}{+}00$ for all seven rows). The sixth row's aggregate column comes from \texttt{displacement\_\allowbreak decomposition.json} and its ASR from \texttt{score\_only/summary.json}, both read-only; the emitter refuses to merge that arm into the ASR sources it shares cell keys with, so it cannot shadow the first row's published number. The influence column is \textbf{aggregator-specific by necessity} (a selector has no weights) and its instantiation is: adversarial share of the Weiszfeld/reputation weight mass for the averager, adversarial share of selected coordinates for the order statistic, and the indicator that an adversarial update is selected at all for the two selectors. The fifth row replicates the first row's arm on a second dataset and a differently configured CNN (EMNIST-byclass with \texttt{simple\_cnn}) with \textbf{its channels measured prospectively, before its ASR existed}; the result is reported below. \textbf{The seventh row replicates it with architecture varying alone} (CIFAR-10 with \texttt{resnet18}), its channels likewise measured before its ASR existed, and it is the one row that shares \emph{both} the first row's dataset and its cell key: the ASR lookup is keyed on the triple (dataset, model, cell) precisely so that the two cannot shadow each other, and the emitter raises rather than resolving a collision by file order (§\ref{app:resnet18_dose}). \textbf{What the $\Delta$~adm.\ column pools}: the four \texttt{cifar\_cnn} CIFAR-10 rows contribute $60$ measured rounds each, $240$ in total, and adversarial mass is present at baseline in $100$ of those $240$ rounds while its support moves in none of them, which is the count Figure~\ref{fig:modeS}'s annotation reports and why that annotation carries no single arm's name. \textbf{That $100$ is not spread evenly, and the split is the answer to the floor objection}: it is $0$ rounds for Krum, $4$ for \texttt{cos\_krum}, and $48$ each for reputation and \texttt{coord\_median}, so the admission reading is carried by the two arms that have room and not by the two that are at or near the floor. The emitter prints each arm's pre-transform admitted fraction, averaged over the rounds an adversary actually joins, and asserts that it is identical at the identity rung and at $\kappa{=}2$, since a level that moved with the dose would not be a baseline. \textbf{It also holds the aggregator fixed and varies the attack instead}, which is the only way to tell an aggregator's floor from an attack's, and the four CIFAR-10 aggregators read as follows under \texttt{committed\_\allowbreak scaling} against \texttt{committed\_\allowbreak pixel}, as level (nonzero rounds of $12$): Krum $0.000$ ($0$) against $0.333$ ($4$), \texttt{cos\_krum} $0.083$ ($1$) against $0.083$ ($1$), reputation $0.0014$ ($12$) against $0.251$ ($12$), \texttt{coord\_median} $0.156$ ($12$) against $0.286$ ($12$). So Krum's floor belongs to the attack: a model-scaling adversary is a norm outlier and Krum is built to reject one, while under the pixel backdoor the same Krum admits adversarial mass at baseline. That weakens the reading on which the floor is a property of the aggregator, and it does not remove the limitation, because the adjudicating arm does commit to scaling.

\end{table}

\paragraph{A second dataset and a differently configured CNN, pre-registered.} The fifth row's arm is the first row's arm re-run with EMNIST-byclass (62 classes, $1{\times}28{\times}28$) and \texttt{simple\_cnn} in place of CIFAR-10 and \texttt{cifar\_cnn}, everything else held: Mode~S, Krum as $d_2$, the committed model-scaling attack, the same four frozen rungs $\kappa \in \{0,0.5,1,2\}$. The decision rule lives in \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak pre\_registration\_dose\_femnist.md}, git-committed before any EMNIST-byclass ASR existed, and the runner refuses to start unless its \texttt{PREREG\_COMMIT} matches that hash.

\textbf{One naming correction, made here rather than left to be inferred.} Earlier drafts labelled this arm ``FEMNIST'' in the tables while the prose said EMNIST-byclass, and the tables were the wrong ones: the \texttt{femnist} key loads \emph{torchvision} \texttt{EMNIST(split="byclass")} and partitions it with this paper's own label-Dirichlet split, \textbf{not} LEAF FEMNIST's partition by writer. So the arm is a second dataset and a differently configured CNN, and it is \textbf{not} a more federated partition than the CIFAR-10 arm; it is the same partition scheme on different data. Every table now reads EMNIST-byclass. The frozen artifact paths, the pre-registration filename and the JSON dataset key retain the \texttt{femnist} spelling because renaming a committed artifact would break the provenance the rest of this appendix rests on.

\textbf{The premise was measured first, with no ASR anywhere.} Krum's selection changes on $0.643$--$0.714$ of rounds across the three disturbed rungs while the support of the adversarial mass it admits is unchanged in all $56$ measured rounds (again a floor: no adversary is admitted at any rung), so the arm has a real disturbance to test. Had the decision change come out near zero the arm would have been reported ineligible rather than run: that is the \texttt{cos\_krum} failure mode, and this check is what distinguishes the two.

\textbf{Suppression shows no evidence of a practically meaningful change.} $\Delta = -0.017$ (mean ASR $0.027 \to 0.010$) against the frozen $\pm 0.15$ practical-equivalence margin, whose lower side the identity mean $0.027$ satisfies by arithmetic before any run, so only the upper side is a test here and what it returns is a bound of $-0.001$ on any rise (App.~\ref{par:reach}), beside CIFAR-10's $-0.026$ on the same cell, with mean clean accuracy \emph{rising} $0.767 \to 0.835$ so the accuracy floor binds on no rung. Under the frozen rule this is a \textbf{replication on a second dataset and a differently configured CNN}. \textbf{That is narrower than the wording the pre-registration froze}, which reads ``a second dataset and architecture'': \texttt{simple\_cnn} and \texttt{cifar\_cnn} are two configurations of one shallow-convnet family, so we report the narrower phrase and leave the frozen file unedited, claiming less than a rule licenses being always permitted where claiming more is not. The prohibition we keep verbatim is the one that matters: never ``generalizes'', which one further dataset does not license.

\textbf{Three disclosures, each of them from the freeze rather than from hindsight.} (i) $n{=}3$ seeds per rung, not $5$, fixed in advance to match the EMNIST-byclass payoff matrix's own trial budget. \textbf{The frozen verdict is that $n{=}3$ one, and the same ladder also reads at $n{=}5$ with no new run needed}: the comparability amendment of §\ref{app:sixcell} ran seeds $45$ and $46$ of this exact cell at all four rungs, so pooling them with the frozen three, frozen rows winning and the frozen directory never written, gives $\Delta{=}{-}0.013$ beside $-0.017$, mean ASR $0.024 \to 0.011$ at accuracy $0.758 \to 0.841$: the same label under the same unmoved margin. Two more seeds \emph{widen} the interval across zero, $[-0.034,+0.009]$ against $n{=}3$'s $[-0.033,-0.001]$, so they remove an apparent small fall rather than confirm one, and \textbf{one of the five carries the opposite sign} (seed $45$, $+0.015$, its identity rung landing at ASR $0.000018$), which a 3-seed mean cannot show. Both counts are reported and neither replaces the other (\texttt{analyze\_dose\_femnist.py --pooled}). Those two seeds were pre-registered in the comparability document before they ran and are post-hoc in this arm's own. (ii) The secondary Jonckheere--Terpstra test was specified to detect a monotone \emph{rise} (the outcome that would have refuted the flat prediction with attenuation already closed by construction) and the observed trend is a \emph{fall} ($J{=}16.0$, $z{=}{-}1.563$, $p_{\uparrow}{=}0.941$; at $n{=}5$, $J{=}57.0$, $z{=}{-}1.211$, $p_{\uparrow}{=}0.887$, the same direction). We report it and we do \textbf{not} score it as support for flatness; the pre-registration states in advance that at $n{=}3$ a null from this test is not evidence of flatness, and two more seeds do not convert one into evidence. (iii) The two branches had unequal headroom. With the identity rung at ASR $0.027$, refuting us required mean ASR$(\kappa{=}2) > 0.177$ against a ceiling of $1$ and was fully reachable, whereas the indeterminate branch ($\Delta < -0.15$) was arithmetically unreachable. \textbf{Equivalence here is to the identity rung, not to a low absolute ASR.}

\textbf{Harness sanity, which is an identity check and not corroboration.} The $\kappa{=}0$ rung is identity$\circ$Krum, i.e.\ Krum alone, and it lands \emph{bit-identically} on the EMNIST-byclass Krum figure from our earlier payoff-matrix sweep ($0.0269784552$ at accuracy $0.7670795973$). Both pipelines are deterministic given a seed and both run three trials from base seed $42$, so the agreement shows they execute the same protocol rather than a similar one: the strongest form the pre-registered check can take, and for the same reason \textbf{not} independent corroboration of that figure: the same computation run twice cannot corroborate itself. \textbf{The pooled seeds of disclosure~\emph{(i)} now carry the same check across two runners.} Having already read those two seeds from the comparability suite, we ran this arm's own runner over the same eight $(\text{rung},\text{seed})$ cells, pre-registered as an amendment at \texttt{ad5479b} before those runs: it reproduces all eight to ten decimal places, largest gap $0.00\mathrm{e}{+}00$, so the pooled reading is not an artifact of which suite measured it (\texttt{analyze\_dose\_femnist.py --pooled}). The same reading applies: identical protocols, not independent confirmation.

\paragraph{A second architecture family, pre-registered, with the gate scored first.} The seventh row's arm is the first row's arm with \textbf{architecture varying alone}: \texttt{resnet18} (11.2M parameters, BatchNorm replaced by GroupNorm-8, \texttt{conv1} adapted to $32{\times}32$, \texttt{maxpool} $\to$ Identity) in place of \texttt{cifar\_cnn}, and CIFAR-10, $N{=}10$, $K{=}5$, $f{=}0.2$, $\alpha$, the committed model-scaling attack and Krum as $d_2$ all at their frozen values. The decision rule is \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak pre\_registration\_dose\_resnet18.md}, git-committed at \texttt{89046f6} before any ResNet18 ASR existed, and the runner refuses to start unless its \texttt{PREREG\_COMMIT} matches that hash. \textbf{Two endpoint rungs only}, $\kappa \in \{0,2\}$ of the frozen four-rung ladder ($\rho = 1.00 \to 54.60$), at seeds $42$--$44$: the interior rungs are not run, \textbf{so this arm carries no trend statistic at all} and the runner prints that restriction rather than leaving it to be inferred.

\textbf{The gate was scored before the ladder, and it is what makes the arm interpretable.} No ResNet18/Krum figure existed on disk, so the pre-registration named the void branch first: unless Krum alone suppresses the committed scaling attack on this architecture (mean ASR $< 0.5$ at mean clean accuracy $\ge 0.35$) \emph{there is no suppression for the ladder to preserve and the arm is void, not negative}. It passed: at $\kappa{=}0$, mean ASR $0.071$ at mean clean accuracy $0.536$, $n{=}3$. Had it failed, the runner refuses the $\kappa{=}2$ leg outright rather than spending it, and we would be reporting a void.

\textbf{Suppression does not rise, and under the rule this arm was frozen against the negative replicates on a second architecture family.} $\Delta = -0.041$: mean ASR $0.071$ at $\kappa{=}0$ against $0.030$ at $\kappa{=}2$, its magnitude inside the frozen $\pm0.15$ practical-equivalence margin, beside \texttt{cifar\_cnn}'s $-0.026$ and EMNIST-byclass's $-0.017$ on the same cell. \textbf{We do not write ``shows no evidence of a practically meaningful change'' about this arm, and disclosure~\emph{(v)} is why}: §\ref{app:sensitivity} defines that phrase by the paired $95\%$ interval lying inside the margin, and at $n{=}3$ this arm's does not. \textbf{Both endpoints are $n{=}3$ on the same three seeds}, so the difference of rung means and the paired mean difference are the same number to four places ($-0.0410$), and \texttt{seeds\_match} is true on the row; no leg is a mixed-$n$ comparison. Mean clean accuracy \emph{rises} $0.536 \to 0.557$, so the $0.35$ floor binds on neither rung. Under the frozen three-way rule ($|\Delta| < 0.15$ replicates, $\Delta > +0.15$ architecture-specific, $\Delta < -0.15$ a fall) this is a \textbf{replication across architecture families}, and it is the arm that earns the phrase: \texttt{simple\_cnn} and \texttt{cifar\_cnn} are two configurations of one shallow-convnet family, whereas an 11.2M-parameter residual network is not.

\textbf{Five disclosures about this arm, four of them limits.} (i) \textbf{The channels were measured prospectively and the admission column is vacuous here}, exactly as on rows~1 and~5. \texttt{results/\allowbreak resnet18\_\allowbreak admission.json} records ``No ASR. Freezes nothing.'' and predates the ladder; it reads the decision disturbed and the admitted adversarial mass not ($\Delta$~dec.\ $0.556$ against $\Delta$~adm.\ $0.000$ at the top rung), which is the premise the flagship arm rests on and the reason the cell was eligible at all. \textbf{But adversarial mass is never present at baseline in any of this arm's $72$ measured rounds}, so its $\Delta$~adm.\ $=0.000$ is vacuous and we do not report it as evidence; \texttt{count\_admission\_rounds.py} prints that verdict per row rather than letting a zero speak for itself. (ii) \textbf{Clean accuracy is far below this architecture's own reach}: $0.536$--$0.557$ against the $0.66$--$0.76$ the ResNet18 mini-sweep of §\ref{app:resnet18_replication_details} attains in the same regime, ResNet18 at $50$ rounds being far from converged. The gate is a floor and not a claim of a converged model, and no statement here is about converged training. (iii) \textbf{One cell, one attack, and it widens nothing else.} It does not add a dataset, an $N$, a $K$, an $f$, a round count or an attack, it carries no composition suite, and it does not touch the sign-reversal cell of §\ref{app:sixcell}. (iv) \textbf{Why this arm fits an overnight budget when §\ref{app:sixcell}'s disclosure~\emph{(iv)} prices ResNet18 at $65$~h}: that figure is a \emph{comparability} cell, two designs $\times$ four rungs $\times$ five seeds $=40$ runs at ${\approx}5900$~s. This arm is one design $\times$ two endpoint rungs $\times$ three seeds $=6$ runs, which is $13.4$~h as measured. The two numbers price different objects and neither supersedes the other. (v) \textbf{The interval is wider than the margin, so the frozen $\pm0.15$ is load-bearing for this arm and, among the arms of the margin ladder, for no other.} The paired $95\%$ interval is $[-0.183,+0.101]$ (per-seed ASR $0.114/0.050/0.048$ at $\kappa{=}0$ against $0.008/0.034/0.047$ at $\kappa{=}2$ on seeds $42$--$44$, hence per-seed differences $-0.106$, $-0.017$, $-0.000$ at $\mathrm{sd}\,0.057$; the Reproducibility statement's per-seed promise covers this arm too, and these are those values), so this arm's $m^{\ast} = \max(|\mathrm{lo}|,|\mathrm{hi}|)$ is $0.183$ against the comparability ladder's binding $0.034$ (§\ref{app:sensitivity}), and the body's ladder claim is scoped to Table~\ref{tab:margin_ladder} for that reason rather than stated of the paper as a whole. TOST against the frozen margin \emph{does} reject, at the $90\%$ interval $\alpha{=}0.05$ reads: $p{=}0.040$, post hoc, the closest call in Table~\ref{tab:tost}, and the smallest margin at which it still rejects is $0.137$. \textbf{So this arm replicates under the rule it was frozen against and establishes equivalence at no tighter margin}; three seeds on two rungs is what that buys, and the remedy is seeds rather than wording. \textbf{And that $m^{\ast}$ is set by the side of the margin that was never at risk}: $0.183$ is $|\mathrm{lo}|$, while the identity-rung mean ASR on this cell is $0.071$, so a fall of $0.15$ is arithmetically unavailable and the lower leg is satisfied before any seed runs (App.~\ref{par:reach}). Read on the one leg that is a test here, the binding quantity is the $95\%$ upper bound $+0.101$, which is inside the frozen margin. We keep the two-sided verdict above because it is the conservative one and it is what the freeze scores, and we state beside it that $\pm0.15$ being load-bearing for this arm is a property of the two-sided form rather than of the evidence against a rise.
\label{app:resnet18_dose}

\paragraph{The same cell at a $4\times$ longer horizon, pre-registered, which answers (ii) with a measurement.} Disclosure (ii) concedes that ResNet18 at $50$ rounds is far from converged, and the objection that follows is that a security negative read at $0.536$--$0.557$ clean accuracy may be an artifact of an undertrained model rather than a property of the composition. We therefore re-ran this exact cell at $200$ rounds: same $N{=}10$, $K{=}5$, $f{=}0.2$, the same committed model-scaling attack, Krum as $d_2$, the same endpoint rungs $\kappa \in \{0,2\}$, the same seeds $42$--$44$ and the same imported \texttt{run\_one}, with only the round count changed. The decision rule is \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak pre\_registration\_dose\_resnet18\_converged.md}, git-committed at \texttt{ca89f96} before the runner was written, and the runner refuses to start unless its \texttt{PREREG\_COMMIT} matches that hash; its harness check first reproduced a stored $50$-round per-seed row through the same imported code path to $<10^{-9}$, a value comparison against stored rows rather than a hash of a results file. Output: \texttt{results/\allowbreak dose\_resnet18\_converged/\allowbreak summary.json}.

\textbf{Both gates passed and the negative survives the longer horizon, but the margin does not bind, because the identity rung has less headroom than the margin.} Mean clean accuracy reaches $0.652$ at $\kappa{=}0$ and $0.688$ at $\kappa{=}2$, clearing the pre-registered $0.65$ convergence-label floor and reaching the $0.66$--$0.76$ band (ii) names for this architecture in this regime (the $\kappa{=}0$ rung sitting just under its lower end), and the parent arm's own validity gate passes as well. $\Delta = \mathbf{-0.022}$, mean ASR $0.045$ at $\kappa{=}0$ against $0.023$ at $\kappa{=}2$, $n{=}3$ on one seed set, so $|\Delta| < 0.15$ and the frozen three-way rule returns the verdict it returned at $50$ rounds. \textbf{We do not report this as a strengthened replication, and the freeze forbade doing so in advance}: an identity rung at $0.045$ cannot fall by more than $0.045$, so $|\Delta| < 0.15$ was attainable by arithmetic alone, and the pre-registered informativeness condition --- identity ASR at or above $0.15$, so that the margin binds --- \emph{failed}, exactly as that document predicted it would. The identity rung reads $0.045$ at $200$ rounds and $0.071$ at $50$ rounds, each stated at its own horizon; \textbf{no difference between the two is taken}, here or anywhere, because a longer horizon lengthens both the training and the adversaries' poisoning exposure, so a cross-horizon $\Delta$ would confound them.

\textbf{What the longer horizon does not buy, from the freeze rather than from hindsight.} (a) $n{=}3$ again: the paired $95\%$ interval is $[-0.182,+0.138]$ at $\mathrm{sd}\,0.064$, with per-seed differences $-0.096$, $+0.015$ and $+0.016$ (per-seed ASR $0.111/0.012/0.011$ at $\kappa{=}0$ against $0.015/0.027/0.027$ at $\kappa{=}2$, which the Reproducibility statement's per-seed promise covers), so the mean is carried by seed $42$, the one seed whose identity rung was appreciably backdoorable at all. This arm's $m^{\ast}$ is $0.182$, so like its $50$-round parent it fits inside no margin below the frozen $0.15$, and \emph{no} equivalence is claimed at any margin tighter than that one. That $m^{\ast}$ is $|\mathrm{lo}|$ and not $|\mathrm{hi}|$, so the arithmetic of the paragraph above carries over from the point rule to the interval: the lower leg is satisfied before any seed runs, and the one leg that tests anything returns a bound of $+0.138$ on a rise (App.~\ref{par:reach}). The remedy for $n{=}3$ is more seeds, which this arm deliberately does not spend. (b) It is \emph{not} a row of the comparability ladder, whose emitter reads only the frozen artifacts (§\ref{app:sensitivity}), so the ladder's binding $m^{\ast}$ and the body's scoping of which single arm needs a margin above $0.15$ are claims about that table and are unchanged by this arm. (c) Two endpoint rungs again, never described as a four-rung ladder, so no trend statistic is computed here either. (d) The six runs cost $51.4$~h of wall time, $6.6$--$14.3$~h each, the spread being contention with a concurrent arm on the same GPU rather than a property of the cell; the freeze budgeted $40$--$54$~h from two measured rates that disagreed and named the slower one as the rate it planned against.

\textbf{What the instrument does \emph{not} hold fixed, named in full, and what it pins exactly.} Mode~S pins the \emph{adversarial} coefficients; it does not pin the benign ones and was never claimed to. So the dose also moves (a) Krum's pairwise distances, hence the statistic itself; (b) \emph{which} benign client Krum selects, in $0.533/0.800/0.733$ of rounds at $\rho{=}2.72/7.39/54.6$; (c) the \textbf{magnitude and geometry of the update actually aggregated}, since the selected client enters scaled by its own coefficient; and (d) the model trajectory, which diverges across rungs in consequence. Channel (c) is not a conjecture but a \textbf{number we already measure and print}: the $\Delta$~agg.\ column of Table~\ref{tab:channels_full} is $\|\mathrm{agg}(T(U)) - \mathrm{agg}(U)\|/\|\mathrm{agg}(U)\|$, the relative displacement of the aggregate the defense \emph{emits}, and it reads $\mathbf{0.892}$ on this cell. The supplement §\ref{app:decomposition} splits that $0.892$, as vectors, into $0.986$ of \emph{re-selection} (a different benign client is chosen, and in $0/11$ of the rounds where that happens it is an adversary) and $0.973$ of \emph{rescaling} (the chosen client arrives scaled by its own coefficient); for \texttt{cos\_krum} the displacement is \emph{entirely} rescaling, since $0/15$ selections change at any rung. The claim this arm licenses is therefore sharper than a claim about the statistic alone: \textbf{a $0.892$ displacement of the emitted aggregate, together with decision changes in up to $80\%$ of rounds, leaves suppression inside the pre-registered practical-equivalence margin, provided the adversarial coefficient share does not move.} That last reading is one-sided on this cell: the identity-rung mean ASR is $0.062$, so the margin's lower leg is satisfied by arithmetic before the dose is applied, and what the interval bounds is a \emph{rise}, at $+0.026$ against the frozen $0.15$ (App.~\ref{par:reach}). \textbf{What is pinned is pinned exactly}: $c_{\mathrm{adv}}{=}1.0$ at every rung with maximum deviation $0.00\mathrm{e}{+}00$ and the share constant to $3.5\mathrm{e}{-}07$ on CIFAR-10, $3.9\mathrm{e}{-}07$ on EMNIST-byclass: float32 norm read-back noise, not slack in the instrument (\texttt{measure\_admission.py}). Naming (a)--(d) is what keeps the claim to the one channel the instrument closes by construction.

\paragraph{Closing channel (c) as well: the pre-registered score-only control.} Channel (c) (the magnitude and geometry of what is actually aggregated) is the one confound Mode~S leaves open, so we closed it too rather than argue about it. \textbf{Score-only Krum} scores on the transformed stack exactly as before and then aggregates the \emph{untransformed} selected update: the dose can change \emph{which} client contributes and nothing whatever about \emph{what} that client contributes. Statistic and decision are disturbed identically to the uncontrolled arm (the selection is computed on the same transformed stack, so $\Delta$~dec.\ is the same $0.733$) while $\Delta$~agg.\ is no longer $0.892$ but the re-selection component alone, $0.986$ relative to $\|u_{s_0}\|$ (the supplement §\ref{app:decomposition}), the rescaling component being closed by construction. This is the reviewer-suggested control in its own terms: hold the selected update's magnitude and direction fixed while perturbing the selection statistic independently.

\textbf{Frozen before the arm existed.} The decision rule is \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak pre\_registration\_score\_only.md}, git-committed at \texttt{35788d9} before any score-only ASR existed, and the runner refuses to start unless its own \texttt{PREREG\_COMMIT} matches that hash. \emph{Primary:} $\Delta = {}$mean ASR$(\kappa{=}2) - {}$mean ASR$(\kappa{=}0)$ against the suite's existing $\pm 0.15$ practical-equivalence margin, on \texttt{krum} / \texttt{committed\_scaling} / CIFAR-10 / \texttt{cifar\_cnn}, the same four rungs and seeds 42--46. $\Delta > +0.15$ would have said that \textbf{the published flat result was partly an artifact of the magnitude channel} and that the paper's central claim narrows to ``with benign magnitudes free to move'': that branch is why the arm was worth three hours of compute. The $\kappa{=}0$ rung is imported rather than re-run, because at the identity the transform returns the stack unwrapped and score-only Krum \emph{is} Krum alone; that is an exact claim, not an approximation, and it was verified bit-identically at one seed before the arm started.

\textbf{The negative survives with the magnitude channel closed.} $\Delta = \mathbf{-0.023}$ (mean ASR $0.062 \to 0.039$) against $-0.026$ for the uncontrolled arm on the identical cell: \textbf{closing the magnitude channel moves the effect by $0.003$}. The refuting threshold $0.212$ is nowhere approached, and mean clean accuracy clears the $0.35$ floor at every rung ($0.565/0.629/0.624/0.630$), so no rung is a collapsed model. \textbf{The central claim is therefore instrumented against two independent confounds} (attenuation, closed by Mode~S, and magnitude, closed here) and the sharpened statement is that a $0.986$ displacement of the emitted aggregate together with decision changes in $0.733$ of rounds leaves suppression inside the pre-registered practical-equivalence margin. That containment is one-sided here as well: the identity-rung mean ASR is $0.062$, below the margin, so its lower leg is satisfied by arithmetic and this arm's $95\%$ bound on a rise is $+0.061$ (App.~\ref{par:reach}).

\textbf{Three disclosures, from the freeze rather than from hindsight.} (i) The secondary Jonckheere--Terpstra test was specified to detect a monotone \emph{rise} (the outcome that would have refuted flatness with attenuation already closed) and the observed trend is a \emph{fall} ($J{=}58.5$, $z{=}{-}1.110$, $p_{\uparrow}{=}0.867$, permutation $p_{\uparrow}{=}0.874$). We report it and do \textbf{not} score it as support for flatness; the pre-registration states in advance that at $n{=}5$ a null from this test is not evidence of flatness. (ii) The two branches had unequal headroom. With the identity rung at $0.062$, refuting us required mean ASR$(\kappa{=}2) > 0.212$ against a ceiling of $1$ and was fully reachable, whereas the indeterminate branch ($\Delta < -0.15$) was \emph{arithmetically unreachable}: ASR cannot fall below $0$, so the largest possible fall on this cell is $0.062$. (iii) \textbf{Selectors only.} A weighted averager and a coordinate-wise order statistic emit no single selected client, so ``the selected update'' is undefined for them and the control does not cover them, which means the \texttt{coord\_median} arm carrying the $+0.098$ rise ($n{=}5$) is \textbf{not} magnitude-controlled, and neither is reputation's $+0.178$ ($n{=}5$). Score-only Krum is also an instrument and not a defense: it aggregates an update its own scoring stage never saw.

\textbf{The corollary the pre-registration staked on \texttt{cos\_krum}, and exactly what it settles.} Section~5 of the same document asserted, before the run, that score-only \texttt{cos\_krum} must be \emph{bit-identical} across rungs: its statistic is exactly invariant under positive rescaling, so the selection cannot move, and with the emitted update untransformed the whole 50-round trajectory must repeat. It does (accuracy $0.528400$ and ASR $0.003111$ at all four rungs, $|\Delta| = 0.00\mathrm{e}{+}00$ throughout) and the $\kappa{=}0$ value reproduces the published cell at that seed exactly, so the two pipelines agree at $0.000000$ rather than approximately. \textbf{What the check does not do is confirm the arm's published fall.} It runs one seed, as frozen, and that seed is unrepresentative of the arm: under the uncontrolled dose its own ladder \emph{rises} $+0.224$ where the eight-seed mean \emph{falls} $-0.425$. Closing the magnitude channel removes all $0.224$ of that seed's movement, which corroborates the \emph{channel} (for \texttt{cos\_krum} the emitted displacement is entirely rescaling, so no other route is open) but attributing the arm's mean fall to magnitude rests on that decomposition rather than on this check, and settling it empirically would need the arm's other seven seeds. Expanding a pre-registered seed count after seeing the data is precisely what pre-registration exists to prevent, so we report the invariance as confirmed and the arm-level attribution as \textbf{corroborated mechanically, not established at the arm mean}.

\subsubsection{The 2$\times$2 channel factorial, and the decomposition it refuses to license}
\label{app:factorial}
Mode~S opens two channels into a selector: the \emph{statistic} channel (the dose moves Krum's pairwise distances, so \emph{which} client is selected changes) and the \emph{magnitude} channel (the selected client enters scaled by its own coefficient). Score-only closes the second. The fourth cell closes the first instead: \textbf{emit-only Krum} scores the \emph{untransformed} stack, pinning the decision to the identity rung's, and aggregates the \emph{transformed} selected update. The four cells are then a factorial, and if the two channels acted independently the three measured effects would satisfy $\Delta_{\mathrm{full}} = \Delta_{\mathrm{SO}} + \Delta_{\mathrm{EO}}$. \textbf{They do not.} Rules were frozen in \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak pre\_registration\_emit\_only.md} at \texttt{b995f1b} before any emit-only ASR existed, with the runner refusing to start unless its own \texttt{PREREG\_COMMIT} matches that hash; the $\kappa{=}0$ rung is imported rather than re-run, because at the identity the transform returns the stack unwrapped and emit-only \emph{is} Krum alone, an exact claim verified bit-identically before the arm started. All three harness checks the freeze required pass at $0.00\mathrm{e}{+}00$ across five published arms and the score-only control, so the refactor that added this cell perturbed no published computation.

\begin{center}\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{5pt}
\begin{tabular}{llcrr}
\toprule
Cell & statistic channel & magnitude channel & mean ASR & $\Delta$ASR \\
\midrule
$\kappa{=}0$ identity (imported) & closed & closed & $0.062$ & --- \\
Score-only & \textbf{open} & closed & $0.032$ & $-0.030$ \\
Emit-only & closed & \textbf{open} & $0.107$ & $+0.046$ \\
Full Mode~S & \textbf{open} & \textbf{open} & $0.023$ & $-0.039$ \\
\midrule
\multicolumn{4}{l}{additive prediction $\Delta_{\mathrm{SO}}+\Delta_{\mathrm{EO}}$} & $+0.016$ \\
\multicolumn{4}{l}{residual against measured $\Delta_{\mathrm{full}}$ (frozen margin $0.05$)} & $\mathbf{-0.054}$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent \textbf{The table above is the published $n{=}5$ design, retained as the pre-registration requires; the verdict it produced has since been withdrawn by our own top-up.} Seeds $47$--$61$ were added under rules frozen at \texttt{684b31e}, and at $n{=}20$ $\Delta_{\mathrm{EO}} = +0.010$ ($95\%$ CI $[-0.028,+0.047]$), so the magnitude channel is \emph{established} inert rather than merely point-inside, while the residual is $-0.019$ ($[-0.061,+0.023]$), \textbf{inside} the additivity margin. \textbf{So the $n{=}5$ reading that the channels interact does not survive}, and what remains is \emph{consistent with} separability and not an establishment of it, the interval reaching $-0.061$ and so not contained in $\pm0.05$. Its half-width, $0.042$, in fact came in \emph{narrower} than the $0.057$ that top-up's own pre-registration projected, so what blocks containment here is the off-centre point estimate rather than the width the projection worried about. Read the $n{=}5$ paragraphs below as the record of what was published and why it was fragile, not as the current verdict.

\noindent \textbf{The primary rung is forced, and the additive model gets the sign wrong.} At $\kappa{=}2$ mean clean accuracy is $0.333$, below the $0.35$ floor, so the primary contrast is reported at $\kappa{=}1$ and \textbf{that substitution is forced by the floor rather than chosen after seeing ASR}: Section~5 of the pre-registration named $\kappa{=}2$ as the rung at risk, and named it for this cell specifically, because emit-only aggregates the \emph{rescaled} update and so degrades training in a way the other three cells do not. Seed-matched at $\kappa{=}1$, $\Delta_{\mathrm{EO}} = +0.046$ sits inside the frozen inert margin of $0.05$, so \textbf{the magnitude channel is inert on its own}, which is what we predicted. But $\Delta_{\mathrm{SO}} + \Delta_{\mathrm{EO}} = +0.016$ against a measured $\Delta_{\mathrm{full}} = -0.039$: an additivity residual of $-0.054$, outside the frozen $0.05$ margin, and \textbf{the additive model does not merely mis-size the joint effect, it reverses its sign}. So \textbf{no per-channel decomposition of the uncontrolled arm is licensed}, and we withdraw any reading of the re-selection/rescaling split as a decomposition of suppression. That split remains sound as the thing it says it is: the supplement's algebraic decomposition (§\ref{app:decomposition}) decomposes a \emph{displacement}, and says in its own terms that it makes no claim about suppression.

\noindent \textbf{Three reasons this verdict is fragile, all of them ours to report.} (i) It is knife-edge on both margins at once, $|\Delta_{\mathrm{EO}}|$ falling \emph{inside} the inert margin by $0.004$ and the residual \emph{outside} the additivity margin by $0.004$. (ii) Both pre-registered secondaries are null: the emit-only per-seed sign count is $3$ up against $2$ down, and Jonckheere--Terpstra across the four rungs gives $z = -0.437$, $p_{\uparrow} = 0.669$. (iii) One seed carries the result. Seed~42's emit-only run collapses to clean accuracy $0.2613$, below our floor \emph{at that seed} though the rung mean of $0.487$ clears it, and its $\Delta$ is $+0.272$ against $-0.007$, $+0.007$, $+0.013$, $-0.058$; excluding it would give $\Delta_{\mathrm{EO}} = -0.011$ and residual $-0.003$, reading as separable. \textbf{We do not exclude it}: Section~5 fixes $n{=}5$ on seeds $42$--$46$ and applies the floor to a rung's \emph{mean} rather than per seed, so no per-seed exclusion is licensed, and the pre-registered top-up settled it without one. \textbf{At $n{=}20$ the knife edge is gone and the verdict it produced is withdrawn}: $\Delta_{\mathrm{EO}} = +0.010$, $95\%$ CI $[-0.028,+0.047]$, so the magnitude channel is \emph{established} inert, and the residual is $-0.019$, $[-0.061,+0.023]$, inside the additivity margin, so the interaction reading does not survive. Seed~42 remains the only draw of $20$ past either outlier threshold and is still excluded from nothing; realized sd falls $0.130 \to 0.080$. Separability is now \emph{consistent with} and \textbf{still not established}, its interval reaching $-0.061$ and so not contained in $\pm0.05$ even though its half-width, $0.042$, came in \emph{narrower} than the $0.057$ that top-up's pre-registration projected at this variance, and dropping the one seed that produces the unwelcome answer is precisely what pre-registration exists to prevent. Section~6.1 of the same document declared, before the run, that at $n{=}5$ only a large $|\Delta_{\mathrm{EO}}|$ would be decisive, and $|\Delta_{\mathrm{EO}}|$ is not large. \textbf{We therefore report this arm as not decisive, and act on it only in the direction that withdraws a claim}: low power is a reason to stop asserting a decomposition, never a reason to assert one. Every number above is emitted by \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak analyze\_emit\_only\_factorial.py}, which reads only frozen artifacts and asserts all four primary quantities reproduce from the per-seed cells to $<10^{-9}$ before printing. \textbf{Selectors only}: the factorial is defined for \texttt{krum} and \texttt{cos\_krum}, the harness raising for any other $d_2$, so it is one selector family, one attack, one dataset, one architecture.

\textbf{What this settles, and what it does not.} The intervention delivers the controlled isolation of C2 the two cross-defense suites could not: within a single defense, with attenuation excluded by construction \emph{and}, in the score-only arm, the magnitude of the aggregated update held exactly fixed as well, \textbf{substantial disturbance of the statistic $d_2$ reads need not produce a corresponding change in suppression once the adversarial coefficient share is held fixed} (on the adjudicating Krum arm under Mode~S and, by pre-registered replication, on a downstream defense in a different invariance class, on a second dataset and a differently configured CNN, and on a second architecture family with architecture varying alone) while \emph{admission} of adversarial input is the channel the evidence points to. The statistic-level reading the framework was first written in is refuted; the admission-level reading is confirmed as a \emph{direction} in these interventions rather than established in general. It does \emph{not} rescue the framework's positive direction wholesale: the pooled two-channel model over all $26$ cells is \textbf{not} confirmed ($R^2{=}0.239$), and the replication refutes its cross-arm ordering outright, so admission is a better single predictor than the statistic without being a quantitative one. It addresses neither recall nor discovery (FG$\to$RFA remains a false negative), and nothing in §\ref{sec:dose_outcome} is rescored: its pooled refutation, its two refuted arms and its indeterminate arm stand exactly as reported.
\paragraph{Mode~M: coordinate masking, a second transformation class.} Every other arm in this paper transforms client updates by a positive per-client scalar, the family Prop.~\ref{prop:invariance} classifies and Thm.~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} bounds. \textbf{Mode~M} is outside it. Benign client $i$ has a random $m$ fraction of the coordinates of each parameter tensor zeroed, drawn from an independent Bernoulli$(1{-}m)$ seeded by \texttt{default\_rng([seed, round, slot])} so it is reproducible and uncorrelated with adversary status, and the surviving coordinates are then \textbf{renormalized to the update's original $L_2$ norm}. Renormalization is load-bearing and was required in the pre-registration \emph{before any ASR existed}: an unnormalized mask shrinks benign norms and re-opens the magnitude channel that §\ref{app:score_only}'s control exists to close. The unnormalized variant was considered and rejected for that reason, and the rejection is recorded rather than omitted. \textbf{Adversarial updates are returned unmodified, exactly}: the maximum deviation of every adversarial coefficient from $1$ is $0.00$ with object identity preserved, a strictly stronger pinning than Mode~S's $3.5{\times}10^{-7}$ float32 read-back. Rungs are $m \in \{0, 0.2, 0.5, 0.8\}$ and $m{=}0$ returns the update list unwrapped, so the identity rung is bit-identical to Krum standalone by construction; we check rather than assert this, the identity rung agreeing to $10^{-9}$ across all three directories that carry those seeds.

\textbf{The channels were measured before any ASR existed}, as the EMNIST-byclass arm's were. Krum's decision changes in $0.000/0.133/0.400/0.467$ of rounds across the ladder while the adversarial mass it admits is $0.000$ at every rung, at an aggregate displacement of $1.095$ against Mode~S's $0.892$ at a \emph{larger} decision change. So the arm was established prospectively as a generalization test, and the pre-registration (\texttt{684b31e}) then split four ways, including two that would have refuted us: a monotone rise in $m$ would confirm the statistic reading, and masking that made Krum admit the adversary would void the equivalence claim at that rung and confirm the \emph{admission} reading. \textbf{Neither fired.} At $n{=}10$ (seeds $42$--$51$) the paired contrast $m{=}0 \to m{=}0.8$ is $\Delta=-0.016$, $95\%$ CI $[-0.051,+0.019]$, contained in the frozen $\pm0.15$ margin at a half-width $23.1\%$ of it (TOST $p{=}5.3{\times}10^{-6}$), every rung mean clearing the $0.5$ ceiling and the $0.35$ accuracy floor, and Jonckheere--Terpstra finding no increasing trend ($p_{\uparrow}{=}0.895$). The mask analogue of the score-only control gives $-0.018$, $[-0.047,+0.010]$, agreeing with the full ladder to $0.002$: since adversarial updates are unmasked in \emph{both} conditions the two ladders differ only when a benign client is selected, so their agreement localizes the absent effect to \emph{which client Krum picks} rather than to the content it emits. Containment is one-sided on this arm too: the identity-rung mean ASR is $0.053$, so the margin's lower leg is satisfied by arithmetic and the leg that tests anything bounds a rise at $+0.019$ (App.~\ref{par:reach}).

\textbf{What this arm does and does not license.} Only the flat-with-admission-unchanged outcome generalizes the negative, and that is the outcome observed; had admission moved, the arm would have been reported as a confirmation of the admission reading instead. \textbf{One mask is one transformation, not the class of non-rescalings}: rotations, projections, quantization and sign compression are untested, so the supportable claim is that the negative replicates on a second, structurally different transformation class, never that it holds for arbitrary upstream processing. Mode~M has nothing to impose in $24/120$ measured rung-rounds, identical to Mode~S's figure, and like Mode~S it is an \emph{instrument} and not a defense, since it reads adversary identity. The \texttt{cos\_krum} mask ladder was considered and dropped for compute before any ASR existed, which is why the masking column carries one outcome and three channel-only entries.

\subsection{Seven cells, and a pre-registered mechanism that did not survive them}
\label{app:sixcell}

\noindent \emph{Is the sign reversal a property of the design, or of this aggregator and attack?}
Table~\ref{tab:comparability} contrasts the two designs on the one arm that motivated the instrument. That contrast is one cell, and a single cell cannot say whether the sign reversal is a property of the evaluation design or of \texttt{coord\_median} under a pixel backdoor. We therefore ran the contrast on seven cells: four that already existed in frozen artifacts and are \textbf{re-scored, never re-run}, and three that did not exist and were run for this purpose ($60$ runs for the first two of them, plus $8$ under Amendment~2 and $40$ for the third under Amendment~4 below, which is the run budget that amendment froze).

\textbf{Both designs are the same computation.} The two ladders differ in exactly two tokens, the family name and whether \texttt{generic\_compose} receives an \texttt{adv\_mask}; data partition, participant draw, attack, local training and seeds are identical. \texttt{run\_comparability\_cells.py} refuses to start unless it first reproduces a published \texttt{results/dose\_response/} cell bit-identically ($|\text{diff}| = 0.000\mathrm{e}{+}00$), so the new cells are the same experiment as the four they are compared against, and \texttt{analyze\_comparability.py} refuses to print any pooled number unless all four published contrasts reproduce.

\textbf{What was frozen, and when.} Before any new run, we pre-registered a mechanism (\texttt{experiments/pre\_\allowbreak registration\_comparability.md}, frozen at \texttt{c986ef4}; two amendments at \texttt{1026a96} and \texttt{f16083b}; the three hashes are kept because every other pre-registration in this paper carries its own, and dropping them for brevity would make this the one prereg citation a reader cannot check): the two designs disagree exactly where the downstream defense admits adversarial mass, gated on the admission-level change $\Delta\Lambda_a$ at the top rung. The four existing cells separate perfectly under that rule, which is precisely why \textbf{they are training data and cannot also be evidence for it}. The rule mechanically generated a prediction for each new cell, committed in advance: DISAGREE for \texttt{coord\_median}/scaling ($\Delta\Lambda_a{=}0.0086$, disclosed at the time as the weakest margin of any cell), AGREE for \texttt{krum}/EMNIST-byclass ($\Delta\Lambda_a{=}0.0000$). The document names in advance what would refute it and commits to reporting refutation rather than fitting a threshold.

\begin{table}[t]
\centering

The four unmarked cells are the training data the rule was read off. \textbf{The rule predicted AGREE for the EMNIST cell and the designs disagree, so it is refuted}; per the frozen clause it is withdrawn, the four-cell pattern is demoted to a description, and the table is reported in full. \textbf{The CIFAR-100 cell carries no prediction and its $\Delta\Lambda_a$ entry is absent rather than zero}, because the rule that would have generated one was already dead when that cell was pre-registered (Amendment~4); it is scored on the two endpoint rungs the primary contrast reads, and because no trend secondary was pre-registered for this table, the trend across all four rungs is reported as a \emph{post hoc} description below rather than as a test here. Every cell emitted by \texttt{analyze\_comparability.py}, \textbf{except row~5's two $n{=}20$ readings, which \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak run\_coordmedian\_\allowbreak scaling\_\allowbreak topup.py} emits from its own artifact}: that top-up leaves \texttt{results/\allowbreak comparability\_\allowbreak six\_\allowbreak cells.json} byte-untouched, so the frozen artifact still reads row~5 at $n{=}5$ and the two emitters are named separately rather than one standing for both.}
