\textbf{The out-of-sample result: one confirms, one refutes, one was never predicted} (Table~\ref{tab:sixcell}). \texttt{coord\_median} under \emph{model scaling} disagrees, as predicted, on the weakest margin in the set: at $n{=}20$ on both legs the outcome-gated ladder reads $+0.062$ $[+0.023,+0.102]$ where the instrument reads $+0.210$ $[+0.173,+0.247]$. This is the cell that matters for the headline, because it is the paper's sign-reversal aggregator under the \emph{other} committed attack, and it says \textbf{the disagreement is not specific to the pixel backdoor}. It is a disagreement, not a second sign reversal, and we do not call it one. \textbf{The top-up that brought this row to $n{=}20$ changed the disagreement's character without changing its label}, and the disclosures below report that change together with the one outcome our own freeze failed to enumerate. The EMNIST-byclass cell then \textbf{refutes the rule}: $\Delta\Lambda_a$ is exactly $0.0000$, the rule predicts the designs coincide, and they do not ($+0.157$ against $-0.013$, opposite signs). \textbf{Those opposite signs are not a second sign reversal, and we do not count them as one:} both intervals contain zero ($[-0.175,+0.489]$ and $[-0.034,+0.009]$), so neither estimate is separated from no effect and what this cell establishes is a failed prediction of the rule, not a reversal of the measured one. Whether the \emph{reversal} travels is a different question from whether the \emph{rule} does, and the third out-of-sample cell is the one that addresses it.

\textbf{The third out-of-sample cell moves the sign reversal to a third dataset, and it replicates} (Amendment~4, committed at \texttt{9b8a395} before the runner was pointed at CIFAR-100). The objection this cell answers is one we sharpen rather than defend: before this row the table already spanned two attacks, two datasets and two CNN configurations, but \textbf{the sign reversal itself did not}. It existed on CIFAR-10 with \texttt{cifar\_cnn} and nowhere else, because the cell that added a second dataset was \texttt{krum}/scaling, a \emph{different} cell, and that cell is explicitly not counted as a reversal. Cell~7 therefore holds the aggregator, the attack, the rung grid and the seed set fixed and changes only the dataset, to CIFAR-100 under the same architecture: a $100$-way task, which moves the backdoor's base rate and the clean-accuracy regime together. The outcome-gated ladder falls $-0.213$ $[-0.284,-0.141]$ where the instrument rises $+0.071$ $[+0.031,+0.110]$, opposite signs with both intervals excluding zero, so under the same frozen definition applied to every other row this is a \textbf{second sign reversal}. \textbf{It is a replication of the phenomenon, not an enlargement of the claim}: three datasets and two CNN configurations is the scope after it, the reversal is \emph{dataset-conditional} on exactly that evidence, and nothing here licenses a statement about a dataset or an architecture outside the three measured.

\textbf{Five disclosures about that cell, every one of them from the amendment rather than from hindsight.} (i) \textbf{The admissibility gate was two-sided and frozen before the run}: the identity rung had to reach mean clean accuracy $\ge 0.35$ \emph{and} mean ASR inside $[0.15,0.85]$, the upper bound because a saturated rung kills the \emph{rise} leg exactly as EMNIST's $0.027$ identity rung killed the \emph{fall} leg in the cell above. It passed on both sides ($0.393$ accuracy, $0.703$ ASR, means over seeds $42$--$46$), and the amendment named in advance the finding it would have reported instead: \emph{no third dataset within this compute budget admits this cell}. (ii) \textbf{No prediction was made, and the $\Delta\Lambda_a$ entry is absent rather than $0.0000$}. The rule was refuted by the EMNIST cell and withdrawn before this cell existed, so it generates nothing here, and \textbf{a dead mechanism is not revived by a cell that happens to agree with what it would have said}. (iii) \textbf{The cell is scored on the two endpoint rungs}, which is exactly what the frozen primary contrast reads, so the row is complete as printed. All four rungs of the frozen grid \emph{are} run, on both designs, at seeds $42$--$46$; \textbf{an earlier version of this disclosure said the interior rungs were not run and that the cell therefore carried no trend statistic, which was true when written and became false when the runner finished}, and we correct it here rather than silently. What the completed interior buys is a description and not a test: \textbf{the comparability pre-registration registers no trend secondary}, so the Jonckheere--Terpstra statistics that follow are \emph{post hoc}, they are emitted with \texttt{pre\_registered:~false}, and no verdict in Table~\ref{tab:sixcell} is a function of them. Both ladders start from the same identity rung and then move apart across every rung, not only at the top: the outcome-gated design falls monotonically ($0.7034$, $0.6804$, $0.6508$, $0.4905$; $J{=}32.0$, $z{=}{-}2.89$, $p_{\downarrow}{=}0.0019$) while the instrument rises monotonically ($0.7034$, $0.7035$, $0.7220$, $0.7740$; $J{=}96.0$, $z{=}{+}1.41$), so the reversal is a divergence of the whole ladder rather than an artifact of its endpoints. \textbf{The rising leg does not clear $0.05$ as a rank trend} ($p_{\uparrow}{=}0.079$, permutation $p_{\uparrow}{=}0.084$) even though its endpoint interval $[+0.031,+0.110]$ excludes zero, and \textbf{we report that weaker reading beside the stronger one} rather than the half that helps: at $n{=}5$ per rung a rise of this size is not separable from chance by ranks alone. Both $p_{\downarrow}$ values here are normal-approximation figures, following the supplement's §\ref{app:dose_detail}, because the permutation leg is computed for the increasing direction only. The CIFAR-10 cell of the same aggregator and attack has the same shape on both legs ($z{=}{-}2.96$, $p_{\downarrow}{=}1.5{\times}10^{-3}$ falling against $z{=}{+}1.48$, $p_{\uparrow}{=}0.069$ rising), under the same caveat on the rising one; its falling leg is the arm-4 trend already reported there, and it agrees because \texttt{analyze\_comparability.py} \emph{imports} that suite's trend test rather than reimplementing one. \textbf{Those four figures are at $n{=}5$, and stay there, even though that cell's endpoint row is now $n{=}20$}: the top-up ran the two \emph{endpoint} rungs only, so its four rungs hold $[20,5,5,20]$ seeds, and Jonckheere--Terpstra's null distribution is a function of those group sizes. A mixed-$n$ trend is therefore not the statistic this grid was described with. The scorer computes it anyway and prints it beside the quoted one so that its exclusion is visible rather than assumed ($z{=}{-}5.54$ falling, $z{=}{+}3.07$ rising); \textbf{we do not quote it}, and no verdict reads it. The quoted values are the trend over the five seeds all four rungs share, which is the pre-top-up block, so they are the same numbers the earlier draft printed and not a re-test on new data. (iv) \textbf{CIFAR-100 was chosen on wall time and we record that as the limitation it is}: at $660$~s per run it was the third dataset that fit an overnight budget, where EMNIST-byclass ($29.5$~h per cell, and an identity rung that fails the gate a priori) and ResNet18 ($65$~h) did not. \textbf{Those are \emph{comparability-cell} costs, two designs $\times$ four rungs $\times$ five seeds, and they do not price the single-design endpoint arm of §\ref{app:resnet18_dose}}, which is six runs and did fit one night; the figures are not in conflict because they cost different objects. This is the third dataset that fit, not the third that was most informative. (v) \textbf{The two designs coincide run for run at the identity rung of this cell}, bit-identically, as they also do for \texttt{coord\_median}/scaling and do \emph{not} for the EMNIST cell, so it is a property of the cell and not of the design. Both $\Delta$ estimates therefore share their $\kappa{=}0$ leg and the difference \emph{between} the two designs is carried entirely by the $\kappa{=}2$ rung, which is where the attenuation channel is open. We say so because two identical columns in an artifact look like duplicated data and are not.

\textbf{Row~5's top-up, and the branch of our own freeze that fired.} \texttt{coord\_median}/scaling was the last row still at the frozen $n{=}5$ on both legs, and it is the cell where both margin legs are genuine tests rather than one being satisfied by arithmetic, so it is where an interval is worth narrowing. \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak pre\_registration\_\allowbreak coordmedian\_\allowbreak scaling\_\allowbreak n20.md} was committed alone at \texttt{74044f1} with the output path verified not to exist, so \texttt{git log} orders the freeze before the data rather than our word doing it; $45$ runs followed at seeds $47$--$61$, the two endpoint rungs only, into \texttt{results/\allowbreak coordmedian\_\allowbreak scaling\_\allowbreak topup/\allowbreak summary.json}. It is $45$ and not $60$ because at $\kappa{=}0$ the two designs are the same computation, and \textbf{that is established on \emph{this} attack rather than inherited from the pixel cell}: four harness runs at seed $42$ reproduced both published $\kappa{=}0$ rows and both published $\kappa{=}2$ rows at $|\Delta| = 0.000\mathrm{e}{+}00$ in ASR \emph{and} clean accuracy, and their verdict dict is persisted into the artifact instead of left in a terminal. The freeze named two refuting branches. \textbf{The one that fired is the second}: the outcome-gated interval, which contained zero at $n{=}5$, excludes it at $n{=}20$. The first did not fire. Had the paired difference between the designs covered zero, this cell would have gone DISAGREE to AGREE and the paper's count of five disagreeing cells would have fallen to four, in the abstract and in Figure~\ref{fig:modeS}. \textbf{So the label survives and the reason for it does not}: at $n{=}5$ one design detected nothing where the other detected an effect, and at $n{=}20$ both detect one and the disagreement is a disagreement about \emph{magnitude}, $+0.062$ against $+0.210$ with disjoint intervals.

\textbf{One outcome fell outside both branches we named, and we report it as a defect in the freeze rather than assign it to the nearer branch.} The paired difference between the designs is $+0.147$ $[+0.103,+0.191]$ at $n{=}20$, against $+0.194$ $[+0.108,+0.281]$ at $n{=}5$. Its \emph{sign} verdict is the one the freeze anticipated, an interval excluding zero, but its \emph{margin} verdict moved from outside the frozen $0.15$ to inside it, and our branches were written over the sign alone. \textbf{The two designs are therefore separated from each other and separated by less than the margin this paper calls practically meaningful, at the same time}, and neither half may be reported as the other. The same tension sits on the outcome-gated leg, which is inside the margin at $|{+}0.062|$ while excluding zero: \textbf{that arm stays inside the frozen margin and can no longer be phrased as no effect}, which is a stronger constraint on our own language than the $n{=}5$ row imposed. Neither half is an \emph{equivalence reading} in the sense §\ref{app:sensitivity} reserves that term for, which is a statement about the \emph{controlled} estimate, and this arm's controlled interval lies outside the margin. \textbf{The property the cell was chosen for survived its own top-up}: the identity rung's mean ASR is $0.557$ at $n{=}20$, above the frozen $0.15$, so the largest available fall still exceeds the margin and both legs remain tests. That figure is an $n{=}20$ level and is \emph{not} the $0.519$ this paper prints for \texttt{pure\_coord\_median} and for CoordMedian's standalone scaling baseline, which are $n{=}5$ readings of the same quantity on seeds $42$--$46$ and which this top-up neither supersedes nor enters. \textbf{Endpoint-only has a declared price}: the four rungs of this ladder now hold $[20,5,5,20]$ seeds, so no trend statistic is computed on the new seeds and none is quoted, exactly as the pixel cell's is not. The $0.35$ accuracy floor was live and no rung is void (lowest rung mean $0.706$, lowest single seed $0.633$). $\Delta\Lambda_a{=}0.0086$ is imported from the frozen channel measurement and was not re-measured here, this arm emits no admission or channel quantity, and its controlled leg is masked, so \textbf{it bears on the oracle objection of §\ref{sec:limitations} not at all}.

% Round 77: this paragraph discharges the Reproducibility statement's per-seed promise, which is a
% UNIVERSAL quantifier over arms (:1038 and :1068) and went false the moment row 5's top-up existed.
% Warning for any later absence or collision audit: the 60 three-decimal literals below are per-seed
% ASR values and they WILL collide with unrelated quantities elsewhere in the paper (seed 56's
% outcome-gated 0.643 against EMNIST Krum's decision fraction at :763, :1826 and :1837, for one), so a
% grep for a bare 3-dp number now has this paragraph as a false hit. Match the (mean, sd, n) triple.
% Blank lines are kept on BOTH sides of this block on purpose: a comment line eats its own newline, so
% a comment sitting flush against prose welds two paragraphs, and the joined gate is body-window-only.

\textbf{Per-seed values for this arm, which the Reproducibility statement promises for every arm.} Identity ASR, then the outcome-gated $\kappa{=}2$ and then the Mode~S $\kappa{=}2$, on the $20$ paired seeds; the first five are imported from \texttt{results/\allowbreak comparability\_\allowbreak cells/\allowbreak summary.json} and the other fifteen were computed here, and each row of the artifact's \texttt{per\_seed} block records which of the two it is. seed~$42$, $0.549 \to 0.486$ and $0.722$; $43$, $0.341 \to 0.432$ and $0.534$; $44$, $0.460 \to 0.491$ and $0.772$; $45$, $0.768 \to 0.711$ and $0.908$; $46$, $0.476 \to 0.483$ and $0.637$; $47$, $0.445 \to 0.666$ and $0.617$; $48$, $0.773 \to 0.846$ and $0.866$; $49$, $0.541 \to 0.693$ and $0.819$; $50$, $0.530 \to 0.688$ and $0.788$; $51$, $0.634 \to 0.710$ and $0.835$; $52$, $0.354 \to 0.515$ and $0.733$; $53$, $0.785 \to 0.750$ and $0.825$; $54$, $0.615 \to 0.600$ and $0.905$; $55$, $0.397 \to 0.550$ and $0.620$; $56$, $0.522 \to 0.643$ and $0.710$; $57$, $0.493 \to 0.620$ and $0.678$; $58$, $0.800 \to 0.752$ and $0.938$; $59$, $0.483 \to 0.539$ and $0.745$; $60$, $0.474 \to 0.478$ and $0.759$; $61$, $0.709 \to 0.743$ and $0.930$. \textbf{Per seed the two designs separate more cleanly than their means alone show}: the controlled difference is positive on all $20$ seeds, its smallest being $+0.040$ on seed~$53$, while the outcome-gated difference is negative on $5$ of the $20$, and the paired difference between the designs is positive on $19$ with seed~$47$ the one exception at $-0.049$. Those three counts are \emph{descriptive and post hoc}: the freeze scored means and paired intervals, so no sign test and no per-seed count statistic is computed on this arm and none is quoted as a verdict.

\textbf{We report the refutation as a refutation.} $\Delta\Lambda_a$ does not explain when the two designs disagree. The four-cell separation that suggested it stands as a description of four cells and nothing more, and no threshold was fitted after the fact to rescue it. \textbf{The observation survives the mechanism's death}: across seven cells the two designs disagree on five, on two different attacks, three datasets and two CNN configurations, and two of those disagreements are sign reversals. What we do not have is an account of \emph{when} to expect it, which is the honest state of this result and a direct limit on how far the protocol of §\ref{sec:protocol} can be turned into a rule of thumb.

\textbf{The short form first}, because it is all the argument needs: we pre-registered a mechanism before the runs that tested it, one out-of-sample cell refuted it, and it is therefore withdrawn. \textbf{One process failure is disclosed below that, because it nearly reversed this section} and because a reader entitled to the first sentence is entitled to how nearly it went the other way. The EMNIST cell was first scored on non-identical seed sets ($n{=}5$ against a frozen $n{=}3$), the disagreement carried entirely by two seeds with no counterpart in the other design. We amended the pre-registration to complete the controlled ladder to the frozen seed set rather than resolve the cell on the three shared seeds, which give AGREE: choosing them would have chosen the answer. The completed comparison is the fully paired $n{=}5$ in the table and it still disagrees. That same amendment asserted that cross-design pairing was the convention the freeze had used. \textbf{It was not}, and what caught the error was not our judgement but the analyzer's own gate: under cross-design pairing the four \emph{published} cells stop reproducing (\texttt{krum}/scaling's controlled ladder moves $-0.010$ at $n{=}20$ to $-0.026$ at $n{=}5$), and the script refused to print. A third amendment withdraws the claim, and the full sequence is left in the pre-registration.


\subsection{Two further regimes: an adversary that adapts to the downstream defense, and $N{=}100$ at $20\%$ participation}
\label{app:regime}

\noindent \emph{Is the design disagreement an artifact of a non-adaptive attacker, or of ten clients at full participation?} Neither question is answered anywhere else on disk, and each is answered by rerunning \emph{both} ladders on the cell the instrument was built for, \texttt{coord\_median} under the committed pixel backdoor. The adaptive machinery this paper already has (§\ref{sec:fg_rfa_flagship}) was pointed at FoolsGold$\to$RFA and scored whether \emph{the screen's} verdict survives, so no artifact held a criterion-aware adversary and \texttt{coord\_median} together; and the existing $N{=}100$ material stores game equilibria, which is why §\ref{app:scale} labels it residual. \textbf{The pre-registration was frozen before its output directory existed} (\texttt{experiments/pre\_\allowbreak registration\_\allowbreak regime\_\allowbreak dissociation.md}, at \texttt{b317af0}, Amendment~1 at \texttt{f4c3fec}), and \texttt{run\_regime\_dissociation.py} refuses to start unless \texttt{PREREG\_COMMIT} matches the amended hash. \textbf{Each regime changes one thing}: A changes the adversary, B changes $N$ and $K$. The cell, the attack, the $\kappa$ grid, $f{=}0.2$, $\alpha{=}0.5$, the round count and \texttt{cifar\_cnn} are held at the frozen values in both.

\textbf{The pre-registered estimand is the disagreement itself and nothing else.} $D = \Delta(\text{confounded}) - \Delta(\text{instrument})$, paired by seed, at a two-sided $95\%$ $t$-interval. Its $\kappa{=}0$ leg cancels exactly, because at $\kappa{=}0$ both ladders return the update list unwrapped and both rungs are \texttt{coord\_median} alone, so $D_s = \mathrm{ASR}_{\mathrm{conf}}(\kappa{=}2,s) - \mathrm{ASR}_{\mathrm{inst}}(\kappa{=}2,s)$. \textbf{Only $D$ is powered at $n{=}3$, and the freeze says so with arithmetic rather than with a hope}: at the $n{=}3$ half-width $4.303\,\mathrm{sd}/\sqrt{3} = 2.484\,\mathrm{sd}$, the frozen variances give $0.299$ for $\Delta$(confounded) against a mean of $-0.272$ and $0.161$ for $\Delta$(instrument) against $+0.098$, so a rule requiring both intervals to exclude zero is one this arm was arithmetically guaranteed to fail; $D$'s frozen mean is $-0.408$ at sd $0.109$, hence a half-width of $0.272$. Pre-registering the two $\Delta$s would have been pre-registering a null. Each $\Delta$ is therefore \textbf{reported descriptively, with its half-width beside it and no significance attached}, and \textbf{no equivalence claim is made anywhere in this arm}: the phrase \emph{no evidence of a practically meaningful change} is defined in the supplement against the paired $95\%$ interval lying inside the margin, $n{=}3$ cannot earn it, and \texttt{EQUIV\_MARGIN} is deliberately not imported by this runner. Table~\ref{tab:regime} reports both regimes seed by seed.

\begin{table}[h]
\centering\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{5pt}

Under an adversary that solves for \texttt{coord\_median} the instrument still moves \emph{up} while the outcome-gated ladder moves down, on all three seeds. At $N{=}100$ with $K{=}20$ both designs move down and only the magnitude disagrees, so \textbf{regime B reproduces the disagreement and not the reversal}, which scopes the reversal to the participation regime rather than refuting it. The frozen row is Table~\ref{tab:comparability}'s published cell, at the $n$ each of its quantities was frozen at, and it is a reference point rather than a fourth arm. \textbf{Three gates, all of them run before the arm was scored, and two of them returned exact zeros rather than small ones.} (1)~\emph{The adaptive adversary must beat the plain backdoor it replaces}, or regime A tests nothing: a grid of $\varepsilon \in \{1,2,4\}$ crossed with \texttt{decorrelate} $\in \{\text{True},\text{False}\}$ at $\kappa{=}0$, seed $42$ only, argmax mean ASR, then frozen for every rung and seed. (2)~\emph{Every rung must clear the $0.35$ clean-accuracy floor}, which both regimes do ($0.769/0.691/0.690$ and $0.474/0.426/0.441$). (3)~\emph{The shared $\kappa{=}0$ rung must be proved shared, not assumed}: the instrument's own $\kappa{=}0$ rung is run at one seed in each regime and differenced against the confounded one, giving $(0.000000, 0.000000)$ in accuracy and ASR at a tolerance of $10^{-6}$ in \emph{both} regimes, the adaptive one included. \textbf{The two frozen runners were also proved unchanged by value before either regime ran}: the three optional keyword arguments this arm adds to \texttt{run\_\allowbreak dose\_\allowbreak response.py} and \texttt{run\_\allowbreak targeted\_\allowbreak dose.py} are exercised at their defaults against stored cells of \texttt{results/\allowbreak dose\_response/} and \texttt{results/\allowbreak reversal\_seed\_topup/}, worst absolute deviation $0.00\mathrm{e}{+}00$ over three runs plus a cross-ladder identity comparison. \textbf{An md5 of an artifact would not have been that proof}, which is why the check is a value comparison against per-seed rows.

\textbf{The calibration grid is the reason regime A exists, and its outcome vindicates a decision taken before the data.} \texttt{decorrelate} confines each adversary to a disjoint coordinate block, which is an anti-FoolsGold device; the freeze argued that against a coordinate-wise median a lone adversary inside its own block is plausibly \emph{weaker}, and refused to import the existing suite's argmax on that ground. The grid says so unambiguously: with \texttt{decorrelate} on, ASR is $0.150$, $0.192$ and $0.199$ across the three radii, \textbf{every one of them below the plain backdoor's $0.395$}, while with it off they are $0.398$, $0.430$ and $0.406$. Importing $\varepsilon{=}1$ with decorrelation because it won against FoolsGold$\to$RFA would have installed an adversary weaker than no adaptation at all, failed gate~(1), and licensed no sentence about adaptivity. \textbf{A hyperparameter's argmax does not travel across downstream defenses}, and the general lesson is the one we would want applied to us: an adaptive-attack baseline tuned against one defense is not an adaptive-attack baseline against another.

\textbf{Six disclosures, five of them limits.} (i) \textbf{The winning adversary beats the plain backdoor by $+0.035$ at one seed} ($0.4299$ against $0.3946$), which is a margin, not a rout. What this arm establishes is that the disagreement \emph{survives} an adversary adapting to $d_2$; it does \textbf{not} establish that a strong adaptive attack against \texttt{coord\_median} was found, and no sentence anywhere in the paper says so. (ii) \textbf{The adversary does not anticipate $d_1$}, and that is a property of the object rather than a gap in the attack: Mode~S reads adversary identity in order to pin the adversarial coefficient, so it is an instrument and not a deployable defense, and no real attacker can adapt to a stage that does not exist in deployment. The arm therefore bounds adaptation to the pipeline a deployed attacker can see. (iii) \textbf{Regime B's sign reversal does not reproduce, and we report that as the finding it is.} Both designs read the dose as protective at $N{=}100$, the instrument's $\Delta$ landing at $-0.199$ and negative on all three seeds ($-0.183/-0.212/-0.201$), so what travels to $20\%$ participation is the disagreement and not its sign. That interval happens to exclude zero, and \textbf{it is still not reported as inferential}, because the freeze forbids it at $n{=}3$ and a bound may not become a claim because it came out convenient. (iv) \textbf{Regime B confounds $N$ with $K$}, deliberately, because the practitioner-facing regime is many clients with partial participation and separating the two would have been a third arm. Nothing here attributes the attenuation of the reversal to either factor alone. (v) \textbf{Two rungs, three seeds, one cell, one attack, one dataset, one architecture.} Neither regime is a four-rung ladder, no trend statistic is computed here and none may be quoted from it, and the arms add no dataset, attack, $f$ or round count. (vi) \textbf{The base rung does not suppress the attack in either regime} ($0.387$ and $0.514$), which the frozen data already showed, so no sentence from this arm may say that \texttt{coord\_median} alone suppresses this backdoor.

\textbf{One unplanned corroboration, reported because it is stronger than what the freeze expected.} The pre-registration cautioned that its $N{=}100$ rung and the residual $N{=}100$ material ``are not bit-comparable'', and on that basis regime B ran its own $\kappa{=}0$ rung and never substituted the older one. It ran anyway, and the two agree exactly: freshly measured ASR $0.4261/0.5532/0.5631$ at accuracies $0.4773/0.4855/0.4598$ against the identical five figures per seed in \texttt{results/\allowbreak cifar10\_100clients\_rich/\allowbreak seed\_4\{2,3,4\}/\allowbreak payoff\_results.json}. \textbf{So the caution was false and it was false in the safe direction}: it caused an extra measurement rather than a substituted one, the two runners are protocol-identical on this cell, and by the standard we apply to ourselves elsewhere, a computation reproduced across two runners establishes protocol identity and not corroboration of the effect. We leave the committed pre-registration as written rather than amend it a second time after seeing results, and record the correction here.

\textbf{Amendment~1, and why it was needed before any run.} The freeze required the runner to assert that the adversarial tensor \emph{changed} at the first round, as a guard against a hook that is silently never called. That assertion is false by design for the committed pixel backdoor, whose payload lives in the poisoned dataset and whose \texttt{manipulate\_update} is the inherited identity, so the runner would have aborted on round zero of every non-adaptive leg. Amendment~1 replaces it with an \textbf{attack-conditional} assertion: the update must change exactly when the attack overrides \texttt{manipulate\_update} or the criterion-aware layer is active, and must be \emph{unchanged} otherwise. \textbf{This is strictly stronger than what it replaced}, because the defect the original clause existed to catch, a hook that never fires, produces ``nothing changed'', which is also this attack's correct behaviour, and that exact collision once left two composition arms in this repository computing the same thing. All four failure modes were deliberately induced and each was caught. The amendment was committed \textbf{before} \texttt{results/regime\_dissociation/} existed and before any run of this arm, and the pre-amendment text remains at \texttt{b317af0}.
