\subsection{One Mode-S intervention off the frozen Dirichlet concentration}
\label{app:heterogeneity_modeS}

Every Mode-S row elsewhere in this paper is at the frozen $\alpha{=}0.5$. This arm moves $\alpha$ alone on the sign-reversal cell (\texttt{coord\_median} under the committed pixel backdoor, endpoint rungs $\kappa\in\{0,2\}$), holding the cell, the attack, the rung endpoints, $N$, $K$, $f$, the round count, \texttt{cifar\_cnn} and the seed block $42$--$46$ at their published values. It is pre-registered in \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak pre\_registration\_heterogeneity\_modeS.md}, committed as its own commit before either output path existed; both scripts refuse to start unless that document is committed at the recorded hash with a clean working tree. The $\alpha$ grid and the treatment of the frozen column are the paper's own: §\ref{sec:oos_pass}(E) already sweeps compositions at exactly $\alpha \in \{0.1, 1.0, 10.0\}$ and carries $\alpha{=}0.5$ as each pair's headline value rather than a rerun, so each row here is comparable to an existing row at the same concentration under the same convention. This arm uses $n{=}5$ where that sweep used $3$, because small $n$ is the objection it answers.

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lccccc}
\toprule
$\alpha$ & $\Delta_S$ & paired $95\%$ CI & $n$ & mean acc.\ $\kappa{=}0/2$ & premise \\
\midrule
$0.1$ & $+0.218$ & $[+0.064, +0.372]$ & $5$ & $0.49/0.47$ & holds \\
$1.0$ & $+0.137$ & $[+0.046, +0.228]$ & $5$ & $0.79/0.75$ & holds \\
$10.0$ & $+0.165$ & $[+0.127, +0.204]$ & $5$ & $0.81/0.77$ & holds \\
\midrule
$0.5$, frozen, not re-run & $+0.098$ & $[+0.018, +0.178]$ & $5$ & --- & holds \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\textbf{Two verdicts, frozen separately before any number existed}, because a previous arm of ours cleared its pre-registered threshold in two regimes while reproducing the sign in only one. The \emph{sign} verdict is that the within-defense rise reproduces at all three concentrations, each interval excluding zero at $n{=}5$. The \emph{magnitude} verdict is that each interval overlaps the frozen $\alpha{=}0.5$ interval, which is an interval-overlap statement and not the equivalence phrase the supplement defines: \texttt{EQUIV\_MARGIN} is not imported by this arm.

\textbf{Both Mode-S premises were measured at every concentration, not assumed.} At each $\alpha$ the identity rung is the exact identity, with zero coefficient and zero channel-displacement violations over $18$ rows tested with equality rather than a tolerance, and the adversarial coefficient is exactly $1.000000$. Baseline adversarial admission into coordinate median is $0.261$, $0.270$ and $0.271$, nonzero in $30$ of $30$ rounds at each concentration, against the frozen $\alpha{=}0.5$ level of $0.315$: the adversary is admitted at every concentration, so no row here rests on an aggregator that excludes it outright.

\textbf{A gate we corrected after seeing it fail, with the threshold unchanged.} Our first implementation compared the adversarial coefficient share within each (seed, round) cell and held the worst spread over cells to the frozen $10^{-6}$. All three concentrations failed that form, at $2.56\mathrm{e}{-}6$, $3.81\mathrm{e}{-}6$ and $2.88\mathrm{e}{-}6$, and were labelled confounded. So does the published $\alpha{=}0.5$ leg, at $3.97\mathrm{e}{-}6$, which is larger than all three, so that form cannot discriminate concentration and the verdict it emitted asserted a mechanism the measurement does not support. The tolerance is calibrated in its own defining comment for the spread of per-rung \emph{mean} shares, recorded there as $3.48\mathrm{e}{-}7$ on CIFAR-10, and that is the statistic both existing users of the constant compare; the gate now tests it with the same strict inequality. The tolerance is still $10^{-6}$ and was not widened. On the corrected statistic the published $\alpha{=}0.5$ reads $9.22\mathrm{e}{-}8$ and the three concentrations read $1.09\mathrm{e}{-}7$, $1.12\mathrm{e}{-}7$ and $6.75\mathrm{e}{-}7$. The per-cell figures are retained per concentration as diagnostics, and the gate now measures the published configuration before judging any concentration and returns indeterminate rather than condemning a new one with an instrument that condemns the published one.

\textbf{What this arm does not establish.} Clean accuracy at $\alpha{=}0.1$ is $0.49$ at either rung against the $0.75$--$0.81$ the other two span, so that leg clears the frozen $0.35$ floor and is interpretable, but its absolute ASR is not comparable to the others. Three concentrations on one cell, one attack, one dataset and two rungs license no statement about heterogeneity in general, and no mean is taken across concentrations: each $\Delta_S$ carries its own $n$ and its own interval, and no concentration's rung is differenced against another's. The frozen $\alpha{=}0.5$ verdict is not amended; it is printed as frozen beside these rows. The attack is the committed pixel backdoor, non-adaptive by construction, so nothing here concerns adaptivity.

\subsection{An oracle-free upstream defense on CIFAR-100: attempted, and not established}
\label{app:normclip_cifar100}

\noindent \emph{Can the Mode~S contrast be run with a real deployed defense as the upstream transform, on a second dataset, at nonzero baseline adversarial admission?} That one experiment would answer three separate objections at once: Mode~S's rungs pin the adversary with an \texttt{adv\_mask} that a deployed server does not have, every composed-pair experiment in this paper is on CIFAR-10, and the flagship arm's admission baseline is a floor. \texttt{norm\_clip} is the candidate, and the fit looks exact: its coefficient is $c_i=\min(1,\tau/\lVert u_i\rVert)>0$, so it lies inside Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}'s positive per-client rescaling class, it needs no mask, its own $\tau$ is the dose, and $T{=}\mathrm{id}$ is recovered at $\tau$ above every client norm. We pre-registered it in two stages with stage~2 gated on stage~1's verdict (\texttt{experiments/pre\_\allowbreak registration\_\allowbreak normclip\_\allowbreak cifar100.md}, committed before either runner existed, with one append-only amendment), ran stage~1, and \textbf{the premise failed}. Stage~2 never started, so \textbf{no composed-pair ASR result on a second dataset is reported anywhere in this paper}, and none may be inferred from what follows. The CIFAR-100 cell of §\ref{sec:targeted} is a different object and is not withdrawn by this: its upstream transform is the Mode~S instrument rather than a deployed defense, so it replicates the design's sign reversal on a second dataset without composing two defenses.

\textbf{The premise was three conditions frozen before any ASR existed, and two of them passed.} (i)~\emph{\texttt{coord\_median}'s baseline adversarial argmedian fraction on CIFAR-100 must clear a pre-stated floor of $0.05$ in at least half its adversary rounds}: measured $0.2885$, nonzero in $15/15$, against CIFAR-10's $0.2855$. The admission \emph{level} therefore replicates across the change of dataset and label space, which is a real if small positive result and the only one this arm yields. (ii)~\emph{At the identity rung every $c_i$ is exactly $1$ and every channel displacement is exactly $0$}: exact at both epoch counts, tested with \texttt{==} rather than against a tolerance. (iii)~\emph{The adversarial share of coefficient mass must not move beyond the frozen $10^{-6}$}: it moves $0.2533 \to 0.2603$ at $\tau^\ast$, a spread of $6.9{\times}10^{-3}$, with $45$ of $90$ client-rounds clipped and the smallest coefficient $0.838$. So $\Delta_c \ne 0$, the arm measures the attenuation channel rather than the statistic channel, and by the freeze's own demotion clause it is \textbf{not established on CIFAR-100}.

\textbf{The freeze's guess at the mechanism was wrong in sign, and its verdict string still stands as the verdict.} That literal reads \emph{arm confounded on this dataset: the clip attenuates adversaries}. The clip attenuates \emph{benign} clients. Over the $15$ adversary rounds the mean coefficient on adversarial clients is $1.000000$ (not one adversary was clipped in any round) against $0.962780$ on benign clients, so $\Delta_c = {+}0.037$ and the adversarial share rises rather than falls. Under a pixel backdoor adversarial updates are not norm outliers; they sit just below the benign median, so a clip placed \emph{at} that median is a benign-only attenuator. We print the pre-committed literal as the verdict, because that is what the artifact records and what was frozen, and the measured direction beside it, rather than letting the literal's wording stand as a finding.

\textbf{The failure is structural rather than a badly chosen $\tau$, and that is this arm's actual contribution.} A clip's coefficient admits exactly two regimes. If $\tau$ sits above every client norm it does not bind, every $c_i$ is exactly $1$, and $\Delta_c=0$ \emph{by construction}, which is precisely our own $\Delta_c=0.000$ row for this transform under this attack (Table~\ref{tab:delta_c}, at $\rho=1.00$ because no client exceeds $\tau{=}5$), a zero that measures nothing, as that table's discussion already says. If $\tau$ binds, $\Delta_c$ is a difference of two group means of $\min(1,\tau/\lVert u\rVert)$ and is zero only by coincidence, its sign set by which group's norms sit higher: ${-}0.721$ under model scaling, ${+}0.037$ here. \textbf{For this family neutrality and informativeness are mutually exclusive}, at any $\tau$ and on any dataset, because it is a fact about the functional form and not about our configuration. So the mask Mode~S carries is not a convenience, and \textbf{no oracle-free member of Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}'s class serves as a clean probe of the statistic channel}. What we had stated about one table row generalizes to an impossibility for the whole family, and we had not drawn that consequence.

\textbf{$\tau$ was fixed by a rule before the number existed, and the amendment is why the confound is visible at all.} The rule is the median client update norm, so roughly half of clients clip; stage~1 measured $\tau^\ast{=}4.0825$. It was not re-chosen after the failure and no second $\tau$ is reported: searching for a $\tau$ that passes condition~(iii) after seeing this one fail is what pre-registering a rule instead of a value was for. The amendment, appended before either runner existed, moved the rule's instantiation from the one-epoch pass that every existing admission measurement in this paper uses to the two-epoch pass the ASR ladders train. \textbf{At one epoch the same $\tau^\ast$ clips $0$ of $90$ client-rounds and condition~(iii) passes with a spread of exactly $0$}: the confound exists only at the epoch count the ladder would have run at, so an unamended freeze would have certified a stage~2 whose upstream defense was inert. Artifact \texttt{results/normclip\_\allowbreak cifar100\_\allowbreak admission.json}, $3$ seeds $\times$ $6$ rounds $\times$ $2$ epoch counts $\times$ $2$ rungs, no ASR anywhere in it.

\subsection{Both channels without adversary identity, at $n{=}20$ off the admission floor: the pre-registered prediction is refuted, and the arm is not a clean statistic probe}
\label{app:oracle_free}

\noindent \emph{Can the statistic-channel or the magnitude-channel measurement be reproduced without using adversary identity to build the intervention?} That is the first of the three objections §\ref{app:normclip_cifar100} set out to answer, and the one its failed premise left open. Nothing else on disk answers it either: every published measurement of those two channels is a Mode~S, Mode~A or Mode~M rung, and \texttt{apply\_d1\_transform} \emph{raises} for all three without an \texttt{adv\_mask}, so every published measurement reads adversary identity by construction rather than by choice. This arm answers it at $n{=}20$ and off the admission floor, and \textbf{two things went against us}. The pre-registered prediction for the statistic channel is \textbf{refuted}, which we report as a contradiction of the reading this paper leads with and not as a scope condition on it, because that is what the freeze requires. And a measurement we made only \emph{after} the verdict shows the arm does not hold the adversarial coefficient share fixed, so it is not the clean statistic-channel probe the freeze took it for. We report the frozen verdict as frozen and that measurement beside it, and we do not use the second to retract the first.

\textbf{The design uses no new transformation class and no new methodology.} It does not need $\Delta_c$-neutrality, which §\ref{app:normclip_cifar100} shows no \emph{oracle-free} member of Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}'s positive per-client rescaling class can combine with informativeness, because it closes a channel \emph{downstream} of the coefficient, for every client at once, using the two off-diagonal cells of the factorial this paper already pre-registered (§\ref{app:factorial}): \texttt{score\_only} scores the transformed stack and emits the selected client's \emph{original} update, closing the magnitude channel (leg~1), and \texttt{emit\_only} is the mirror, pinning the decision and leaving magnitude open (leg~2). Neither control is told who the adversary is. The upstream transform is \texttt{rfa}, a deployed defense this paper composes elsewhere, whose per-client coefficients are the final Weiszfeld weights of its geometric-median iteration and are computed from the update stack alone; its realized spread is $\rho \in [1.306, 2.194]$, so it is comparable in size to Mode~S's $\rho{=}2.72$ rung rather than a weaker intervention. Three arms, $20$ seeds each, $42$--$61$, fixed before the first run with no top-up clause; $60$ runs, $17.3$ h. Pre-registered in \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak pre\_registration\_\allowbreak oracle\_free\_\allowbreak channels.md}, committed as its own commit at a point when both output paths were verified not to exist, with both scripts refusing to start unless that document is committed at the recorded hash with a clean working tree. Of §\ref{app:normclip_cifar100}'s three objections this arm answers two, adversary identity and the admission floor, and not the third: it is on CIFAR-10, so it adds nothing to §\ref{app:normclip_cifar100}'s standing statement that no composed-pair ASR result on a second dataset is reported anywhere in this paper.

\textbf{The host cell is off the admission floor, by measurement rather than by assertion.} Krum under the committed pixel backdoor admits baseline adversarial mass $0.333$, nonzero in $4$ of $12$ adversary rounds, against $0.000$ in $0$ of $12$ for the same Krum under committed scaling. Admission has somewhere to fall here, so an unchanged admission in this cell is a measurement and not a ceiling artifact, and the floor the flagship arm is charged with is the attack's rather than the aggregator's. This arm recomputes that baseline itself rather than carrying it across from the flagship arm's admission artifact, and reproduces it exactly. The identity rung's mean ASR is $0.3232$, against $0.062$ on the cell the published score-only control ran (§\ref{app:score_only}), where we disclose that a fall of more than $0.062$ was \emph{arithmetically unreachable}. \textbf{That difference in headroom is the whole point of moving cells}, and it is why this arm could return a verdict that one could not. It is also why §\ref{app:pixel_headroom} runs the masked Mode~S dose on this same cell.

\textbf{Oracle-freeness is enforced by the callee, not intended by the caller.} The runner passes \texttt{adv\_mask=None} whenever the upstream stage is named directly, so a dose family reached by this path raises instead of running. That the transform actually ignores identity is asserted on live update stacks rather than read off the branch structure: over $5$ rounds, \texttt{apply\_d1\_transform} with \texttt{adv\_mask=None} is bit-identical to the same call with the true mask, tested with \texttt{torch.equal} on every tensor of every client, and it is not the identity, with first-round per-client norm ratios $1.301$, $0.794$, $0.943$, $1.297$ and $0.665$. The same check confirms that the published Mode~S transform raises without the mask, so both halves of the contrast are witnessed by one measurement rather than by two conventions.

\textbf{The premise was measured before any ASR existed, on a three-way rule frozen before its numbers were read, and it passed.} Across $5$ seeds $\times$ $3$ live rounds, with both rungs on the same raw updates, \texttt{rfa} moves Krum's \emph{decision} in $8$ of $15$ rounds ($0.533$) and its \emph{admitted adversarial mass} in $0$ of $15$, at an aggregate displacement of $0.868$. The identity rung runs through the same code path with $d_1{=}$\texttt{fedavg}: over $15$ rows neither quantity moves in a single one, every realized weight ratio reads $1.000000000000$, and every adversarial coefficient is exactly $1$ on every round where an adversary is sampled, so the measurement reproduces its own baseline exactly rather than to a tolerance. The rule's two constants, a decision floor of $0.10$ and an admission-flatness bound of $0.05$, are \emph{imported} from the Mode~M measurement rather than restated, and the rule was applied to the published Mode~S/Krum arm before it was applied here: at decision change $0.733$ and admission change $0.000$ it types that arm the same way, so this is not an instrument that condemns only what is new. One diagnostic explains the choice of host: under the same \texttt{rfa}, \texttt{cos\_krum}'s decision does not move at all ($0.000$), which is why that aggregator cannot carry this arm and Krum can. \textbf{This measurement is $15$ rows at rounds $0$--$2$ and not the $50$-round ladder}, so the admission channel is asserted flat where it was measured and not throughout the runs behind the ASR figures below.

\textbf{Both pre-registered predictions fail, in opposite directions.} Leg~1 was frozen as $|\Delta\mathrm{ASR}|$ \emph{inside} the $0.15$ equivalence margin; it reads $\mathbf{+0.2969}$ ($\mathrm{sd}\ 0.4243$, paired $95\%$ CI $[+0.0983, +0.4955]$, $n{=}20$), outside the margin \emph{and} excluding zero, which is the exact conjunction the pre-registration named in advance as contradicting this paper's headline reading. Mean ASR rises $0.3232 \to 0.6201$ at mean accuracy $0.5158 \to 0.5671$, so no accuracy collapse explains it, and no rung on either arm of this leg crosses the $0.35$ floor. \textbf{An earlier version of this sentence added that no rung comes \emph{within} $0.10$ of the floor, and that was false of the identity arm}: three of its twenty rungs do, reading $0.3962$, $0.4261$ and $0.4474$, while the statistic arm has none and its minimum is $0.4571$. We correct it here rather than drop it, because §\ref{app:channel_separation} draws a contrast against it that is now a quantitative one. Leg~2 was frozen as nonzero with its interval excluding zero; it reads $-0.0167$ ($\mathrm{sd}\ 0.0874$, CI $[-0.0588, +0.0254]$, $n{=}19$), inside the margin with the interval containing zero. The two verdicts are read separately per leg, as the freeze requires, and neither is reported as the other.

\textbf{What the $+0.297$ is, stated as the distribution rather than as its mean.} This cell is bimodal: per-seed ASR sits either above $0.5$ or below $0.10$, which is why a real effect carries an $\mathrm{sd}$ of $0.4243$. The identity arm has $6$ of $20$ seeds in the high mode and $12$ below $0.10$; the statistic arm has $12$ of $20$ high and \emph{none} below $0.10$. Eight seeds move from the low mode to the high one and none move the other way, so the effect is directional seed by seed and not an artifact of averaging: score-only \texttt{rfa} doubles the number of seeds in which the backdoor takes hold and empties the near-zero mode. Four per-seed differences are negative, and the two large ones ($-0.575$, $-0.505$) are identity-high seeds landing mid-range at $0.350$ and $0.474$ rather than seeds the intervention protected.

\textbf{Leg~2 is unresolved, which is not a zero, and one rung of it is void.} The paired half-width is $\pm0.042$, so the arm is consistent with no effect and equally with an effect of a few points either way; it is not evidence of absence, and the equivalence phrase the supplement defines against the stricter of our two interval conventions is not applied to it. Seed~$61$'s magnitude rung reads clean accuracy $0.3334$, below the frozen $0.35$ floor, so that rung is \emph{void rather than negative} and the pair is dropped, which is why leg~2 reads $n{=}19$ against leg~1's $n{=}20$; both verdicts are unchanged if it is retained ($-0.0159$, CI $[-0.0557,+0.0240]$, $n{=}20$), so nothing turns on the exclusion. The runner's own accuracy gate compares each arm's \emph{mean} against the floor and prints the per-seed minimum beside its verdict, which is why its emitted line reads \texttt{PASS} next to a minimum of $0.3334$; the per-seed exclusion is applied on top of it and the per-run marker in the log is what flags the rung. This leg leaves the mode structure nearly intact ($6$ high, as in the identity arm, and $13$ below $0.10$ against its $12$) while differing from the identity arm on all $20$ seeds, so the channel is open and simply does not resolve. \textbf{Its failure was foreseeable from an artifact we already had} and we should have said so in the freeze: §\ref{app:factorial}'s own top-up reads $\Delta_{\mathrm{EO}} = +0.010$, $95\%$ CI $[-0.028,+0.047]$ at $n{=}20$, and calls the magnitude channel \emph{established inert}. Our leg-2 prediction was taken from Proposition~\ref{prop:nonidentifiability}'s second clause rather than from that number, and the two intervals in fact overlap, so this leg is better read as consistent with a published inertness on a different attack and transform than as a surprise.

\textbf{The measurement we made after the verdict: this arm does not hold the adversarial coefficient share fixed.} \texttt{rfa} pins nothing, and pinning the adversarial coefficient is exactly what Mode~S needs the mask for, so we checked post hoc what the freeze did not gate on. Over the same $12$ adversary rounds, the adversarial share of coefficient mass moves in $\mathbf{12}$ of $\mathbf{12}$, by up to $0.0859$, against the frozen \texttt{SHARE\_TOL} of $10^{-6}$ that Mode~S holds to $3.5{\times}10^{-7}$; the implied $\Delta_c$ has mean $-0.089$ over a range $[-0.537,+0.147]$, changing sign across rounds ($5$ positive, $7$ negative). So the attenuation channel that Figure~\ref{fig:modeS}'s design closes by construction is \textbf{wide open here}, and \texttt{score\_only} does not close it: it fixes what the selected client contributes, not what every client's coefficient does to the scoring that selects it. The mean direction, adversaries attenuated relative to benign clients, is the direction that moves an adversarial update toward the benign cluster in Krum's distances, so it is a named candidate route to the $+0.297$ rather than a channel we can exclude. \textbf{Three things follow, and we state all three.} (i) \emph{The frozen verdict is not amended.} Leg~1 is refuted, and the paragraph above reports it as a contradiction, because the freeze forbids converting a refutation into a scope condition after the fact and this measurement does not license doing so. (ii) \emph{This arm is not a clean probe of the statistic channel alone}, so its refutation does not transfer to the published Mode~S result, whose own settled statement is conditional on \emph{once the adversarial coefficient share is held fixed}; the two interventions differ in two factors at once, the attack and the upstream transform, and this arm decomposes neither. (iii) \emph{The defect is in our pre-registration and it is ours to own.} §\ref{app:normclip_cifar100} demotes an arm on exactly this condition, our freeze recorded \texttt{share\_tol} in its own rules block, the runner recorded \texttt{adv\_coeff\_share} on every round, and the gate was still not written. By the paper's own existing precedent this leg would have been typed confounded in the attenuation channel had the gate been included, and we did not include it.

\textbf{What the arm therefore establishes, which is narrower than either prediction and is not good news for us.} Removing adversary identity re-opened the channel the flagship design exists to close, because closing it is what the oracle is for. That is the empirical companion to §\ref{app:normclip_cifar100}'s impossibility for the whole positive-rescaling family: there we showed no oracle-free member of that class is simultaneously $\Delta_c$-neutral and informative, and here an oracle-free transform outside the neutrality requirement produces a large suppression change with $\Delta_c$ open. \textbf{The honest summary is that the oracle is load-bearing for our negative rather than a laboratory convenience}, and that we have not produced an oracle-free measurement of the statistic channel on any cell. Proposition~\ref{prop:nonidentifiability} is an existence claim over exhibited counterexamples and is not disproved by a cell in which a statistic disturbance does cost suppression; what fails is the \emph{generalization}, and a reader who wanted the negative to travel without an oracle should read this arm as evidence that it does not.

\textbf{Both controls are asserted at the bit level against independently constructed aggregates.} The standing failure mode in this codebase is a manipulation hook that is never called while the run passes silently, so neither control is taken on its docstring. \texttt{emit\_only}'s returned aggregate equals FedAvg over the \emph{raw}-stack selection's transformed update, and \texttt{score\_only}'s equals FedAvg over the \emph{transformed}-stack selection's raw update, both tested with \texttt{torch.equal} rather than against a tolerance, each against an aggregate the checker builds for itself from the selection that control is defined to use. So \texttt{emit\_only}'s decision is pinned to the identity arm's by construction and not by approximation, and no seed's \texttt{emit\_only} ASR equals the identity arm's, so the pinned decision did not make the arm inert. The two controls select different clients in $3$ of the $5$ rounds checked, so they are not the same computation.

\textbf{Three harness checks ran before the ladder, and their verdicts are in the artifact rather than in a terminal.} The one keyword this arm adds to shared code is proved bit-neutral by recomputing a frozen Mode~S cell through the patched runner, at $\Delta=(0,0)$ on both accuracy and ASR; the identity arm is proved to be Krum standalone \emph{across suites} by reproducing the pilot's own \texttt{krum} under pixel row at a shared seed, also at $\Delta=(0,0)$, which is the cross-suite assertion an existing dose-response runner already makes against that same pilot file; and the \texttt{rfa} branch is proved entered and non-inert. All three verdicts are persisted, because a check whose verdict is discarded leaves the claim it supports witnessed only by a terminal and costs hours to repeat.

\textbf{What this arm does not establish.} One aggregator, one attack, one dataset and one upstream defense license no statement of the form \emph{the negative fails without an oracle} in general, any more than they would have licensed its converse; what they license is \emph{in this cell, without adversary identity}, and that is the only form used above. The refutation is of a pre-registered directional prediction, in the sense this paper fixes for that word throughout, and not of any mathematical claim. The two controls modify the aggregator's internals, so this remains an instrument for causal identification and not a deployable defense: it answers the adversary-identity objection and leaves the deployability one open exactly where the rest of the paper leaves it, on the same ground already given for the published control, that score-only Krum aggregates an update its own scoring stage never saw. The attack is the committed pixel backdoor, non-adaptive by construction, so nothing here concerns adaptive robustness. And no row is pooled with any published row: the $n{=}3$ pilot value for this cell is a harness-check target and is never used as data, so all $60$ runs behind the numbers above are new. §\ref{app:channel_separation} takes the gap this arm leaves as its subject: it screens every deployed oracle-free family for an instrument that would close it, reports what that screen returned, constructs an instrument when the screen returns nothing eligible, and runs it, with the result going against this paper a second time.

\subsection{Separating the two channels: a screen that returns nothing eligible, a pre-registration of ours that failed its own gate before any result existed, and a constructed boundary instrument whose ladder contradicts the reading we lead with}
\label{app:channel_separation}

\noindent \emph{Can the decision channel be moved while the adversarial coefficient share is held to the tolerance Mode~S itself meets, and without reading adversary identity?} §\ref{app:oracle_free} moved both channels at once and disclosed it post hoc, and the paragraph above records that the gate was recorded in that freeze's own rules block and then not written. This subsection writes it. Four things are reported: a screen of the deployed oracle-free class that returns nothing eligible, a constructed boundary instrument that meets the gate on a supremum taken over every possible adversary set, so that no mask is needed to compute it, a pre-registration of our own that failed that gate in a premise check run before any result existed, which cost us a superseding document, and the ladder that document froze. \textbf{The branch that fired is the one the freeze named in advance as contradicting this paper's headline reading.} With the adversarial share of coefficient mass held an order inside \texttt{SHARE\_TOL}, the tolerance Mode~S itself meets, on every round of both arms, and with no adversary identity read anywhere in the construction, moving Krum's selection across a single boundary crossing moves ASR by $\mathbf{+0.322}$ at $n{=}20$, outside the frozen $0.15$ margin and with the paired interval excluding zero. We report that as a contradiction rather than convert it into a scope condition, because that is what the freeze requires, and the paragraphs below state precisely what it does and does not contradict. The first three things reported read the update stack and no outcome; the fourth is an outcome measurement and is the only one of the four that bears on whether suppression survives the intervention.

\textbf{The screen returns \texttt{NO\_ELIGIBLE\_FAMILY}, and an empty result is the outcome it was pre-registered to be allowed to return.} Over $5$ seeds and $3$ live rounds on the same host cell as §\ref{app:oracle_free}, Krum under the committed pixel backdoor, each of the four oracle-free $d_1$ families \texttt{apply\_d1\_transform} implements is measured on one raw stack per round: decision movement on Krum, admission flatness, and the adversarial share of coefficient mass. Every threshold is \emph{imported}, the decision floor $0.10$ and the admission-flatness bound $0.05$ from the Mode~M measurement and \texttt{SHARE\_TOL} $=10^{-6}$ from the admission measurement, so none was chosen after these numbers were read. The identity rung is exactly flat on all $15$ rows, with $\max|\rho - 1| = 0$. \texttt{norm\_clip} moves nothing at all, and that is a fact about $\tau{=}5.0$ against these update norms rather than a property of the defense: the largest client norm seen over $5$ rounds is $3.044$, so $c_i \equiv 1$ and the transform \emph{is} the identity. The other three all move the decision and all move the share past tolerance in $12$ of $12$ adversary rounds: \texttt{reputation} at decision rate $0.200$ with share excursion up to $0.0998$, \texttt{foolsgold} at $0.533$ with $0.400$ and an admission change of $0.067$ that also breaches the flatness bound, and \texttt{rfa} at $0.533$ with $0.0859$, which is §\ref{app:oracle_free}'s own confound recovered by an instrument that was written to look for it. \textbf{The dichotomy is the finding}: on this cell every family that moves the decision moves the share, and the only share-neutral family is the one that does nothing. That is the quantitative shadow of §\ref{app:normclip_cifar100}'s impossibility, measured across the deployed class rather than argued for one member, and it is \emph{not} a claim that no such transform exists in general.

\textbf{The constructed instrument, which we call the \emph{boundary blend}, and why it does not contradict the impossibility.} Blend each client's coefficient toward \texttt{rfa}'s, $c_i(t) = (1-t) + t\,c_i^{\mathrm{rfa}}$ for $t\in[0,1]$, reading $c^{\mathrm{rfa}}$ back from the transformed norms exactly as the admission measurement already does. Krum's argmin changes at isolated values of $t$ while the share moves continuously, so a pair of points straddling one crossing has a decision change by construction and a coefficient difference that shrinks with the bracket. The difference vector does not depend on which clients are adversarial, so the neutrality is \emph{uniform} rather than neutrality for one mask, and we measure it as such: the reported quantity is the supremum of the share gap over all $30$ nonempty proper subsets of the $K{=}5$ participants, which upper-bounds the realized gap for every adversary set and needs no mask to compute. The impossibility in §\ref{app:normclip_cifar100} is about \emph{exact} neutrality, which oracle-freeness forces to hold for every adversary set at once and which therefore forces $c$ constant; the blend is not exactly neutral, so it is consistent with that theorem, and what this instrument adds is the tolerance-parameterized statement that \emph{approximate} uniform neutrality is reachable at any tolerance. The price is stated rather than hidden: informativeness is confined to rounds in which a crossing lies on the blend path, which is $13$ of the $25$ rounds measured here.

\textbf{Our first freeze of that instrument failed its own share gate, and we report the failure against ourselves.} It bisected the crossing of the float64 Gram statistic; the shipped \texttt{krum\_selection} runs in float32 and crosses at a slightly different $t$, so the converged bracket is tight around the wrong predicate. Measured on live stacks before any arm was run, that instrument's supremum share gap exceeds \texttt{SHARE\_TOL} in $13$ of $13$ flip rounds, up to $1.059{\times}10^{-4}$, and its realized gap exceeds it in $11$ of the $12$ rounds that carry an adversary; it also fails its own \emph{flip} gate, the shipped selections differing across the pair in only $10$ of $13$. One sentence in that document needs correcting rather than defending: the $5.109{\times}10^{-7}$ it offered as an existence proof of a within-tolerance pair is the \emph{minimum} over those same $12$ rounds and the only one inside tolerance, and the supremum on that same round is $8.368{\times}10^{-5}$. The repair its own linearity argument implies, shrink the bracket and bisect the same float64 predicate to convergence, is measured too: it is share-neutral to $3.3{\times}10^{-16}$ and the shipped selections differ in $0$ of $13$, so it is perfectly neutral and informative on nothing. \textbf{The general lesson is one sentence: bisect the predicate you intend to satisfy, and stop on the gated quantity rather than on a proxy for it.}

\textbf{The corrected instrument, and an append-only supersession.} The bisection now runs on the shipped selection itself; a float64 scan may only \emph{propose} a bracket, which the shipped predicate must confirm at both endpoints, and an unconfirmed bracket yields no dose for that round rather than a dose the gate cannot vouch for. It halts when the mask-free supremum is at or below \texttt{SHARE\_TOL}$/10$, which is the gated quantity and not a proxy. Measured on the same rounds: $13$ of $13$ shipped selection differences at a supremum of at most $9.60{\times}10^{-8}$, in $20$ to $21$ bisection steps and $2$ to $26$ shipped Krum evaluations per round. The step cap of $40$ is a \emph{cost} bound, so exhausting it is a gate failure and never a relaxed tolerance. One shipped evaluation costs $66.0$ ms at $D = 1{,}117{,}354$. The realized cost, re-derived from the finished artifact rather than carried over as the arithmetic we froze, is $40$ runs in $8.7$ h, of which the search is $29{,}925$ shipped Krum evaluations, or $0.55$ h and $6\%$ of the total; we had projected $0.95$ h of search on an $11.5$ h ladder from §\ref{app:oracle_free}'s measured per-run cost, so this arm came in under its own estimate rather than over it. The first freeze, \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak pre\_registration\_\allowbreak oracle\_free\_\allowbreak decomposition.md} at commit \texttt{bed6562}, is left byte-untouched and superseded by \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak pre\_registration\_\allowbreak oracle\_free\_\allowbreak decomposition\_refined.md} at \texttt{947c000}, which cites it, records the failure with these numbers, pins the premise artifact by md5, and corrects that sentence; the runner refuses to start unless the superseded document is present, last committed at its recorded hash, and clean, so the append-only claim is enforced mechanically rather than promised. This is the same course §\ref{app:normclip_cifar100} took with a frozen arm whose premise failed, and it is why the count of pre-registration documents rises rather than holding. \textbf{Four harness checks ran before the ladder and their verdict dict is persisted into the artifact rather than left in a terminal.} The one keyword this arm adds to shared code is bit-neutral on a frozen Mode~S cell at $\Delta=(0,0)$; the blend at $t{=}0$ reproduces §\ref{app:oracle_free}'s identity arm at a shared seed, also at $\Delta=(0,0)$, \emph{and} reports that the hook was invoked $50$ times over $50$ rounds, which is the assertion that matters, because the standing failure mode in this codebase is a manipulation hook that is never called while the run passes silently; the Gram proposal equals the shipped selection at both endpoints on live stacks; and both gates pass on the ladder's own pair in all $12$ flip rounds that carry an adversary, re-measured live in the same process rather than read back from a file.

\textbf{Both verdicts on the leg, read separately.} The primary estimand is the paired difference $B - A$ at the same $20$ seeds, $42$--$61$, both arms new, no mixed-$n$ leg and no top-up clause. It reads $\mathbf{+0.3218}$ ($\mathrm{sd}\ 0.3288$, paired $95\%$ CI $[+0.1680, +0.4757]$, half-width $\pm0.1539$, $n{=}20$). The \emph{sign} verdict is that the interval excludes zero, positive. The \emph{margin} verdict is that $|\Delta|$ lies outside the frozen $0.15$ margin. Neither is reported as the other, because an interval can sit inside the margin and still exclude zero, and the freeze fixed the two labels separately for that reason. Mean ASR rises $0.2485 \to 0.5703$ while mean accuracy \emph{also} rises, $0.4944 \to 0.5271$, so no accuracy collapse explains the move. The frozen $0.35$ floor is live and no rung is void: the per-seed minimum is $0.3604$ on arm~A at seed~$61$ and $0.3713$ on arm~B at seed~$55$. The floor nonetheless sits closer here than on §\ref{app:oracle_free}'s leg~1, even though nothing crosses it on either: five arm-A rungs and four arm-B rungs come within $0.10$ of it, nine of forty, against three of forty there, which is the count that paragraph now states after correcting an earlier claim of none.

\textbf{What the $+0.322$ is, stated as the distribution rather than as its mean.} This cell is bimodal in the same way §\ref{app:oracle_free}'s is, which is why a real effect carries an $\mathrm{sd}$ of $0.3288$. Arm~A has $4$ of $20$ seeds above ASR $0.5$ and $13$ below $0.10$; arm~B has $11$ above $0.5$ and $1$ below $0.10$. Six seeds move from the near-zero mode into the high one and none move the other way, so the effect is directional seed by seed rather than an artifact of averaging: one grid step across the crossing nearly empties the mode in which the backdoor fails to take hold. Eighteen of the $20$ differences are positive and the median is $+0.2445$, below the mean, so the distribution is right-skewed and the mean is the larger of the two summaries. The mean is \emph{attenuated} by arithmetic rather than inflated: four seeds already sit above ASR $0.9$ on arm~A with almost no headroom left, and their differences are $+0.033$, $+0.001$, $+0.010$ and $-0.003$ for that reason alone. Of the two negative differences only one is material, seed~$42$ at $-0.108$, from $0.434$ down to $0.326$; the other is $-0.003$ on a saturated seed.

\textbf{The share gate is asserted on every round of both arms rather than sampled.} Across all $2{,}000$ rounds, $2$ arms $\times$ $20$ seeds $\times$ $50$ rounds, the mask-free supremum over all $30$ nonempty proper adversary subsets never exceeds \texttt{SHARE\_TOL}, and never exceeds the stricter stopping target of \texttt{SHARE\_TOL}$/10$ either: its maximum anywhere is $9.999{\times}10^{-8}$, against the $3.5{\times}10^{-7}$ Mode~S holds to \emph{with} a mask, and the largest per-client coefficient gap is $4.98{\times}10^{-7}$. Of those rounds $1{,}128$ carry a crossing and the shipped selections differ in every one of them, so the dose is delivered and not merely sought; the remaining $872$ are bit-identical across the arms at a supremum of exactly $0$. No round exhausted the $40$-step cost bound, the most any round needed being $21$ steps and $26$ shipped Krum evaluations.

\textbf{What the arm contradicts, and the one channel it still cannot separate.} It contradicts the second half of the dissociation this paper leads with, that Krum's decision can flip while suppression stays inside the pre-registered $\pm0.15$ margin: on this cell, without adversary identity, and with the attenuation channel held to $10^{-7}$, an order inside \texttt{SHARE\_TOL} and $3.5{\times}$ tighter than the $3.5{\times}10^{-7}$ Mode~S reaches with a mask, a decision flip carries $+0.322$ and leaves that margin. We report that as a contradiction and do not convert it into a scope condition. \textbf{But it separates the decision channel from attenuation only, not from admission}, and that limitation is structural rather than an omission we could repair: deciding whether a flip moved Krum from a benign client to an adversarial one requires exactly the mask this instrument refuses, so the artifact records no admission quantity and could not. The $+0.322$ may therefore run through admission, through the scoring alone, or through both, and this arm does not decompose it. Two consequences follow and we state both. The frozen verdict is not amended by that limitation, on the same principle §\ref{app:oracle_free} applied to its own post hoc measurement: the refuting branch fired on its own stated terms and we do not use a caveat discovered afterwards to retract it. And the contrast is \emph{local}, at the boundary: both arms are nearly-\texttt{rfa} stacks at adjacent points on the blend path, the per-run mean crossing sitting at $t^\star \in [0.578, 0.832]$, so this is not an identity arm against a statistic disturbance and must not be read as one. The flip direction is uncontrolled, which the freeze records in advance, so $+0.322$ is the price of \emph{perturbing} Krum's selection at the boundary and not a benefit a defense could collect by choosing a side.

\textbf{What this subsection does not establish.} The masked Mode~S measurement stands exactly as measured; what this arm denies is its generalization to this cell without the oracle, and §\ref{app:oracle_free}'s standing summary, that the oracle is load-bearing for our negative rather than a laboratory convenience, is strengthened by it rather than revised. A reader looking for an oracle-free measurement of the statistic channel will not find one here either, and §\ref{app:oracle_free}'s standing statement that we have not produced one is unchanged, for the reason the paragraph above gives: that measurement needs an identity arm against a disturbed arm, and this ladder compares two disturbed arms one grid step apart. The screen's empty result is a statement about four families, at one cell, on $5$ seeds and $3$ rounds, at Mode~S's tolerance, and the premise check is $25$ rounds of instrument behaviour with no outcome attached. The ladder is one aggregator, one attack, one dataset and one upstream transform, at $20$ seeds paired within arm, so it licenses no statement of the form \emph{the negative fails without an oracle} in general, any more than it would have licensed its converse; what it licenses is \emph{in this cell, without adversary identity, with the coefficient share held to $10^{-7}$}, and that is the only form used above. Its freeze named the branch that contradicts this paper's headline reading as plainly as the branch that supports it, and the branch that fired is the first of the two. One population note, so that a number is not mistaken for a revision: the realized \texttt{rfa} spread over the $25$ rounds measured here is $\rho\in[1.306, 2.708]$, against $[1.306, 2.194]$ reported in §\ref{app:oracle_free} over its own $15$ rounds, which is a larger population and not a correction to that interval.

\subsection{Mode~S on the one cell where both margin legs bind: the two-sided containment fails, and the one-sided non-increase holds by a wide margin, in the direction of \emph{more} suppression}
\label{app:pixel_headroom}

\noindent \emph{What does the Mode~S contrast return on a cell where both readings of preservation have somewhere to fall?} The cell this paper scores its flagship reading on is uninformative twice over, and App.~\ref{par:reach} is this round's admission of the second half. Krum under committed scaling admits $0.000$ adversarial mass at every rung, so the zero there is a floor rather than a measurement; and that cell's identity rung has mean ASR $0.0439$, so with ASR bounded below by $0$ a fall of $0.15$ is arithmetically unavailable and only the upper margin leg is a test. This arm moves to the cell §\ref{app:oracle_free} identified for exactly this reason, Krum under the committed pixel backdoor, where baseline adversarial admission is $0.333$ in $4$ of $12$ adversary rounds and the identity rung's mean ASR is $0.3232$, above the frozen $0.15$: \textbf{both legs are genuine tests here, so a two-sided reading would be earned if one held}. It does not hold. \textbf{The outcome falls in a branch our own freeze did not enumerate, and we report that as a defect in the freeze rather than assign the result to the nearest branch it did name}: the paired difference is large, negative, and outside the margin, so the dose does not leave suppression where it found it, it improves suppression sharply. Pre-registered in \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak pre\_registration\_\allowbreak modeS\_\allowbreak pixel\_\allowbreak headroom.md}, committed as its own commit at \texttt{c89e963} with both output paths verified not to exist; $40$ new runs at $20$ seeds, $42$--$61$; artifact \texttt{results/\allowbreak modeS\_\allowbreak pixel\_\allowbreak headroom/\allowbreak summary.json}.

\textbf{The freeze registers no prediction for the sign, and that was an admission rather than caution.} Two of our own measurements pointed opposite ways before this arm ran: the flagship's $-0.010$ under a masked dose on the scaling attack, and a positive, margin-leaving difference under an oracle-free construction on this very cell (§\ref{app:channel_separation}). Registering a direction would have been choosing which of our own results to believe, so what was frozen is the branch structure instead: three verdicts per estimand, \emph{sign} (does the paired $95\%$ interval exclude zero), \emph{margin} (is $|\Delta|$ below the frozen $0.15$) and \emph{the one-sided non-increase} (is the $95\%$ upper bound below $+0.15$), with a standing prohibition on reporting any one of the three as another. \textbf{Four branches were enumerated and none of them covers what happened}: \emph{confirming} (interval containing zero inside the margin), \emph{refuting} (interval excluding zero with $|\Delta| > 0.15$ toward \emph{increased} ASR, reported as a contradiction and never converted into a scope condition), a small consistent rise that the document called the one to expect, and \emph{unresolved}. All four presuppose that a move which leaves the margin is upward, and the measured move leaves the margin downward. \textbf{That gap is ours and we name it rather than round the result to the nearest branch}: what the freeze does bind is the three verdicts, the prohibition on substituting one for another, and the rule that no mechanism may be written inside a verdict literal, and those are what the paragraphs below are held to. Two endpoint rungs only, $\kappa{=}0$ and $\kappa{=}2$ at $\rho{=}1$ and $\rho{=}54.598$: the interior rungs are not run, no Jonckheere--Terpstra statistic is computed for this arm, and it is nowhere described as a ladder.

\textbf{The identity leg is imported rather than re-run, and the import is asserted at the bit level before the ladder starts.} Mode~S at $\kappa{=}0$ returns the update stack unwrapped before the permutation is drawn, and the permutation when drawn uses a local $\texttt{default\_rng}([\mathrm{seed},\mathrm{round}])$ rather than the global stream, so this path and §\ref{app:oracle_free}'s identity arm are the same computation. That is asserted and not assumed: the published row's accuracy and ASR at seed~$42$, $0.3962$ and $0.7787777777777778$, are reproduced through this runner at $\Delta=(0,0)$ on both quantities, and again with \texttt{score\_only} set, which is why both estimands share one subtrahend. \textbf{The check is blocking rather than decorative}, because the published rows ran with \texttt{adv\_mask=None} through a \texttt{d1\_override} and this path passes the real mask; had it failed, the freeze required re-running both identity legs as $40$ further runs with no reconciliation attempted. A third check establishes that the dose pins the adversary on this cell as it does on the published one: over $32{,}000$ coefficient vectors, every adversarial coefficient is exactly $1$ (maximum deviation $0.0\mathrm{e}{+}00$) and the adversarial share of coefficient mass equals $n_{\mathrm{adv}}/K$ to $5.6{\times}10^{-17}$ against a $10^{-12}$ tolerance, taken \emph{exhaustively} over all $32$ subsets of the $K{=}5$ participants rather than over the masks the runs happened to draw. All three verdicts are persisted into the artifact, because a check whose verdict is discarded is witnessed only by a terminal.

\textbf{The three verdicts on $\Delta_{\mathrm{full}}$, read separately.} The primary estimand is the paired difference between Mode~S at $\kappa{=}2$ and the identity rung at the same $20$ seeds. It reads $\mathbf{-0.2857}$ ($\mathrm{sd}\ 0.4243$, paired $95\%$ CI $[-0.4842, -0.0871]$, half-width $\pm0.1986$, $n{=}20$). The \emph{sign} verdict is that the interval excludes zero, negative. The \emph{margin} verdict is that $|\Delta|$ lies \emph{outside} the frozen $0.15$, so the two-sided containment this paper reports on the flagship cell fails here. The \emph{one-sided} verdict is that the $95\%$ upper bound on a rise is $-0.087$, which is not merely below the frozen $+0.15$ but below zero, so the non-increase holds more strongly than on any arm in Table~\ref{tab:reach}. Those are three independent labels and none is reported as another: this arm is the mirror image of the flagship, where the two-sided reading passes on a leg that was never at risk, and it is the clearest evidence we have that the relabelling of App.~\ref{par:reach} is substantive rather than cosmetic. Mean ASR falls $0.3232 \to 0.0375$ while mean clean accuracy \emph{rises}, $0.5158 \to 0.5549$, so no destroyed model explains the fall; the frozen $0.35$ floor is live, no rung is void, and the per-seed minimum rises from $0.3962$ on the identity arm to $0.4983$ under the dose.

\textbf{What the $-0.286$ is, stated as the distribution rather than as its mean, and the per-seed form of the reachability arithmetic.} This is the same bimodal cell §\ref{app:oracle_free} reports, and the imported identity arm reproduces that paper's own count exactly: $6$ of $20$ seeds above ASR $0.5$ and $12$ below $0.10$, with a maximum of $0.988$. \textbf{Under the dose the high mode is empty}: no seed reaches $0.11$, the largest dosed ASR anywhere being $0.1074$, and all six high-mode seeds fall below $0.10$ while none moves the other way. So the effect is directional seed by seed rather than an artifact of averaging. The median difference is only $-0.0252$ against a mean of $-0.2857$, and $12$ of the $20$ differences are smaller than $0.05$ in absolute value, which is \emph{arithmetic and not noise}: $13$ of the $20$ identity rungs sit below $0.15$, so those seeds individually cannot fall past the margin however good the defense becomes. The seven seeds whose identity ASR exceeds $0.15$ are \emph{exactly} the seven whose difference falls past $-0.15$, on both estimands. Reachability therefore governs this cell per seed even where it does not govern it in the mean, and a cell can clear the margin on its identity mean while most of its seeds cannot. Five differences are positive and all five are small, the largest being $+0.107$ on seed~$61$, whose identity rung reads ASR $0.0001$. The Reproducibility statement's per-seed promise covers this arm, and these are the seven values that carry it, identity ASR against the full dose and then the score-only dose: seed~$42$, $0.779 \to 0.013$ and $0.001$; $44$, $0.932 \to 0.053$ and $0.011$; $49$, $0.419 \to 0.088$ and $0.023$; $51$, $0.925 \to 0.052$ and $0.011$; $54$, $0.979 \to 0.020$ and $0.000$; $58$, $0.983 \to 0.023$ and $0.002$; $59$, $0.988 \to 0.007$ and $0.051$. The other thirteen seeds start at $0.110$ or below and end at $0.107$ or below under the full dose and $0.079$ or below under the score-only one, and the artifact's \texttt{per\_seed} rows carry every value quoted in this subsection.

\textbf{The magnitude channel is closed and the fall survives, and this is the reviewer-suggested control of §\ref{app:score_only} run at all $20$ seeds with a paired interval.} $\Delta_{\mathrm{score}}$, the same contrast with \texttt{score\_only} Krum scoring the transformed stack and emitting the selected client's original update, reads $\mathbf{-0.2974}$ ($\mathrm{sd}\ 0.4288$, CI $[-0.4981, -0.0968]$, $n{=}20$), with the same three verdicts and the same branch. \textbf{Closing the magnitude channel moves the effect by $0.012$}, and no seed's two differences disagree by more than $0.105$, so the route to the fall is not the magnitude or geometry of the aggregated update. Mean accuracy rises further on this leg, $0.5158 \to 0.6149$ at a per-seed minimum of $0.5149$, and every dosed rung is below ASR $0.08$. This leg was staged behind the first and its running condition is recorded in the artifact: it would have been skipped had $\Delta_{\mathrm{full}}$ fired the refuting branch, so the staging could only ever have withheld a control that followed an unfavourable-to-nobody result, never suppressed an unfavourable one. The published score-only control remains at $n{=}5$ on the flagship cell and is not restated at $n{=}20$; this is a second cell rather than a top-up of that one, and §\ref{app:score_only}'s seed count is unchanged.

\textbf{The same-cell contrast with our two oracle-free arms, and what it does and does not identify.} Both published arms on this cell move ASR \emph{up} by about a third: §\ref{app:oracle_free}'s leg~1 reads $+0.2969$ ($95\%$ CI $[+0.0983,+0.4955]$, $n{=}20$) and §\ref{app:channel_separation}'s boundary ladder reads $+0.3218$ ($[+0.1680,+0.4757]$, $n{=}20$). The first of those is the directly comparable one, and comparable in an unusually strong sense: it is also a \texttt{score\_only} arm, at the same $20$ seeds, against the \emph{bit-identical} identity rows this arm imports. Two score-only interventions on one cell against one baseline therefore return $+0.2969$ and $-0.2974$, near mirror images. \textbf{What this removes is one of the two confounds that paragraph disclosed}, which recorded that its refutation could not transfer to the published Mode~S result because the two interventions differed in the attack \emph{and} the upstream transform and it decomposed neither: holding the cell and the attack fixed, the disagreement survives, so the cell is not a sufficient explanation of it. \textbf{That inference is post hoc and is marked as such.} The freeze attached a conclusion of this shape to its confirming branch, which did not fire, so what carries it here is not a branch's authority but the arithmetic that two interventions against one baseline returned opposite signs. \textbf{What this does not do is identify the cause}, because the two arms still differ in two respects at once: \texttt{rfa} reads no adversary identity and this dose reads it by construction, and \texttt{rfa}'s realized spread is $\rho \in [1.306,2.194]$ against this dose's $\rho{=}54.598$. Oracle access and dose magnitude are not separated by anything here, and the sign difference is not attributed to either. §\ref{app:oracle_free}'s standing summary, that the oracle is load-bearing for our negative rather than a laboratory convenience, is untouched by this arm, as is its statement that we have not produced an oracle-free measurement of the statistic channel on any cell: \textbf{this arm is masked, so it is not one}.

\textbf{What is not measured here, so that nothing is inferred from a quantity this arm never produced.} \texttt{run\_one} returns clean accuracy and ASR and nothing else, so \textbf{no channel quantity and no admission quantity is measured by this arm at all}. The $0.333$ baseline admission that made the cell eligible is \emph{imported} from the frozen measurement §\ref{app:oracle_free} reports and is not re-measured here, and Krum's decision-change rate under this dose on this cell is simply unknown. So it may not be said that a statistic disturbance costs or buys suppression here, in either direction: what is measured is that the same masked dose, at the same endpoint, with the adversarial coefficient share pinned to $5.6{\times}10^{-17}$, moves ASR down by $0.286$ on a cell whose identity mean $0.3232$ left a fall of $0.323$ available and a rise of $0.677$, so the room was not symmetric and the measured move used $88\%$ of the smaller side. Nothing here bears on whether admission predicts security, which this paper denies establishing, and the improvement is not a defense: Mode~S needs an \texttt{adv\_mask} no deployed server has, and \texttt{score\_only} Krum aggregates an update its own scoring stage never saw.

\textbf{What this arm does not establish.} One aggregator, one attack, one dataset, one architecture and two rungs license no statement of the form \emph{the dissociation holds generally} and none of the form \emph{it fails generally}; what they license is \emph{in this cell, at $n{=}20$, with both margin legs binding and admission off its floor}, and that is the only form used above. No published row is revised: the flagship's $-0.010$, $95\%$ CI $[-0.032,+0.012]$ at $n{=}20$ on Krum under committed scaling stands exactly as printed, and this arm is a different cell rather than a correction to it. No pre-registered directional prediction is refuted, because the freeze registered no sign; the word \emph{refuted} keeps the meaning this paper fixes for it throughout and is not used of this arm. The interval is wide, $\pm0.199$, which a bimodal cell at $20$ seeds buys, so the magnitude is poorly determined even though its sign and its position relative to the margin are not. And the two-sided containment failing here says nothing about whether it would fail on the flagship cell, where it cannot be tested on both legs at all, which is the asymmetry App.~\ref{par:reach} exists to make visible.

\section{A subordinate boundary study: what the screen buys, and what it does not}
\label{sec:composability_validation}
\label{app:validation_full}

Criterion~\ref{thm:composability} predicts for each two-defense pair whether composition achieves low max-committed ASR. All experiments in this section and §\ref{sec:fg_rfa_flagship} use one deployment regime throughout (CIFAR-10, $N{=}10$ clients, $K{=}5$ per round, adversarial fraction $f{=}0.2$, 50 rounds, 4-layer CNN), with committed attacks model\_scaling and backdoor\_pixel. \textbf{Convention:} unless stated otherwise, \emph{ASR} means \emph{max-committed ASR} (the maximum over the two committed attacks of the per-attack mean across seeds) thresholded at $0.5$: the level at which the trigger fires on the majority of triggered inputs, so that ``suppresses'' means the backdoor is not the model's dominant response. \textbf{That $0.5$ is an operational classification threshold, not a theoretically privileged security boundary}: nothing in the propositions depends on it, and a deployment with a lower tolerance should read the per-cell ASR rather than the label. adaptive-attack, order-sensitivity and per-seed figures are labelled explicitly where they differ. A second gate is needed because a destroyed model has a low ASR for the wrong reason: every cell in this section, §\ref{sec:fg_rfa_flagship} and §\ref{app:ladder}--§\ref{app:targeted_full} must also hold mean clean accuracy $\ge 0.35$ (our \textbf{accuracy floor}), below which we call the cell \emph{uninterpretable} rather than suppressed, in either direction. \textbf{That scope is wider than an earlier version of this sentence stated}: it named §\ref{app:ladder}--§\ref{app:targeted_full} alone, while the paragraph it sits in scopes itself to this section and §\ref{sec:fg_rfa_flagship}, so the floor was announced as a second gate and then withheld from most of the cells it governs. Widening it to what this paragraph already claims reclassifies exactly one cell, fg$\to$rfa at $\alpha{=}0.1$ (§\ref{sec:oos_pass}(E)), and that reclassification \emph{weakens} a limitation we state against ourselves, which is why both readings are printed there rather than only the gate-applied one. \textbf{Sample sizes are small and were fixed before the runs}: $n{=}5$ seeds per cell in the interventional suites ($n{=}8$ for \texttt{cos\_krum}, $n{=}3$ frozen for the second-dataset replication, which also reads at a pooled, post-hoc $n{=}5$, §\ref{app:targeted_full}), $n{=}30$ for the FoolsGold$\to$RFA characterization, so intervals are wide, and no null result is read as flatness unless it clears a pre-registered practical-equivalence margin. \textbf{``Refuted'' means one thing throughout}: a pre-registered \emph{directional} hypothesis was rejected against the rule frozen for it, never that a mathematical claim was disproved. Adversaries are the fixed client identities $\{0,\dots,fN-1\}$ and each round samples $K$ participants uniformly without replacement from all $N$, with no stratification, so the per-round adversary count is \emph{hypergeometric} rather than fixed: for $N{=}10$, $f{=}0.2$, $K{=}5$, $P(n_a{=}0)=P(n_a{=}2)=56/252=0.222$ and $P(n_a{=}1)=140/252=0.556$ (measured over 100 rounds: $0.19/0.54/0.27$). Two consequences the data bear out: CoordMedian's count-majority condition $n_a < K/2 = 2.5$ holds for \emph{every} attainable $n_a\in\{0,1,2\}$, i.e.\ in 100\% of rounds, while TrimmedMean's budget $\lfloor\beta K\rfloor = \lfloor 0.2\cdot 5\rfloor = 1$ is exceeded whenever $n_a{=}2$: $22.2\%$ of rounds, ${\approx}11$ of 50. That is a second, count-based reason FoolsGold$\to$TrimmedMean fails ($0.508$) while FoolsGold$\to$CoordMedian succeeds ($0.107$) under an identical upstream transform; we retain the pre-registered C3-FAIL label, the budget violation being an additional mechanism rather than a relabelling. We validate in two waves. The \textbf{development set} (18 targeted pairs, Table~\ref{tab:composability}) spans all criterion prediction categories and was used to refine the criterion (adding C3). The \textbf{held-out set} (24 pairs, Table~\ref{tab:composability_heldout}) was \emph{pre-registered}: all 24 predictions were committed to version control (commit \texttt{7e4d9b1}) \emph{before} any held-out experiment was run, eliminating post-hoc classification concerns. The criterion predicts LOW ($<$0.5) or HIGH ($\geq$0.5) max-committed ASR. The verdict categories are:

norm\_clip $\to$ rfa & C3 & HIGH & 0.853 & \checkmark \\
rfa $\to$ trimmed\_mean & C3 & HIGH & 0.678 & \checkmark \\
rfa $\to$ coord\_median & C3 & HIGH & 0.485 & $\times$ \\
foolsgold $\to$ trimmed\_mean & C3 & HIGH & 0.508 & \checkmark \\
norm\_clip $\to$ coord\_median & C3 & HIGH & 0.433 & $\times$ \\
\midrule
\multicolumn{5}{l}{\emph{DEGEN: $d_1$ has no per-client transform (predicted HIGH)}} \\
fedavg $\to$ norm\_clip$^\ast$ & DEGEN & HIGH & 0.935 & \checkmark \\
fedavg $\to$ trimmed\_mean$^\ast$ & DEGEN & HIGH & 0.846 & \checkmark \\
\midrule
\multicolumn{5}{l}{\small Overall: 16/18 = 89\%. HIGH direction: 13/15 correct (87\%). Under the tightened C1 the} \\
\multicolumn{5}{l}{\small certified PASS set is 2/2 correct; fg$\to$rfa is reclassified as a synergy false negative.} \\
\multicolumn{5}{l}{\small $^\ddagger$Reclassified from C2-FAIL: by Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance}(a) clipping preserves FoolsGold's} \\
\multicolumn{5}{l}{\small \phantom{$^\ddagger$}similarities exactly, so C2 holds and the pair fails C1. Prediction and outcome unchanged.} \\
\multicolumn{5}{l}{\small $^\S$Pixel arm $n{=}30$ (seeds $47$--$71$ pre-registered at \texttt{de77a7f}), scaling arm $n{=}5$; the pixel} \\
\multicolumn{5}{l}{\small \phantom{$^\S$}arm sets the max, and its interval $[0.412,\,0.514]$ still crosses $0.5$ at $n{=}30$.} \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\textbf{PASS precision: $2$ of the $2$ selected pairs are low-ASR.} That is $100\%$ as a descriptive statistic, and the positive class is two cases, so it resolves the direction and not the rate. Under the tightened per-attack C1 (§\ref{sec:composability_criterion}) the certified in-distribution set is 2 pairs, fg$\to$cm ($0.107$) and rep$\to$cm ($0.463$ at $n{=}30$), both correct; fg$\to$rfa ($0.093$) is no longer certified and is reported below as a false negative of our own criterion. This is the practitioner-relevant result: the criterion's positive predictions are reliable, at the cost of 40\% recall. Overall development-set accuracy: \textbf{16/18 = 89\%}. Two misses, both in the HIGH direction:

\textbf{One certified pair is measured to the threshold, and a pre-registered top-up did not resolve it.} Rep$\to$CM's LOW label rests on its pixel arm, whose $n{=}5$ interval $[0.231,\,0.616]$ crossed $0.5$ with one seed at $0.665$, so we pre-registered seeds $47$--$71$ (\texttt{de77a7f}: seed list, unchanged $0.5$ rule and the three admissible outcomes all fixed before any run) and took that cell to $n{=}30$, the same power as the pair the criterion rejects. It is \textbf{still unresolved}: mean $0.463$, $95\%$ interval $[0.412,\,0.514]$, with $10$ of the $30$ seeds above the threshold. Six times the seeds narrowed the half-width $3.8\times$ ($0.192 \to 0.051$) and moved the mean \emph{up} from $0.423$, toward the threshold rather than away, so the cell is near-threshold rather than merely noisy. The LOW label holds by the frozen mean rule, the pre-registration forbids adding seeds once the outcome is seen, and we therefore stop here and report it unresolved: the certified in-distribution set is $2/2$ correct on the mean, and only fg$\to$cm is certified \emph{and} resolved below threshold. The frozen seed reproduced bit-for-bit before the top-up ran ($0.43178$ at seed $42$), so the $25$ new seeds extend the original five rather than replacing them; the scaling arm stays at $n{=}5$ ($0.020$), where it cannot set the max. Clean accuracy is $0.755$ over the $30$ runs, and $0.50$, $0.68$, $0.76$ on the three cells, all well clear of the collapse regime.

\paragraph{Two boundary conditions, stated alongside the primary result.} Both are developed in full later, but neither should be met late. \textbf{(i) Extreme heterogeneity breaks the PASS pairs: two of three on the raw reading, one of three once our own accuracy floor is applied.} At $\alpha{=}0.1$, fg$\to$rfa reaches $0.569$ and rep$\to$cm $0.548$; only fg$\to$cm survives, at $0.433$. fg$\to$rfa's cell sits at clean accuracy $0.20$, below our $0.35$ floor, so it is \emph{uninterpretable} rather than a crossing and the gated count is $1/3$; both counts are carried, because the floor softens a limitation we state against ourselves and rep$\to$cm's crossing ($0.548$ at $0.48$) survives it either way. What makes this more than a negative datapoint is that the failure appears in the theorem's own quantity: RFA's separation margin $m := \Delta'_{\mathrm{sep}}/[2(R'_B+\delta')]$, measured on the transformed points, collapses from an all-round mean of $3.24$ to $1.01$ (exactly the $m \to 1$ boundary at which Corollary~\ref{cor:aggregate_mass}'s bound degrades to $40\%$ adversarial mass), so a practitioner running the 5--10 round pilot would measure $m \approx 1$ and decline to deploy. We emphasise that this is a post-hoc measurement of a known failure, not a pre-registered prediction of it; full sweep and caveats (bimodality at $n{=}3$, accuracy collapse to $0.20$--$0.48$) in §\ref{sec:oos_pass}(E). \textbf{(ii) Mechanism preservation is not adaptive-attack robustness.} A criterion-aware adversary solving FoolsGold's and RFA's constraints jointly (decorrelated coordinate blocks to evade the similarity test, $\epsilon$-ball projection to stay inside the geometric median's radius) raises fg$\to$rfa from $0.064$ (seed-matched at $n{=}30$, and on the pixel arm the attack is built from) to $0.157$ within our evaluated composition-aware attack family (§\ref{sec:fg_rfa_flagship}). Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} bounds what the preserved mechanism admits, not what an adversary reshaping its update to satisfy both constraints at once can do, and the $2.4\times$ increase is the size of that gap on our flagship pair.

\textbf{Held-out validation (pre-registered): 22/24 = 92\%} (\emph{Screening}: an evaluation ordering, and by Proposition~\ref{prop:identification_full} not an identification of the mechanism's contribution)\textbf{.} Table~\ref{tab:composability_heldout} reports the 24 pre-registered pairs. Combined across both waves: \textbf{38/42 = 90.5\%}. \textbf{Read against its base rate that figure is nearly uninformative, which is why it appears here, where it is computed, and in no summarizing position.} Only 5 of the 42 pairs are low-ASR (the $12\%$ base rate of §\ref{sec:condition_scoring}), so the constant predictor that calls every pair HIGH already scores $37/42 = 88.1\%$; $90.5\%$ is one pair better than that, and the tally is carried by correct negatives. The informative quantities are precision $2/2$ and recall $40\%$ (§\ref{sec:condition_scoring}). The two held-out misses are both LOW-predicted pairs that were flagged as \emph{speculative} in the pre-registration (neither is a clean C1$\wedge$C2$\wedge$C3 PASS):

Only the two LOW predictions are shown individually; the 22 HIGH predictions (all correct) are summarized.}
\label{tab:composability_heldout}
\centering
\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{lccccl}

\midrule
