\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\noindent FG$\to$RFA is the correct choice under a \emph{security-first} objective (e.g., safety-critical FL where any backdoor is unacceptable). For operators with accuracy constraints, FG$\to$CM (acc $0.68$, max ASR $0.107$) buys $37\%$ more accuracy for suppression that is statistically indistinguishable on the worst-case arm the two share (Welch $p = 0.52$ on committed scaling, $0.093$ against $0.107$). It is \emph{not} Pareto-superior and we do not claim it is: its point estimate is the higher of the two, and on the pixel arm, which is neither pair's worst case, the gap does reach significance in FG$\to$RFA's favour ($0.064$ against $0.099$, Welch $p = 0.022$). Rep$\to$CM (acc $0.76$, max ASR $0.463$) offers near-baseline accuracy with moderate security. The criterion certifies FG$\to$CM and Rep$\to$CM; FG$\to$RFA reaches the lowest ASR of the three but fails the tightened per-attack C1 and is therefore a false negative of our own criterion rather than a certified recommendation. The choice among them is an operator-specific utility decision, not a criterion failure.

\section{Related work}
\label{sec:related}
\label{app:related}

\noindent\textbf{The contrast in one sentence.} Prior work asks whether a defense withstands an attack, and the composition literature treats composition quality as an \emph{outcome to predict}. We ask a different question: the \textbf{identifiability of mechanistic explanations} for that outcome, under the evaluation designs commonly used to compare defenses. Concretely, whether an evaluation criterion can causally attribute a composition's security to preservation of the downstream mechanism.

\textbf{Byzantine-robust aggregation, studied in isolation.} Krum~\citep{blanchard2017machine}, Trimmed Mean~\citep{yin2018byzantine}, RFA~\citep{pillutla2022robust} and FoolsGold~\citep{fung2020foolsgold} are each proposed and evaluated as a \emph{single} stage. \textbf{Two names have left that list, and the audit of §\ref{app:audit} is what removed them.} FLAME~\citep{nguyen2022flame} combines three mechanisms in one round (model clustering, weight clipping and adaptive noise), and it gives the constituent-complementarity reason for combining them, clustering and clipping being there to ``minimize the required amount of noise'' a differentially private defense would otherwise need. FLTrust~\citep{cao2021fltrust} applies a server-bootstrapped trust score \emph{and} a magnitude renormalization to the same round, which is two mechanisms under any granularity the audit's distinctness rule admits. \textbf{The FLTrust error was ours to catch one section earlier, and we did not}: §\ref{sec:prospective}'s near-identity finding rests on precisely that two-part structure, since FLTrust gates on the scale-invariant $\cos(u_i, g_{\mathrm{srv}})$ and then renormalizes every update to $\|g_{\mathrm{srv}}\|$, which is why a positive per-client rescaling is exactly cancelled. So this paper described FLTrust as two mechanisms where the finding needed two and as a single stage where the contrast needed one. Both are composed defenses and both are coded as such below. \textbf{Bulyan}~\citep{elmhamdi2018hidden} is the earliest composed defense among those we name and predates the framing here: it runs Krum-style selection and then a trimmed-mean-style aggregation over the selected set, so it is an ordered pair of exactly the kind we study, chosen by its authors rather than by any screen. It falls outside the audit's frame as frozen, and precisely why matters: the frame's conference screen requires \texttt{federated} in the title, which Bulyan's does not carry, and the arXiv sweep runs 2019--2026 (§\ref{app:audit}). Its \texttt{references.bib} entry is added here, \emph{after} the audit was coded, so the audit's first route never saw it, and we report that rather than re-run a frozen instrument: Bulyan is named as related work and is \emph{not} a retrospective audit hit. Every count in §\ref{app:audit} is unchanged. We propose no new defense; we reason about \emph{ordered pairs} of existing ones, which is a different object: the upstream stage's output is the downstream stage's input, so the question is no longer whether a mechanism works but whether it \emph{survives} a transform applied before it. Ensemble adversarial training~\citep{tramer2018ensemble} \emph{assumes} that upstream processing preserves downstream signals; we make that assumption explicit, checkable, and (as it turns out) \textbf{the wrong abstraction for security}.

\textbf{Adaptive attacks are why an inherited-robustness guarantee is not a security guarantee.} Optimized model poisoning defeats Krum, trimmed mean and FoolsGold once the attack is chosen \emph{against} the defense~\citep{shejwalkar2021manipulating,bhagoji2019analyzing}, and the same holds for distributed and durable backdoors~\citep{xie2019dba,wang2020attack,zhang2022neurotoxin}. Our C1 quantifies over a \emph{committed} attack by construction, so this literature bounds what any inherited-suppression criterion (ours included) can promise. Our single whitebox composition-aware stress test (§\ref{sec:fg_rfa_flagship}) is reported in order to \emph{measure} that gap ($0.064 \to 0.157$ seed-matched at $n{=}30$ on the pixel arm, $2.4\times$), not to defend the criterion against it; a screening condition stated against an adaptive adversary is what the framework most lacks.

\textbf{Evaluation methodology, which is where this paper sits.} The adjacent precedent is adversarial robustness, where reported numbers proved unreliable until evaluations were required to be adaptive~\citep{croce2020robustbench}. Our point is methodological in the same way but about \textbf{identification} rather than attack strength. Wherever a screen gates candidate pairs on standalone effectiveness and then reads the composition's outcome as evidence about the \emph{mechanism}, Proposition~\ref{prop:identification_full} shows that inference cannot work at \emph{any} sample size: the counterfactual is excluded by the gate, not undersampled. \textbf{We do not claim this is how the FL literature generally proceeds, and that sentence is now a pre-registered measurement rather than a disclaimer.}

\noindent The rubric of §\ref{app:audit} was frozen at a commit hash before any paper was retrieved, and it screens $622$ retrieved records down to $59$ included composed-defense papers, each coded against five criteria that every code must support with a verbatim quotation and a locator. Outcome-gated attribution, defined there as two or more mechanisms applied jointly to the same round (C-a), paired for their constituents' standalone effectiveness or complementary threat coverage (C-b), validated by the composed system's own adversarial outcome (C-c), carrying an actual mechanism-attribution claim (C-e), and running no identifying contrast (not C-d), holds for \textbf{$6$ of $59$ papers, $10.2\%$}. The rate is $5$ of $59$ if the audit's mechanism-distinctness rule is dropped, and §\ref{app:audit} reports both, because that rule is one of two amendments written after all $59$ rows had been coded. \textbf{A low rate was pre-registered as meaning that the decline above is kept and evidenced rather than replaced}, and that is what we do with it.

\noindent \textbf{The audit's first casualty is the sentence it was built to support, and we report that ahead of anything else it found.} \citet{fenaux2025hammer} was our one named external instance, and it codes C-d $=$ YES on strict grounds rather than by the rubric's benefit-of-the-doubt rule: its Table 3 holds the Type-2 removal stage fixed, varies the upstream Type-1 defense across six choices, and reads the requirement on the pairing off that variation, concluding that success ``ultimately relies on selecting a Type 1 defense restrictive enough to overlap with CSFT's effective $\delta$-range.'' That is the intervention Proposition~\ref{prop:identification_full} asks for, performed on the upstream stage with the downstream mechanism held fixed, so the instance is withdrawn and the audit we ran to support the claim is what withdraws it. \textbf{Nor is such a contrast unusual in the frame}: one is present in \textbf{$46$ of $59$} papers, $78.0\%$, and of the \textbf{$33$} papers that have the outcome-gated \emph{design}, \textbf{$27$} run one. The $6$ are therefore papers that had the design and did not run the contrast, not papers for which the contrast was unavailable or unheard of. The secondary that would have counted against our reading, a paper with the design that makes no attributional claim at all, occurs \textbf{$0$} times in those $33$.

\noindent \textbf{What the frame does lack is narrower, and it is the measurement this paper makes}: no included row varies an upstream stage and asks whether the \emph{ordering} of two downstream arms survives it, with intervals over seeds. The three closest rows are what that gap is made of. \citet{tabassum2026starfl} report an ordering that flips across datasets, ``robust aggregation alone outperforms STAR-FL in terms of ASR'' on FMNIST while ``the full framework provides greater benefits on complex datasets such as CIFAR-10,'' which is this paper's phenomenon observed and left as a remark; the paper reports no seed count, standard deviation or confidence interval anywhere, so there is no interval on either side of the flip. \citet{tory2026geoindist} put both factors on the bench, a graded privacy ladder upstream and four aggregation rules downstream, and decline the cross in one sentence of their own limitations: ``the restriction to coordinate-wise median aggregation limits immediate generalization to other Byzantine-robust rules like Krum or FLTrust.'' \citet{xu2024lasa} come closest to the interventional form, reporting with the filter held fixed that ``the accuracy of Spar$+$Di is higher than that of Di,'' which establishes that the upstream stage helps and not whether it preserves the downstream mechanism's discrimination.

\noindent \textbf{Two further failure modes appear in the frame that Proposition~\ref{prop:identification_full} does not cover, and both are worth naming separately from it.} \citet{kasyap2026provefl} establish the invariance analytically instead of measuring it, deriving that since the ``mask preserves distance ordering, the plaintext post-processing is identical to the classical Krum rule''; their empirical arm has the upstream contrast and the adversarial outcome in one figure and never crosses them, the plaintext condition existing only at zero adversaries. \citet{nath2026cqsa} do not measure a composed outcome at all, and say so: ``We do not simulate FL with Byzantine-robust aggregation schemes explicitly, due to limited space. However, theoretical analysis proved that the statistical relationships are preserved.'' That is a C-c failure rather than a C-d one, and it is a different defect from the one we study. \textbf{The gap is also not one of capability}: \citet{abacha2026fedsurrogate} run an additive component ladder whose final step is exactly an identifying contrast, ``Each component contributes incrementally to MTA, with the rescue stage providing the largest single gain,'' so the design that would answer the question is already ordinary practice and what is absent is the specific quantity. Composition itself is named as an open problem in the field's survey of them~\citep{kairouz2021advances}.

\noindent The claim we do make is structural rather than sociological, and its clearest instance is \emph{our own} screen: Lemma~\ref{lem:testability} shows C0$\wedge$C1 \emph{force} the downstream defense to suppress the attack alone, so the gate is constitutive of an inherited-suppression screen and not an incidental filter on it. Statements of a composition-preservation intuition in that literature are informal; what is new here is the \emph{level hierarchy} at which the intuition fails (Definition~\ref{def:levels_full}) and the class of designs that can test it (§\ref{app:boundary}, §\ref{app:targeted_full}). \textbf{So what makes this a machine-learning contribution and not an application of standard causal-identification theory is a combination of four things, and the three-level form of the argument is in §\ref{sec:testability} rather than repeated here}: a structural identification boundary for a shape of evaluation, the preservation hierarchy that produces the failure, an interventional instantiation in FL that measures it, and the empirical non-implications (P4)$\not\Rightarrow$(P3) and (P4)$\not\Rightarrow$(P5) that the collider argument alone does not predict.

\textbf{Attacks and defenses.} Backdoor attacks~\citep{chen2017targeted,bagdasaryan2020backdoor,wang2020attack,xie2019dba} and robust defenses~\citep{pillutla2022robust,sun2019can,blanchard2017machine} form the landscape. Beyond our norm-based menu, certified robustness~\citep{xie2021crfl} and filtering defenses~\citep{nguyen2022flame,fung2020foolsgold} build stronger single mechanisms; we compose fixed defenses deterministically rather than strengthening a single mechanism, so these are complements to our object rather than baselines for it. Randomized transformations~\citep{xie2018mitigating,liu2018towards} in centralized settings are the conceptual neighbor of a \emph{randomized-menu} question, which this paper does not ask: every claim here concerns deterministic joint application (§\ref{sec:composability_criterion}). \textbf{An earlier version of this paper compared against a randomized menu and reported that deterministic composition dominates it; that comparison is not part of this submission and no claim here rests on it.} That withdrawal stands: the artifact behind it is unpaired and compute-confounded. What replaces it is a separate, pre-registered, compute-matched test of the narrower question (App.~\ref{app:randomization}), whose result is reported there and is not scored against the withdrawn one.

\textbf{Backdoor persistence and durability.} The persistence mechanism we formalize has empirical precursors. \citet{bagdasaryan2020backdoor} design model-scaling explicitly to embed triggers that survive gradient updates; \citet{zhang2022neurotoxin} (Neurotoxin) engineer triggers to occupy ``less-used'' gradient directions, achieving multi-round persistence even under strong norm clipping. These works establish the phenomenon, and this paper \emph{uses} it rather than modelling it: persistence is why a committed attack stays committed across rounds, which is what makes a single committed attack against a fixed defense pair the right setting for an identification question. \textbf{We make no formal claim about persistence.} An earlier version of this paper advanced a $(\gamma, \alpha, p_{\mathrm{eff}})$ model of when persistence eliminates the value of defense switching, together with a heuristic boundary as $\gamma \to 1$; that material is not part of this submission, and we withdraw the claim rather than leave it asserted without the evidence that supported it. What remains is $\gamma$ as a measured descriptive quantity (Appendix~\ref{app:notation}) and the DBA persistence replication of §\ref{app:dba_f06}, which returns an \emph{intermediate} verdict and establishes no boundary.

\textbf{Defense composition and ensembling.} Composing defenses is well-studied in adversarial ML for centralized models: adversarial training combined with input purification~\citep{croce2020robustbench}, detection-then-rejection pipelines, and ensemble adversarial training~\citep{tramer2018ensemble}. These works implicitly require that upstream processing does not destroy downstream signals; our signal-preservation criterion formalizes this requirement explicitly with measurable margins. In FL the closest \emph{structural} precedent is bucketing~\citep{karimireddy2022byzantine}, which restores existing robust aggregators under heterogeneous data by inserting an upstream reshuffling step ahead of them: this is the object we study, an upstream transform feeding a fixed downstream aggregator. Its target is convergence under heterogeneity rather than suppression of a committed attack, so it does not meet Proposition~\ref{prop:identification_full}; what it shows is that the contrast our protocol demands is the natural one for this object, since establishing that an upstream stage helps at all requires comparing the same aggregator with and without it. In Byzantine-tolerant distributed systems, protocol composition (e.g., layered BFT) has a long history; the key distinction is that Byzantine agreement composes for fault tolerance among replicas, while our setting composes for poison resilience across client updates where the adversarial signal is a continuous gradient, not a binary fault. \textbf{A predictive criterion for FL composition does exist in recent work, and we withdraw the priority claim we previously made here.} \citet{fenaux2025hammer} categorize defenses by the deviation of adversarial updates from the mean of the updates and predict that pairing a large-deviation defense with a small-deviation one closes the gap either leaves alone. Our object is prior to theirs and different in kind: not which pairings succeed, but whether a comparison across defenses can identify \emph{why} one did. Proposition~\ref{prop:identification_full} therefore applies to their criterion and to ours alike, and it constrains what validating evidence for such a criterion can establish rather than whether the criterion is correct.

\subsection{The pre-registered composition audit: rubric, frame and the 59 coded papers}
\label{app:audit}

\noindent §\ref{sec:related} reports one rate out of this audit and withdraws one claim because of it. This subsection is the artifact behind both. The rubric is \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak pre\_registration\_literature\_audit.md}, the coder's records are one JSON file per paper under \texttt{results/\allowbreak literature\_audit/\allowbreak coding/}, and every count in this subsection is emitted by \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak analyze\_literature\_audit.py} into \texttt{results/\allowbreak literature\_audit/\allowbreak summary.json}. \textbf{The body of Tables~\ref{tab:audit_rows_a} and~\ref{tab:audit_rows_b} is a file that script writes}, \texttt{results/\allowbreak literature\_audit/\allowbreak table\_rows.tex}, so no code and no count in them is transcribed by hand.

\textbf{Not all of the rubric predates the coding, and that is the first thing to report rather than the last.} Table~\ref{tab:audit_timeline} is the chronology. Pre-registered without qualification, and unchanged from the freeze to now: the primary quantity, the five criteria's text, the four ambiguity rules, the frame and its screening procedure, the ten-paper floor, and the three admissible outcomes. Amendment 1 re-pointed the retrieval endpoint before any paper had been retrieved, and changed no query string, no criterion and no threshold. Amendments 2 through 5 are adjudication rules for questions the frozen criteria underdetermine, and they were written with rows already in hand. \textbf{Exactly one of them moves a reported number.} Under Amendment 4a's mechanism-distinctness rule the primary is $6/59$; without it, $5/59$. The single row that turns on it is \texttt{2601.06466}, whose three-way threshold decision over a Mahalanobis score and a trajectory score is three components under 4a and one under the coarser reading, and both rates are reported everywhere either is.

\textbf{The frame is a procedure, not a reading list.} Three routes in: every \texttt{references.bib} entry that proposes or evaluates an FL poisoning or backdoor defense; five frozen query strings issued against the arXiv search interface, paginated to exhaustion, over 2019--2026; and the complete title listings of USENIX Security, NDSS, IEEE S\&P and PMLR screened by the frozen regex \texttt{federated} AND (\texttt{backdoor} OR \texttt{poison} OR \texttt{byzantine} OR \texttt{robust}). A one-hop snowball through the defense-comparison tables of \citet{nguyen2022flame}, \citet{rieger2022deepsight} and \citet{fenaux2025hammer} closes it, and no second hop is taken. \textbf{Adding a query after seeing results is forbidden by the rubric and none was added.} The bias this realizes is stated in the same breath as the rate: coding requires quotable full text, so the frame is \emph{arXiv-reachable} FL poisoning-defense work, ACM CCS is a coverage gap rather than a sample because its proceedings pages return HTTP 403, \textbf{and CCS is not the only gap}: OpenReview is unscreened, so ICLR and NeurIPS proceedings enter only by the \texttt{references.bib} and snowball routes, as do IEEE venues other than S\&P on IEEE Xplore. A paper whose title carries none of the regex's tokens enters only by those same two routes. \textbf{We do not close these gaps by hand}: the rubric forbids adding a retrieval route after seeing results, and a hand-added paper would void the pre-registration it is supposed to be evidence under, so the gaps are named and left open.

\textbf{The funnel.} $622$ records retrieved, $593$ after deduplication, all $593$ screened on title and abstract, $59$ full texts sought and $59$ obtained, $59$ included, $534$ excluded. Of the exclusions, $362$ are single-mechanism or non-pipeline on their own title and abstract, $71$ are a retrieval artifact in which a bibliography line was captured as a record, $38$ were adjudicated individually on other stated grounds, $24$ name two mechanism families with no joint application, $18$ are retrieval-stage losses with neither an abstract nor a resolvable identifier, $15$ are surveys, reviews or position papers, and $6$ are outside federated learning. Of the $593$ screening decisions, $495$ are mechanical and every one of those is an exclusion; the remaining $98$ were adjudicated by hand, the $59$ inclusions and $39$ further exclusions, because the mechanical rule can only exclude and \emph{no} paper enters the audit without a hand decision. Every exclusion carries its reason string in \texttt{results/\allowbreak literature\_audit/\allowbreak screening.json}, and the analyzer refuses to print any rate if one does not.

\textbf{The criteria, and why the primary is a conjunction with a negated term.} C-a: two or more mechanisms applied jointly to the same round. C-b: the pairing is selected or motivated by the constituents' standalone effectiveness or their complementary threat coverage, and by nothing else. C-c: the composed system's own adversarial outcome, its ASR, backdoor accuracy or robust accuracy, is what validates it. C-d: an identifying contrast, meaning an experiment or derivation that varies the \emph{upstream} stage with the downstream mechanism and the attack held fixed. C-e: a mechanism-attribution claim is actually made. \textbf{The primary is C-a$\wedge$C-b$\wedge$C-c$\wedge$C-e$\wedge\neg$C-d}, and the C-e term is what stops it from measuring ``nobody runs the contrast we invented'', which would be near total and worth nothing. Any UNCLEAR criterion sends the paper's verdict to UNCLEAR, and UNCLEAR papers leave both the numerator and the denominator, so the measured prevalence can only be smaller than a guessing coder's. No row resolved to UNCLEAR.

\textbf{The result, with the secondaries it must be read against.} Primary $6/59 = 10.2\%$, or $5/59$ without Amendment 4a. The outcome-gated \emph{design}, C-a$\wedge$C-b$\wedge$C-c, holds for $33/59 = 55.9\%$. An identifying contrast is \emph{present} in $46/59 = 78.0\%$, and among the $33$ design papers, $27$ run one. \textbf{So the six are papers that had the design and did not run the contrast, not papers for which it was unavailable}, and any sentence implying the contrast is rare in this literature is false against our own audit. The pre-registered secondary that would count against the sharp reading, a design paper making no attributional claim at all, is $0/33$; the one C-e $=$ NO row in the frame, \texttt{1909.05125}, has no design either, and a paper with neither is evidence in neither direction. The six are \texttt{2307.00356}, \texttt{2310.13403}, \texttt{2502.07011}, \texttt{2507.16134}, \texttt{2601.01053} and \texttt{2601.06466}.

\textbf{What the $21$ C-a $=$ NO rows are made of, in three kinds.} First, Amendment 3's purpose test excises constituents whose own stated purpose is privacy, communication efficiency or server verifiability rather than resisting corrupted updates, which is what leaves \texttt{2607.06612} and \texttt{2609.03420} with exactly one defense-purposed mechanism per round. Second, several papers decompose a \emph{single} mechanism into stages that exist to serve it: \texttt{2505.17226}'s median filter repairs an estimate the Krum-derived rule needs, and \texttt{2311.15894}'s four steps are four steps of one rule. Third, \texttt{2302.07173} evaluates its schemes as alternatives to one another, which C-a's own exclusion list names as a menu rather than a composition. The per-row basis is in each record's C-a locator, and the reader who disagrees with a call can see the sentence it rests on.

\textbf{C-b fails on five of the $38$ C-a $=$ YES rows, for four distinct reasons, and the reasons matter more than the count.} \texttt{2508.12978} composes for \emph{guarantee preservation}, proving that adding its compression does not destroy what the aggregator already provides, which is a reason about the composition rather than about the constituents' effectiveness. \texttt{2601.04930} and \texttt{2602.22269} give a real constituent-property reason of the residual-coverage kind, but the surface being closed is one an Amendment-3-excluded constituent creates, so the pairing reason does not connect two counted mechanisms. \texttt{2503.18284} names the deficiencies of \emph{prior} schemes as motivation for its framework and states the integration of its own two stages as a fact and an optimization convenience. \texttt{1909.05125}'s only stated reason is computational and communication efficiency. \textbf{We record these rather than fold them in}: three of the four are pairing reasons of kinds the rubric could plausibly have admitted, and it does not, so C-b is stricter than it might have been in a direction that shrinks the primary.

\textbf{The C-d route field stratifies the $46$ contrasts and decides nothing.} Of them, $21$ are hyperparameter sweeps of an upstream defense parameter with an adversarial outcome read at each setting, $21$ are component contrasts in which an upstream stage is removed, substituted or cumulatively built up, and $4$ are analytic. Precedence when a row satisfies more than one is sweep, then contrast, then analytic, fixed in advance of the backward pass rather than deferred to which finding a paper calls central. \textbf{Amendment 5's backward pass was run across all $46$ rows and relabelled four}, \texttt{2409.01435}, \texttt{2502.00587}, \texttt{2505.10297} and \texttt{2604.03862}, moving the stratification from $25/17/4$ to $21/21/4$; no rate moved, because the field enters none. \textbf{It is a floor and not a census}, since it records only routes a record actually quotes. LASA is the proof: its appendix sweep of the upstream sparsification level, with accuracy under three attacks at every rung and the downstream filter fixed, was in the paper and merely pointed at in the record until the backward pass quoted it.

\textbf{One contrast in the frame is confounded by its own design, and it is coded YES anyway.} \texttt{2609.03420} varies its upstream privacy budget as a graded dose with the downstream median and the attacks fixed, and states the reason: ``This selection isolates privacy-fairness interactions from aggregation-induced variance.'' But the client learning rate is a declared function of that budget, so two things move together along the ladder, and the paper demonstrates that this matters by reporting collapse rather than a graded decline under a static rate. The rubric's fourth ambiguity rule gives C-d the benefit of the doubt, and the code follows the rule rather than our preference: a rule that resolves toward finding the contrast present is a rule that shrinks our own primary.

\textbf{Quotation integrity, and the substitutions this table cannot avoid.} Every quotation in every record is asserted verbatim against the paper's own extracted full text by \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak verify\_quotes\_literature\_audit.py}, which exits non-zero on any failure: $59$ records, $295$ quotations, $334$ spans, all verbatim, with four spans under $40$ characters and two pairs out of source order reported rather than failed, the locator saying in each case that the spans are not one passage. \textbf{The first run of that check over the whole set failed $28$ quotations in $15$ records, and the repair log is committed} at \texttt{results/\allowbreak literature\_audit/\allowbreak quote\_repair\_log.md} with the pre-repair records beside it. \textbf{No code changed in any repair}, and two repairs produced \emph{stronger} evidence for the code already recorded, which is the uncomfortable part: the defective quotations were faithful paraphrases, so a reader checking the argument would not have caught them and only the machine check did. One consequence lands in this subsection. Verbatim spans contain a true minus, an en dash inside a citation range, typographic apostrophes, \texttt{ff} and \texttt{fi} ligatures, Greek and math-italic letters and a set-membership sign, none of which this paper's source may carry, so quoted spans are rendered with a LaTeX equivalent, for instance \texttt{CSFT's} for the typographic apostrophe and $\delta$ for math-italic delta. \textbf{The JSON records, not this appendix, are the authority for what each paper says.}

\textbf{Extraction hazards, named because they are what a re-adjudication will hit first.} \texttt{pdftotext} interleaves the columns of a two-column layout, which spliced a word from the adjacent column into a sentence in \texttt{2503.18284} and produced adjacent-but-unrelated spans in \texttt{2602.16480} and \texttt{2607.06612}; three papers, \texttt{2606.31066}, \texttt{2608.08574} and \texttt{2608.21172}, emit table bodies in column-major order, so a number's row label is not the label above it in the stream; and displayed fractions and page numbers appear mid-sentence. Every affected locator says so. Separately, \texttt{2609.03064} carries a journal line dated 2024 against a 2609 arXiv identifier; we code it under the arXiv year and record the inconsistency rather than choose silently.

\textbf{What this audit does not establish, recorded before the numbers existed and unchanged by them.} It is one coder with no inter-rater statistic, and the mitigation is the quotation rather than a claim of reliability. Four rows were known to us before the freeze and are marked in the tables: coding them is not blind. The rate is about this frame, which is arXiv-reachable, preprint-heavy and 2026-heavy, and it is not an estimate of ``the FL literature''. And the rate carries no claim that the six papers' conclusions are wrong: the audit measures whether an evaluation design can identify the mechanism it attributes to, which is a question about what the evidence licenses and not about whether the defense works.

\section{Limitations, in full}
\label{sec:limitations}

\textbf{(L1) Limited scope and architecture-conditional boundaries.} Three attack families (model\_scaling, pixel, DBA), two architecture families (CifarCNN, ResNet18; plus \texttt{simple\_cnn}, a third CNN configuration and not a third family, on the replicated arm), and one dataset (CIFAR-10) for \emph{every} composition suite without exception; the two arms that leave CIFAR-10, EMNIST-byclass and CIFAR-100, are single-arm intervention ladders and neither carries a composition. The collapse boundary is architecture-conditional (ResNet18 suppression at NC10/rep90: $0.865$ vs.\ CifarCNN $0.097$, a $9\times$ shift; per-seed values in §\ref{app:resnet18_replication_details}), so results at other $N$, $K$ or datasets may differ. \textbf{That gap is also evidence for this paper's own thesis}: a swing of that size under a change of architecture alone is why we state the preservation conditions as mechanisms to be measured per setting rather than as architecture-independent empirical laws. \textbf{The Mode-S replication of §\ref{app:resnet18_dose} does not soften this, and the two are not in tension}: what holds across both architecture families there is the \emph{dissociation}, a large decision disturbance costing no suppression, and not any suppression \emph{level} --- which is precisely the quantity this $9\times$ swing moves. A mechanism can transfer where the number it produces does not, and we claim only the former.

\noindent Cross-distribution transfer is validated across architecture, scale ($N \in \{10,100\}$) and adversarial fraction ($f \in [0.05,0.6]$; §\ref{sec:oos_pass}(D)), and \emph{dataset} transfer is now tested on two arms and no more: the flagship Mode-S arm on EMNIST-byclass with \texttt{simple\_cnn}, where the negative replicates at $\Delta = -0.017$ (§\ref{app:targeted_full}), and the \texttt{coord\_median}/pixel comparability cell on CIFAR-100, where the confounded-versus-controlled sign disagreement replicates, $-0.213$ against $+0.071$ (§\ref{app:sixcell}).

\noindent \textbf{For the screen itself the obstacle turned out to be sharper than an untested transfer: on EMNIST-byclass C1's precondition has no input on the committed pixel arm.} C1 requires that some constituent suppress each committed attack \emph{alone}, and under the identical composition protocol the pixel backdoor is essentially unsuppressed there: re-measured here, \texttt{coord\_median} reaches $0.609$ at clean accuracy $0.836$ (seeds $0.717/0.493/0.618$), and over the seven single-defense pixel cells measured there that $0.609$ is the \emph{minimum}, with \texttt{krum} next at $0.660$ and the other five between $0.988$ and $0.998$ (six \emph{distinct} aggregators, since \texttt{multi\_krum} reduces to \texttt{fedavg} at $K{=}5$). \textbf{So no pair drawn from them can satisfy C1 there}: strategy B certifies nothing over them, every such pair is predicted HIGH, and the strategy comparison of §\ref{sec:strategies} cannot be made on that dataset from these inputs. \textbf{The coverage is exactly those cells, and we state the gap rather than generalize past it}: \texttt{foolsgold}, \texttt{reputation} and the cosine mirrors have no EMNIST-byclass pixel cell, so what is established is C1's failure over the measured menu and \emph{not} its unsatisfiability over the whole one. \textbf{The failure is the pixel arm's, and we do not escape it by dropping the other.} On the model-scaling arm \texttt{krum} does suppress there ($0.027$ at clean accuracy $0.767$), so a scaling-only protocol would supply a C1 input and manufacture the comparison. We decline it for the reason §\ref{sec:prospective} declines the mirror move: the committed protocol requires \emph{each} attack, and choosing the arm that happens to have an input after seeing which does is attack-shopping regardless of the resulting labels. \textbf{On any of these readings it is a scope limit on the criterion's precondition and not a failed replication}: no prediction was made and refuted, and the question a screen answers does not arise where nothing suppresses the attack under test. We report it rather than lower $f$, subset the classes or lengthen training until a positive appeared, each of which is a new design axis chosen after seeing the obstacle. The \texttt{coord\_median} figure is \emph{bit-identical} to the corresponding cell of \texttt{results/femnist/payoff\_results.json} ($0.6091787658\ldots$ at $0.8363207047\ldots$), which by the standard we apply to ourselves in §\ref{app:targeted_full} (the same computation run twice cannot corroborate itself) establishes protocol \emph{identity} and not corroboration; what it does buy is that the matrix's other cells are protocol-comparable, so we did not spend $21$ h re-measuring them (\texttt{experiments/measure\_femnist\_c1\_inputs.py}, \texttt{results/femnist\_c1\_inputs/summary.json}). \textbf{We then screened the scaling arm for a further comparability cell and report the screen rather than a cell} (supplement §\ref{app:cell7}): of the five defenses with no scaling cell there, \texttt{fltrust} is excluded by argument (its admission is bit-identical under this rescaling) and we measured the other four, of which \texttt{foolsgold} and \texttt{cos\_reputation} are the only two that would add a downstream defense and both fail the \emph{accuracy} floor, $10\times$ rescaling collapsing the model to $0.046$ and $0.100$ clean accuracy, so \textbf{no defense outside Table~\ref{tab:sixcell}'s four is admissible on that axis}. \texttt{reputation} does meet the precondition ($0.3465$ at $0.704$), but on seeds $0.9947 / 0.0152 / 0.0296$, and a replication resting on a spread that wide is one we decline to run.

\noindent \emph{Dataset transfer therefore remains untested for every composition suite, both arms that leave CIFAR-10 being single-arm ladders}: the preservation conditions are dataset-agnostic in mechanism (cosine similarity, geometric distance) while the downstream decision boundary may shift with output dimensionality. \emph{The $2\times2$ channel factorial is narrower still}: it is defined only for the two selectors whose decision can be transplanted (\texttt{krum}, \texttt{cos\_krum}) and the harness raises for any other $d_2$, so it is one attack on one aggregator family, reported at $n{=}5$ as \textbf{not decisive} (§\ref{app:factorial}) and acted on only where it withdraws a claim. \emph{Two further gaps in the intervention}: neither DBA nor Neurotoxin was run through it, and the score-only control is a single arm, one aggregator, one attack. \emph{And scale is a prediction, not a measurement}: the \emph{pre-registered} PASS predictions hold at $N{=}100$/ResNet18 (FG$\to$RFA $0.069$) but far from converged accuracy, so they bound nothing about converged training, and \textbf{the FAIL controls run at that scale proved uninformative}, both landing below $0.5$ (§\ref{app:oos_detail}). \emph{What transfers:} the qualitative PASS/FAIL prediction and the mechanism. \emph{What does not:} the exact suppression ASR. A practitioner at $N{=}10^4$ should use the criterion to select candidates and then pilot at their target scale: it identifies \emph{what to try}, and architecture-specific calibration determines \emph{how well it works}.

\textbf{(L2) The composability criterion is an evaluation-prioritization rule, not a security screen and not a discovery tool.} \textbf{It has no established predictive validity}: it orders candidates for evaluation, and the pooled model that would let it predict is unconfirmed ($R^2{=}0.239$) with its cross-arm ordering refuted (§\ref{app:targeted_full}). The criterion gives \emph{sufficient} conditions (C1$\wedge$C2, with C3 as a margin diagnostic): it reliably identifies mechanism-preserving compositions in the common case where one defense is known-effective, but it cannot certify synergistic compositions where no single defense suppresses the attack alone. The conservative bias is by design: false negatives are operationally cheap to recover from (try the composition anyway) while false positives would deploy an insecure stack. Accuracy is 16/18 $=$ 89\% on the development set, 22/24 $=$ 92\% on the pre-registered held-out set (90.5\% combined 2-way) and 9/10 $=$ 90\% on pre-registered 3-way triples (supplement §\ref{sec:three_way}). \textbf{None of those tallies should be read as discrimination}: only 5 of the 42 two-way pairs are low-ASR, so a constant HIGH call scores $37/42 = 88.1\%$ and the combined $90.5\%$ beats it by one pair. Precision ($2/2$) and recall ($40\%$) are the quantities that carry information, and they are why we present the criterion as a prioritizer. All clean C1$\wedge$C2$\wedge$C3 PASS predictions are correct; the four misses are either the predicted intermediate behavior of rank-based aggregators (Proposition~\ref{prop:rank_invariance}: dev-set NC$\to$cm, rfa$\to$cm; held-out fedavg$\to$cm) or one speculative LOW call the criterion does not license (held-out fg$\to$NC, a C1 failure). Formalization is partial: Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight} proves C2$\wedge$C3 for the weighting$\to$rank/geometric class with explicit margins, the remaining families are empirically validated, and C3's threshold condition depends on $d_2$'s discrimination boundary, which in practice requires calibration runs.

\textbf{(L3) Per-round aggregator switching only.} We test per-round defense selection. Randomized \emph{internals} (DP noise, subsampling smoothing à la CRFL, random participation masks) modify the embedding mechanism directly and may retain value even under persistence. Three adaptive attacks are tested against composition: projection-based against rep$+$tm (ASR saturating at $0.639$ for $\epsilon \geq 2$, $0.597$ at $\epsilon{=}1$; the same attack against fg$\to$rfa reaches only $0.079$, Table~\ref{tab:fg_rfa_flagship}), Neurotoxin-style~\citep{zhang2022neurotoxin} top-$k$ gradient projection, and a two-phase consensus-shifting attack against rep$+$tm that biases updates toward the target at rate $r$ for 25 rounds before injecting full backdoor updates (App.~\ref{app:secondary}). At shift\_rate $\in \{0.01,0.05\}$: mean ASR $0.684 \pm 0.138$ ($n{=}10$) and $0.667 \pm 0.137$ ($n{=}8$), comparable to committed\_pixel ($0.679$) and \emph{below} the rep30/tm70 temporal mix's $0.915$. \textbf{Those last two are not the same metric, and the comparison is the weaker one because of it:} $0.679$ and the two shift-rate means are single committed attacks against the composition, whereas $0.915$ is the mix's full-information reading over both attacks, which no single attack can be expected to match. What the row supports is that the two-phase attack does not exceed the mix, not a calibrated gap between them. At $f{=}0.2$ the honest $80\%$ anchor the coordinate-wise median, limiting Phase-1 drift to ${\sim}r/(1{-}f)$ per round, so Phase 2 degenerates to standard committed\_pixel: a mechanism that weakens monotonically with $f$, and at $r \leq 0.05$ over $T \geq 200$ rounds cumulative drift may eventually overcome it. The robustness claim is therefore specific to the tested regime ($f{=}0.2$, $T{=}50$).

\textbf{(L4) The falsifiable prospective positive evidence is one cell, and C0 was added retrospectively.} This is the sharpest limitation we can state about our own design, and we state it rather than let a reader find it. Until §\ref{app:census} there was none at all, and one cell is what changed. Of the prospective suite's 8 predicted-LOW pairs (§\ref{sec:prospective}), 6 are $\cdot{\to}$FLTrust near-\emph{identities}: FLTrust gates on a scale-invariant cosine and renormalizes every update to the server-gradient norm, discarding exactly the magnitude information an upstream positive rescaling modifies (verified bit-identical per seed for pass-through and non-clipping $d_1$), so Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance}(a) permits no other outcome and these confirmations cannot falsify it; the other 2 have FLTrust as $d_1$, where suppression is inherited and C0 puts them out of scope. C0 was itself formulated \emph{after} seeing this suite, so its 7 exclusions are retrospective. The three matched contrasts that appeared to probe Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance} most directly (a $19$--$20\times$ ASR gap at fixed $d_1$) are \textbf{withdrawn}: two ran against MultiKrum, which at $K{=}5$ selects all five participants and is bit-identical to FedAvg, and the genuine selector leaves a gap that measures C1 failure rather than disturbed invariance (§\ref{app:mkrum}).

\noindent \textbf{The decisive experiment does not exist, and that is structural.} We had named it as a downstream defense with a rescaling-invariant admission signal that is \emph{weak in isolation}; Lemma~\ref{lem:testability} shows no such pair can be certified in scope. §\ref{sec:metric_swap} runs the achievable substitute, holding the suppression \emph{principle} fixed while varying only the invariance class, and it did not deliver a positive test either, standalone effectiveness being unholdable here because the magnitude information the mirrors discard is what the originals use to suppress a magnitude attack ($8/8$ frozen labels, TP${}=0$). So the strongest C2-specific evidence is the \emph{post-hoc} within-defense contrast of §\ref{sec:metric_swap}, which holds C1 exactly fixed but was not pre-registered and is resolved on only one of its two cells at $n{=}3$.

\noindent \textbf{Three pre-registered attempts followed, and each refuted something of ours.} §\ref{app:ladder} delivers exactly one falsifiable prospective positive (arm 2, reputation under model-scaling, $0.017 \to 0.779$ at $p = 1.7\times10^{-6}$ with clean accuracy nearly unchanged) while refuting two of its own four predictions and its pooled prediction, leaving the \emph{destruction} half of C2's mechanism confirmed and the preservation half's control indeterminate (§\ref{sec:dose_outcome}). The targeted intervention of §\ref{app:targeted_full} closes the attenuation channel by construction and isolates C2 within a single defense: substantial disturbance of the statistic does not change suppression \emph{on the adjudicating Krum arm under Mode~S}, with admission the channel the evidence points to there, and it \emph{refutes} the preservation-half reading, the exactly-invariant \texttt{cos\_krum} neither suppressing nor holding ASR fixed. The third was designed to be the falsifiable prospective \emph{positive}: a replication arm chosen because the statistic and admission readings order its rise \emph{oppositely}, frozen in git before the arm ran. It replicated the negative in a different invariance class and \textbf{refuted the positive ordering}, the arm with the largest measured admission change rising \emph{less} ($+0.098$ at $n{=}5$) than one whose admission change was smaller.

\noindent \textbf{The census of §\ref{app:census} is the one place this changed, and it changed by exactly one cell.} We closed the $3\times3$ region where Prop.~\ref{prop:invariance}(a) settles C2 algebraically, registering its two unspent cells at \texttt{96f8856}; the single certified pair was registered LOW with \emph{low} confidence and an explicit \texttt{uncertain} flag, and measured LOW at $0.263$. Unlike every predicted-LOW pair above it is neither a near-identity ($0$ of $3$ seeds identical to $d_2$ alone, on both arms) nor an inherited suppression, since \texttt{reputation} holds only model-scaling and \texttt{cos\_krum} only the pixel backdoor. \textbf{That is a falsifiable prospective positive, and it is one cell at $n{=}3$ with a per-seed spread of $0.000$ to $0.681$}: it establishes that the criterion's precision survives a blind test in the one region our invariance result decides exactly, and nothing quantitative. A validated \emph{positive} predictor therefore remains absent: the pooled two-channel model is unconfirmed over $26$ cells ($R^2 = 0.239$) and its cross-arm ordering refuted outright, so admission is \emph{more causally proximal} than the statistic without being a quantitative predictor.

\textbf{(L5) Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}'s conditions need a pilot, and (1)'s is not operationally checkable as stated.} Neither $\rho < r^\star$ nor $\Delta'_{\mathrm{sep}} > 2(R'_B + \delta')$ is readable off a $(d_1,d_2)$ definition pair: both need a pilot measurement. \textbf{So two kinds of cheapness have to be kept apart, and the paper has previously run them together.} Prop.~\ref{prop:invariance}'s classes are \emph{algebraically} checkable: (a) and (c) are decided from the definitions of $d_1$ and $d_2$ alone, with no data, no runtime cost and no per-deployment work. Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}'s conditions are closed-form inequalities and are \emph{still} none of those things, because their terms have to be instantiated by measurement on the deployment they are being checked for. \textbf{Algebraically checkable does not imply deployment-time cheap}, and only the first of the two is something this paper delivers. Regime~A is worse than that. Its ordering-survival condition is $\rho < r^\star$ with $r^\star$ the attack's coordinate extremeness against the benign scale. Building the dose--response ladder (§\ref{app:ladder}) required us to \emph{measure} $r^\star$ rather than stipulate it, and it is not a stable per-attack constant: on model-scaling it ranges $0.00$--$12.59$ across the 12 measured rounds that sampled an adversary (median $0.57$), on the pixel backdoor $0.61$--$1.63$ (median $0.92$). An illustrative $r^\star{=}10$ is a stipulation, not a measurement, and §\ref{sec:restricted_theorem} stipulates no value for $r^\star$ at all: the theorem states the condition symbolically. Two consequences we state rather than work around. First, a practitioner cannot check $\rho < r^\star$ from a single round: the quantity swings by orders of magnitude round to round, and on both committed attacks its median is below $1$, so Regime~A's condition fails even at the identity rung under this estimator. Second, we therefore built \emph{no} prediction in §\ref{app:ladder} on $r^\star$, resting the ladder on directly measured disturbance of $d_2$'s statistic instead. The margin form of the theorem remains valid as a statement about $\rho$ and $r^\star$; what is not established is that $r^\star$ admits a stable estimator at the round granularity a deployment would have to check it at, and a usable Regime~A needs an $r^\star$ estimator with characterized variance, which we do not provide. \textbf{And where the geometric condition \emph{is} measurable it stops being informative in the regime that matters most}: the margin it rests on is exactly what heterogeneity closes, RFA's all-round mean falling from $3.24$ to $1.01$ at Dirichlet $\alpha{=}0.1$, the limit at which Corollary~\ref{cor:aggregate_mass} degrades to $n_a/(n_b{+}n_a)$ and certifies nothing (§\ref{sec:restricted_theorem}, §\ref{sec:oos_pass}(E)). So under extreme heterogeneity the conditions are not merely awkward to check but empty, which is the sense in which §\ref{sec:framework} calls the bound vacuous there.

\textbf{(L6) Measured disturbance is not a transferable predictor, and our dose instrument varies the wrong quantity for three of its four arms.} The pre-registered ladder (§\ref{sec:dose_outcome}) refutes the pooled claim it was built to test ($\rho_s = +0.351$, $p = 0.091$) and refutes two of four per-arm shapes, with ASR \emph{falling} as the dose rises in three arms. Two distinct faults, both ours. First, the abscissa: at matched measured disturbance the real upstream transforms sit up to $+0.79$ ASR above the synthetic curve, and Krum's within-margin arm does not reproduce the very contrast (§\ref{sec:metric_swap}) we attributed to disturbance of its statistic, so the rate at which a transform flips a defense's decision is not a sufficient statistic for what the transform does: \emph{which} clients are reweighted matters, and $\rho$ does not measure that. We now measure the quantity that does, $\Delta_c$ (the supplement §\ref{app:delta_c}): the transforms carrying the two anchors that miss are targeted far beyond any ladder rung ($-1.21$, $-0.72$ against the ladder's $-0.34$) at $\rho$ that is $3.7$ and $13\times$ smaller. That names the axis of the mismatch without supplying a replacement predictor: $\Delta_c$'s magnitude does not order suppression either. Second, the instrument: mean-1 normalization fixes the aggregate's scale but not any client's relative weight, so with the dose assigned independently of adversary status a large $\rho$ mostly dilutes whichever client carries the poison (measured: the ladder's $\Delta_c$ drifts monotonically negative with the dose, $0.000 \to -0.140 \to -0.240 \to -0.336$), and three arms end up measuring Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}'s attenuation regime rather than C2 disturbance. The adversary-targeted dose that separates the two, recorded as not run before the freeze (caveat (iii), the supplement §\ref{app:dose_caveats}), is run in §\ref{app:targeted_full}, and in retrospect Mode~S is the \textbf{$\Delta_c$-pinned} instrument ($\Delta_c$ exactly $0$ at every rung, $\rho$ up to $54.6$) and Mode~A the \textbf{$\Delta_c$-swept} one ($-1.14$ to $+2.47$): with every adversary's weight pinned, Krum's statistic is disturbed in up to $80\%$ of rounds while the admission of adversarial input does not move and ASR does not rise past the pre-registered practical-equivalence margin, whose lower leg ASR's own floor satisfies here so that only the upper leg is a test (App.~\ref{par:reach}), so \emph{which} clients are reweighted, not how often a decision flips, is the distinction the two readings turn on in this arm, and the ladder confound is \emph{explained} (reputation's rise shrinks from $+0.762$ to $+0.178$ once the adversarial share is held fixed). That isolates C2's causal content within a single defense; it does not rescue the pooled positive direction ($R^2 = 0.239$ over $26$ cells, cross-arm ordering refuted by the replication).

\noindent Table~\ref{tab:evidence} is the ledger; the rest of this section is each row's provenance.

\textbf{Proved:} the invariance classes of Proposition~\ref{prop:invariance}; sufficient conditions for mechanism preservation on the weighting$\to$aggregator class (Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}, Lemma~\ref{lem:annihilation}, Corollary~\ref{cor:aggregate_mass}); the identification boundary for outcome-gated screening (Proposition~\ref{prop:identification_full}, with Lemma~\ref{lem:testability} its FL instance and Corollary~\ref{cor:within_design} the within-defense design it licenses); the recall bound on inherited-suppression screens (Proposition~\ref{prop:emergent_full}, whose cap reaches $0$ on a menu whose suppression is emergent throughout, Corollary~\ref{cor:zero_recall}); and, formalizing the interventions, statistic-level non-identifiability (Proposition~\ref{prop:nonidentifiability_full}) and the independent controllability of $\rho$ and $\Delta_c$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:rho_deltac}). \textbf{The two boundary results are general} (they are statements about the shape of a screen, not about FL), so what carries beyond this paper is the design they license, not the screen. \textbf{Empirically supported:} \textbf{that choosing between the two admissible designs sets the inferred direction and not merely its precision}: on \texttt{coord\_median} under the pixel backdoor at $n{=}20$ the outcome-gated design moves $-0.273$ $[-0.334,-0.213]$ where the within-defense design moves $+0.125$ $[+0.095,+0.155]$, disjoint and both excluding zero, and the reversal replicates on CIFAR-100 at $n{=}5$, with $\Lambda_a$ moving in both designs so that no $p$-value attaches to the difference between them (§\ref{app:targeted_full}, Table~\ref{tab:sixcell}); that substantial disturbance of the downstream statistic need not produce a corresponding change in suppression when the adversarial coefficient share is held fixed, with adversarial admission the channel the evidence favours, \emph{on the adjudicating Krum arm under Mode~S} and, by pre-registered replication, on a downstream defense in a \emph{different} invariance class, on a \emph{second dataset and a differently configured CNN}, and on a \emph{second architecture family} with architecture varying alone (§\ref{app:targeted_full}); that the \emph{size} of that effect is not constant across cells, $-0.010$ on the adjudicating arm against $-0.2857$ on a second Krum cell, one where both margin legs bind and baseline admission sits off its floor rather than on it, an arm measuring ASR alone, so that neither cell generalizes to the other and no statement of the form \emph{the dissociation holds generally} is licensed by it (App.~\ref{app:pixel_headroom}); that the screen behaves as a conservative prioritizer across 42 pairs; and that in the one region where Prop.~\ref{prop:invariance}(a) settles preservation \emph{exactly}, a closed $9$-cell census whose two unspent cells were registered blind contains a single low-ASR composition and it is the one the criterion certifies, at precision $1/1$ and recall $1/1$ for a tenth of the runs against statistic preservation's $11\%$ (§\ref{app:census}, one cell at $n{=}3$, gated reading). Those two $100\%$s are contingent on the frozen seed count twice over, and disclosure \emph{(v)} of §\ref{app:census} states the second contingency: the C1 input that certifies the pair reads $0.300$ over seeds $42$--$44$ and climbs with the seed count, to $0.490$ at $n{=}5$ (still below the threshold, by $0.010$) and to $0.584$ at $n{=}8$, where C1 fails and the criterion certifies nothing in that census. \textbf{Not established:} the positive predictive direction of the criterion, since the pooled two-channel model is unconfirmed over $26$ cells ($R^2{=}0.239$) and the replication refutes its cross-arm admission ordering, so admission is a direction and not a quantitative predictor; any ability to discover emergent synergy; and generalization beyond star-topology FL, or beyond CIFAR-10 for anything but the two replicated arms, EMNIST-byclass and CIFAR-100, neither of which carries a composition suite; on the EMNIST arm no single defense measured suppresses the committed pixel backdoor alone, so C1's per-attack precondition fails over the menu measured there and the strategy comparison is out of scope there rather than merely untested ((L1), which also states what the seven do \emph{not} cover). \textbf{The open problem}, which the sections above refer to by pointing here: every reading of (P4) in this paper comes from an instrument that knows which clients are adversarial, and no deployed defense knows that, so whether the adversarial contribution a downstream mechanism admits can be estimated \emph{without} adversary identity is open. Nothing here bounds it, and the design we license identifies that channel rather than screening on it.

\section{The preservation chain in one figure}
\label{app:story}

\textbf{The lower arc, and why the last two links must be broken by intervening rather than comparing.} $T$ can reach ASR by attenuating whichever client carries the poison (Lem.~\ref{lem:annihilation}), \textbf{without passing through $d_2$'s statistic at all}. Mode~S cuts that arc by construction, pinning every adversary at $c{=}1$. \textbf{The three numbers annotating Figure~\ref{fig:modeS}(a), which Figure~\ref{fig:story} redraws without panels, are three scopes, not one experiment}: Krum's flip rate; the admission support \emph{pooled} over Table~\ref{tab:channels}'s four arms, so it names no arm and Krum's own zero is a floor; and \texttt{cos\_krum}/pixel at its first gate-passing rung (§\ref{sec:targeted_rest}).

\section{Notation}
\label{app:notation}

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}ll@{}}
\toprule
symbol & meaning \\
\midrule
$c_i$ & upstream coefficient applied to client $i$'s update, $T(u_i) = c_iu_i$, $c_i>0$ \\
$A$ & the set of adversarial participants in a round \\
$\rho$ & statistic-level dispersion, $\max_i c_i/\min_i c_i$: \emph{how far apart} the coefficients are \\
$\Delta_c$ & adversary-correlated dispersion, $\mathbb{E}[c_i \mid i \in A]-\mathbb{E}[c_i \mid i \notin A]$: \emph{who} receives them \\
$r$ & adversary-to-benign weight ratio, $w_a/\max_b w_b$; the Mode-A dial, $r = e^{\nu}$ \\
$r^\star$ & the attack's coordinate extremeness, $\min_k\min_a\min_b |u_{a,k}|/|u_{b,k}|$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:bounded_reweight}(1)) \\
$S$ & the downstream statistic $d_2$ reads, as in $S(T(U))$ (§\ref{sec:general_form}) \\
$m$ & RFA separation margin, $\Delta'_{\mathrm{sep}}/2(R'_B{+}\delta')$ \\
$\Lambda_a$ & aggregate adversarial influence, instantiated per aggregator: Weiszfeld/reputation \\
 & weight share, selected-coordinate share, or the selection indicator \\
$\kappa$ & the Mode-S / ladder dispersion dial, $\rho = e^{2\kappa}$ \\
$\gamma$ & attack \emph{persistence}, $\mathrm{ASR}(t{+}1)/\mathrm{ASR}(t)$ after a clean round ($\approx 0.66$); never a weight \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}



\section{Equivalence testing at the existing seed counts: where the frozen margin passes and where it only appears to}
\label{app:tost}

\textbf{Why this is here and what it is not.} The pre-registered rule scores a \emph{point} estimate
against a margin: $|\mathrm{mean}\,\mathrm{ASR}(\kappa{=}2) - \mathrm{mean}\,\mathrm{ASR}(\kappa{=}0)| <
0.15$ and every rung $< 0.5$. A point estimate inside a margin is consistent with a negligible effect
\emph{and} with an effect the sample cannot locate, and those are different claims. Table~\ref{tab:tost}
scores the same frozen margin as a two-one-sided-$t$ (TOST) equivalence test on the \emph{paired}
per-seed differences, declaring equivalence only when the $90\%$ interval lies inside $(-0.15,+0.15)$.
It is \textbf{post-hoc}, reads only frozen artifacts, \textbf{adds no runs and revises no published
verdict}; the emitter is \texttt{experiments/analyze\_tost\_existing.py}. Pairing is not a modelling
choice: every rung of an arm runs at the same seeds, so each difference holds one data partition fixed
and the seed-to-seed spread that dominates this suite drops out of the contrast.

\paragraph{Which leg is a test: on most of these arms ASR's own floor satisfies the lower one before
any run.}
\label{par:reach}
Equivalence at a symmetric margin is two claims at once, that the effect does not rise past $+0.15$ and
that it does not fall past $-0.15$, and on most of these arms the second one cannot fail. ASR is bounded
below by $0$, so for \emph{any} dosed outcome whatsoever, a perfect defense included, the paired mean
obeys $\mathrm{mean}_i(\Delta_i) \ge -\,\mathrm{mean}_i \mathrm{ASR}^{\mathrm{id}}_i$: the largest fall
arithmetically available on a cell is its identity rung's own mean ASR. \textbf{Where that mean is below
the margin, the lower leg is satisfied before a single run happens.} It is not a test, and the honest
form of the claim is a pre-registered \emph{non-increase} reported with its $95\%$ upper bound. Where the
identity mean exceeds the margin both legs are tests, the two-sided reading is earned, and those arms
keep it. Table~\ref{tab:reach} scores every arm of Table~\ref{tab:tost} on this, and §\ref{app:targeted_full}
already states the same arithmetic for one arm's ladder branch, in the same words (\emph{arithmetically
unreachable}, at an identity rung of $0.062$); what is added here is that it holds on nearly every arm
this paper reads an equivalence off, which we had not said. \textbf{The one Mode~S arm on which the
lower leg \emph{is} a test reports a fall that leaves the margin} (§\ref{app:pixel_headroom}, identity
mean $0.3232$, $\Delta = -0.286$): the two-sided reading fails there in the direction of more
suppression while the one-sided bound on a rise holds at $-0.087$, which is why the distinction drawn
here is substantive rather than a change of wording.

\noindent \textbf{Which leg sets the published $p$, and the answer is the wrong one.} TOST reports
$\max(p_{\mathrm{lower}},p_{\mathrm{upper}})$, so exactly one leg sets the number each row prints, and on
$5$ of the $6$ arms this table marks equivalent that maximum is attained by the leg ASR's floor has
already settled. The flagship's printed $p{=}0.0014$ is its \emph{lower} leg's; the $p$ for the claim
actually being made, that suppression does not meaningfully \emph{rise}, is $0.0004$. The same holds for
the score-only control ($0.0071$ printed, $0.0024$ one-sided), the EMNIST-byclass row ($0.0004$,
$0.0003$) and ResNet18 ($0.0404$, $0.0143$). \textbf{So the relabelling reports a stronger number than
the one already published on each of those arms, not a weaker one}, and no published $p$ is revised
anywhere: $p_{\mathrm{tost}}$ stays exactly as printed and the upper-leg $p$ is reported beside it as the
separate quantity it is. The one equivalent arm whose $p$ is set by the leg that \emph{is} a test is the
CIFAR-100 row, at an identity mean of $0.703$.

\noindent \textbf{Beyond this table, and the count that may be quoted.} Three further arms carry an
equivalence reading and are scored by the same emitter. The body's $n{=}20$ top-up of row~1 has identity
mean $0.044$ and upper bound $+0.012$; Mode~M, the coordinate-masking class of §\ref{app:factorial}, has
$0.053$ and $+0.019$; the $200$-round ResNet18 replication has $0.045$ and $+0.138$. All three have an
arithmetic lower leg. Counted over \emph{distinct arms} rather than rows, because the flagship and its
EMNIST replication are each scored in more than one place at more than one seed block, the paper makes
equivalence readings about $5$ arms and \textbf{$4$ of the $5$ have an arithmetically satisfied lower
leg}. The fifth, \texttt{coord\_median} / scaling under the outcome-gated design (identity mean $0.557$,
$95\%$ CI $[+0.023,+0.102]$, $n{=}20$), keeps a genuinely two-sided reading and is reported as one;
after its seed top-up that interval is inside the margin \emph{and} excludes zero, which separates the
margin reading from a reading of no effect on this arm. So
\emph{every} equivalence claim is not the right quantifier and is not used. \textbf{The frozen
point-estimate rule has the same arithmetic on its lower side}: $\Delta > -0.15$ is free whenever the
identity mean is below $0.15$, so a sentence reading $|\Delta| < 0.15$ off one of these arms inherits the
same one-sidedness. \textbf{Nothing here re-margins, re-tests or withdraws a published interval}; the
emitter is \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak measure\_\allowbreak margin\_\allowbreak reachability.py},
which reads frozen artifacts only, recomputes every interval with the estimator its own table used, and
asserts each one equal to the published value before it reports anything about that arm's legs
(\texttt{results/\allowbreak margin\_\allowbreak reachability.json}).
\textbf{The fifth arm's $n{=}20$ figures above are the one exception to that provenance, and not to the
claim}: that artifact predates this arm's top-up and still carries its identity mean at $0.519$ on
$n{=}5$, so the $0.557$ and the interval quoted beside it are read from
\texttt{results/\allowbreak coordmedian\_\allowbreak scaling\_\allowbreak topup/\allowbreak summary.json}
instead, and no frozen value is rewritten to agree with them.

\begin{table}[t]
\centering\small\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}
\caption{\textbf{Is the lower TOST leg a test?} For each arm of Table~\ref{tab:tost}: the identity
rung's mean ASR, which is the largest fall arithmetically available because ASR cannot go below $0$;
whether that exceeds the frozen $0.15$ and so makes the lower leg a test; the $95\%$ upper bound, which
is what the one-sided reading is reported with; and the two legs' own $p$ values, whose \emph{maximum} is
the $p$ Table~\ref{tab:tost} prints. \textbf{A ``no'' in the third column is not a criticism of the
arm's result; it says that one of the two claims the margin makes was never at risk on that cell}, and
the row's verdict is therefore reported as a non-increase with its upper bound. Read-only over frozen
artifacts, no runs added, no published interval or verdict revised. Emitted by
\texttt{experiments/\allowbreak measure\_\allowbreak margin\_\allowbreak reachability.py}.}
\label{tab:reach}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lrrcrrr@{}}
\toprule
Arm & $n$ & identity mean & lower leg a test? & $95\%$ upper & $p_{\mathrm{lower}}$ & $p_{\mathrm{upper}}$ \\
\midrule
Krum / scaling (flagship) & $5$ & $0.062$ & no & $+0.026$ & $0.0014$ & $0.0004$ \\
Krum / scaling (score-only) & $5$ & $0.062$ & no & $+0.061$ & $0.0071$ & $0.0024$ \\
Krum / scaling (EMNIST-byclass) & $3$ & $0.027$ & no & $-0.001$ & $0.0004$ & $0.0003$ \\

% their parent arm; the emitter prints ARMS order. Every number in every row is the emitted one.
Krum / scaling (ResNet18) & $3$ & $0.071$ & no & $+0.101$ & $0.0404$ & $0.0143$ \\
Reputation / scaling & $5$ & $0.017$ & no & $+0.469$ & $0.0175$ & $0.6002$ \\
\texttt{cos\_krum} / pixel & $8$ & $0.584$ & \textbf{yes} & $-0.118$ & $0.9639$ & $0.0015$ \\
\texttt{coord\_median} / pixel & $5$ & $0.443$ & \textbf{yes} & $+0.178$ & $0.0005$ & $0.0734$ \\

\textbf{What it shows, including against us.} \textbf{The flagship passes}, and passes at $n{=}5$:
$p{=}0.0014$, the pairing holding $\mathrm{sd}$ to $0.042$, so \emph{the equivalence claim on the
adjudicating arm does not depend on a larger seed count}. On this arm that $p$ is set by the margin's
\emph{lower} leg, which ASR's own floor satisfies before any run: the one-sided $p$ for the claim actually
made, that suppression does not weaken, is the smaller $0.0004$. Table~\ref{tab:reach} prints both legs for
every row of this table and revises no published $p$. So do the score-only control ($p{=}0.0071$)
and the EMNIST-byclass replication ($p{=}0.0004$, $n{=}3$, $\mathrm{sd}{=}0.007$, and $p<0.0001$ at
the pooled $n{=}5$ of §\ref{app:targeted_full}, where the interval widens across zero but stays well
inside the margin). \textbf{The ResNet18 replication passes as well, and it is the closest call in the
table}: $p{=}0.040$ at $n{=}3$ on two endpoint rungs, its $90\%$ interval reaching $-0.137$ against the
$-0.15$ boundary. \textbf{Its $95\%$ interval, $[-0.183,+0.101]$, is wider than the margin}, so that arm's
equivalence is established at the frozen $\pm0.15$ and at no margin below $0.137$; §\ref{app:resnet18_dose}
discloses this beside its pre-registered point-estimate verdict, and §\ref{app:sensitivity}'s ladder
statement is scoped to the comparability arms for exactly this reason. The two arms whose
suppression genuinely moves correctly \textbf{fail} it, which is the positive control working:
reputation ($+0.178$) and \texttt{cos\_krum} ($-0.425$). \textbf{And one arm passes the frozen rule
while failing the test}: \texttt{coord\_median}'s $+0.098$ ($n{=}5$) sits inside $\pm0.15$, but its $90\%$
interval reaches $+0.160$ and TOST gives $p{=}0.073$. We therefore report that arm's equivalence as
\textbf{not established} rather than as inherited from the point estimate. \textbf{This is the one place
the paper's frozen scoring rule was more permissive than the evidence warrants, and we say so rather
than leave the width implicit.} \texttt{cos\_krum} additionally sits above the $0.5$ ceiling the frozen
rule conjoins with the margin ($0.584$ at the identity), which the body already states as its never
suppressing.

\noindent \textbf{Two arms are equivalent and detected at once, and the table marks them.} The
EMNIST-byclass row at the frozen $n{=}3$ and the CIFAR-100 row of §\ref{app:sixcell} both have $90\%$
intervals inside the margin \emph{and} $95\%$ intervals excluding zero: a real effect, smaller than the
margin. Nothing is inconsistent here, but a bare ``equivalent'' on such a row would be read as ``no
effect'', so both carry $^{\dagger}$. The two rows differ in one further way, which Table~\ref{tab:reach} scores: the
EMNIST-byclass row's identity-rung mean ASR is $0.027$, so its lower leg is satisfied by arithmetic and its
only test is the upper one, which bounds a rise at $-0.001$; the CIFAR-100 row's identity mean is $0.703$, so
both of its legs are genuine tests and it is the one row \emph{marked equivalent} here whose two-sided
reading is earned.
\textbf{The CIFAR-100 row is the sharper case}: §\ref{app:sixcell}
reports it as a \emph{rise} of $+0.071$ against the confounded design's fall of $-0.213$, and that
reading stands. What this row adds is its size, under half the frozen margin, which is why the
sign reversal there is reported as a reversal of \emph{direction} and never as a large controlled
effect. \textbf{This arm is scored here and excluded from the binding-arm search of §\ref{app:sensitivity}},
on the separate ground that a margin-sensitivity analysis may only rest on arms about which the paper
makes an equivalence claim, and it makes none about this one.

So the margin is deliberately generous and \textbf{the conjunction is what stops a generous margin from certifying a high-ASR arm}; on the flagship cell every rung's \emph{mean} in fact sits below $0.07$, the quantity the frozen rule scores.
