\section{Composition and Adaptive-Attack Details}
\label{app:composition_adaptive}
\label{app:secondary}

\subsection{Composition Baseline Details}
\label{app:composition}

Simultaneous composition (NormClip $\tau{=}5$ then reputation every round), $N{=}10$, $K{=}5$, $f{=}0.2$, 50 rounds, cifar\_cnn, 5 seeds. Per-seed results:
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{l|cc|cc}
\toprule
 & \multicolumn{2}{c|}{model\_scaling} & \multicolumn{2}{c}{backdoor\_pixel} \\
Seed & Accuracy & ASR & Accuracy & ASR \\
\midrule
42 & $0.783$ & $0.766$ & $0.774$ & $0.837$ \\
43 & $0.788$ & $0.783$ & $0.774$ & $0.792$ \\
44 & $0.794$ & $0.835$ & $0.784$ & $0.828$ \\
45 & $0.762$ & $0.889$ & $0.771$ & $0.931$ \\
46 & $0.780$ & $0.763$ & $0.766$ & $0.823$ \\
\midrule
Mean $\pm$ std & $0.781 \pm 0.011$ & $\mathbf{0.807 \pm 0.048}$ & $0.774 \pm 0.006$ & $\mathbf{0.842 \pm 0.047}$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Composition partially suppresses scaling (ASR $0.807$ vs.\ pure NormClip $\sim 0.95$, and $0.961$ under the NE3 temporal-mixing policy defined in §\ref{app:dba_f04}) but does not approach pure reputation ($0.017$). Norm clipping preserves the attack direction while reducing magnitude; the clipped update then passes reputation's consensus-distance filter. Pixel attacks are similarly persistent under composition (ASR $0.842$). Both attack families remain effective despite simultaneous application of both defenses.

\subsection{DBA at $f{=}0.4$: per-seed details}
\label{app:dba_f04}

\textbf{Three run labels are carried over from an earlier framing and used here only to identify runs}, not to make any equilibrium claim: \emph{NE3} is the mixed server policy that framing computed, which randomizes over defenses across rounds; \emph{reputation eq} is the pure-reputation policy; and \emph{BR} is the adversary's best response to the policy it faces. NE3 BR on CIFAR-10 with DBA in place of model scaling at $f{=}0.4$ (raising adversarial co-participation to $K \cdot f = 2.0$ from the $f{=}0.2$ value of $1.0$). $N{=}10$, $K{=}5$, 5 seeds 42--46, 50 rounds. Per-seed realized ASRs:

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{c|cc}
\toprule
seed & DBA realized ASR & backdoor\_pixel realized ASR \\
\midrule
42 & 0.222 & 0.972 \\
43 & 0.103 & 0.945 \\
44 & 0.176 & 0.913 \\
45 & 0.603 & 0.993 \\
46 & 0.077 & 0.934 \\
\midrule
mean ± std & $0.236 \pm 0.190$ & $0.952 \pm 0.028$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

The DBA per-seed spread is $7.8\times$ between min and max; only seed 45 clearly persists, three (0.08, 0.10, 0.18) are below the threshold ($\geq 0.20$) we would call active persistence, and one (0.22) is borderline. Pixel persists cleanly at $f{=}0.4$ ($0.952 \pm 0.028$), so the persistence mechanism survives an adversarial-fraction shift on the original attack. DBA's seed-variability reflects per-round co-participation alignment (whether $\geq 2$ of the 4 adversarial clients land in the same round to coordinate the distributed trigger) rather than the structural argmax-collapse mechanism we describe for scaling. The cleanest evidence for the headline persistence claim therefore remains model scaling; DBA at $f{=}0.4$ is suggestive but not a robust second-attack demonstration.

\subsection{DBA at $f{=}0.6$: per-seed details and bimodality reproduction}
\label{app:dba_f06}

Follow-up to Appendix~\ref{app:dba_f04}, designed to test whether the DBA bimodality at $f{=}0.4$ is purely a per-round co-participation effect or whether it persists as a per-seed admissibility latent at higher $f$. At $f{=}0.6$, expected adversarial co-participation per round is $K \cdot f = 3.0$, making $\geq 2$ adversarial co-participation per round near-certain. NE3-style BR with committed adversary playing DBA; 5 seeds (42--46), 50 rounds, $N{=}10$, $K{=}5$, \texttt{cifar\_cnn}. Two deployed policies: \emph{NE3 mix} (FedAvg $26\%$ + NormClip $74\%$) and the \emph{reputation equilibrium} (NormClip $83\%$ + reputation $17\%$).

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{c|cc}
\toprule
seed & DBA ASR (NE3 mix) & DBA ASR (reputation eq) \\
\midrule
42 & 0.360 & 0.339 \\
43 & 0.295 & 0.278 \\
44 & 0.386 & 0.415 \\
45 & \textbf{0.734} & \textbf{0.705} \\
46 & 0.154 & 0.130 \\
\midrule
mean $\pm$ std & $0.386 \pm 0.192$ & $0.373 \pm 0.190$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\textbf{Observations.} (i) Mean DBA ASR improves over $f{=}0.4$ ($0.236$) to $\sim 0.38$ on both policies, but does not reach the $\geq 0.85$ persistence band scaling achieves. (ii) Per-seed bimodality persists at $f{=}0.6$: seed 45 alone reaches $> 0.7$ in both policies; the remaining four seeds cluster in $[0.13, 0.42]$. (iii) Crucially, \emph{the same seed is the strong-persistence seed under both policies} (NE3 mix: 0.734; reputation eq: 0.705), and \emph{the same four seeds are the weak-persistence seeds under both}: the per-seed cross-policy correlation rules out per-round co-sampling alignment as the dominant source of bimodality. The bimodality is a per-seed training-initialization latent in DBA itself: whether the distributed trigger embeds at all in the early rounds. This is the DBA analogue of the model-scaling binding-cell Bernoulli, but at a much lower mean (admissibility prevalence $\approx 1/5$ for DBA at $f{=}0.6$ vs.\ $\approx 4/5$ for scaling at $f{=}0.2$).

\textbf{Pre-specified verdict: INTERMEDIATE.} The four-way rule that emits it, STRONG then PARTIAL then NEGATIVE then INTERMEDIATE with its thresholds, is in \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak run\_dba\_f06.py}, so the label is decided by the runner rather than chosen after reading the table. Overall mean $0.380$ falls between PARTIAL ($> 0.5$) and NEGATIVE ($< 0.3$). Reading: DBA at $f{=}0.6$ does \emph{not} replicate the persistence collapse with the cleanness scaling achieves; the trigger-alignment mechanism has its own admissibility constraint we do not control by raising $f$. The cleanest headline-persistence evidence remains model scaling under norm-based menus, as acknowledged in L3.

\section{Scale, Architecture, and External Validity}
\label{app:scale_external}

\subsection{Scale Validation ($N=100$) and Defense-Inference Check}
\label{app:scale}
\label{app:inference}

\textbf{Scale check at $N=100$ ($4{\times}5$ game, 5 seeds).} \textbf{Residual material from an earlier framing, recorded rather than dropped.} Nothing in the screen, the identification result or the dose--response evidence depends on it. Write VoPD (value of policy diversity) for the gain the server's equilibrium strategy achieves over its best \emph{pure} defense, so VoPD~$=0$ exactly when the equilibrium is pure. \textbf{This is not the quantity an earlier draft called ``realized VoPD'', and that draft described its own quantity with the bound direction inverted, which is worth recording rather than quietly dropping.} There the reported difference was oracle-minus-committed, and it was said to \emph{lower}-bound the gap an adversary with no information about the server's draw could realize. It does the opposite: the committed policies are a \emph{subset} of that adversary's available policies, so its best policy is at least as good as the best committed one, and subtracting the larger quantity makes oracle-minus-committed an \emph{upper} bound on the gap. That quantity is withdrawn with its framing, nothing in this paper uses it, and the VoPD defined above is a different object, non-negative by construction and sharing only the name. The equilibrium is pure on seeds 43 to 46 (model scaling vs.\ RFA, VoPD~$=0$) and mixed on seed 42 (pixel + scaling vs.\ FedAvg + RFA, VoPD~$= 0.012$); per-seed values in Table~\ref{tab:scale100}.

\toprule
Seed & Type & Adversary & Server & VoPD \\
\midrule
42      & Mixed & Pixel + Scaling & FedAvg + RFA & 0.012 \\
43--46  & Pure  & Model scaling   & RFA          & 0.000 \\

\subsection{ResNet18 architecture-replication mini-sweep: per-seed details}
\label{app:resnet18_replication_details}

Per-seed details for the ResNet18 architecture-replication mini-sweep behind the architecture-conditional collapse boundary in §\ref{sec:limitations}. \texttt{resnet18} with BatchNorm replaced by GroupNorm (8 groups), conv1 adapted to $32\times 32$ (kernel $3$, stride $1$, maxpool $\to$ Identity); 11.2M parameters. Identical FL parameters to cifar\_cnn: $N{=}10$, $K{=}5$, $f{=}0.2$, $50$ rounds, $\tau=5$. Committed-scaling BR, 3 mix points $\times$ 5 seeds = 15 runs at $\sim 100$ min/run on MPS. \textbf{Reading the table.} \emph{mix (NC/rep)} is the server's per-round mixture over the two upstream defenses, \texttt{norm\_clip} (NC) and \texttt{reputation} (rep), so the $90/10$ row draws \texttt{norm\_clip} in $90\%$ of rounds and \texttt{reputation} in the other $10\%$; every cell is trigger ASR under the committed scaling attack; the two \emph{mean} columns are over the same five seeds, the second on \texttt{cifar\_cnn} from \texttt{results/}\allowbreak\texttt{cifar10\_mix\_ratio\_sweep/}\allowbreak\texttt{summary.json}, whose sweep carries five mix points of which the three run here are shown. Both spreads are population standard deviations over the five seeds, recomputed here from the per-seed values and not the Student-$t$ intervals the rest of the paper reports.

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{l|ccccc|c|c}
\toprule
mix (NC/rep) & s42 & s43 & s44 & s45 & s46 & ResNet18 mean & cifar\_cnn mean \\
\midrule
90/10 & 0.926 & 0.929 & 0.965 & 0.974 & 0.962 & $\mathbf{0.951 \pm 0.020}$ & $0.945 \pm 0.021$ \\
50/50 & 0.924 & 0.971 & 0.813 & 0.920 & 0.903 & $\mathbf{0.906 \pm 0.052}$ & $0.815 \pm 0.104$ \\
10/90 & 0.917 & 0.733 & 0.783 & 0.975 & 0.915 & $\mathbf{0.865 \pm 0.091}$ & $\mathbf{0.097 \pm 0.090}$ \\

\end{center}

NC-dominant endpoint replicates within $0.01$; mid-mix is $\sim 9\%$ higher on ResNet18; the reputation-dominant endpoint shows an order-of-magnitude gap (no CI overlap). Seed 45's accuracy collapses to $0.153$ at NC10/rep90 (the persistent attack effectively breaks the model) while trigger ASR remains $0.975$. \textbf{Verdict: MEDIUM} (monotonic ordering preserved, endpoint criterion fails), on the scale stated in \texttt{experiments/\allowbreak run\_mix\_ratio\_sweep\_resnet18.py}; that label name is reused with different meanings by other runners in this repository, which is why its criterion is restated here rather than referenced. The cifar\_cnn collapse boundary at $\sim 70$--$90\%$ reputation is therefore a property of the smaller-architecture regime. This sweep bounds the architecture-conditional claim of §\ref{sec:limitations} and establishes no boundary of its own; we draw no conclusion here about which mixture of the two defenses survives, since mixture policies are outside this paper's scope (§\ref{sec:composability_criterion}).

\section{Is randomization itself costly? A compute-matched, pre-registered test}
\label{app:randomization}

\textbf{What this is, and what it is not.} This is a new experiment, pre-registered in
\texttt{experiments/}\allowbreak\texttt{pre\_registration\_randomized\_composition.md} and committed at
\texttt{e3fb038} before the first run. It is \emph{not} a reinstatement of the randomized-menu comparison
§\ref{sec:related} withdraws: that artifact stays withdrawn, because it is unpaired and
compute-confounded, and nothing below is scored against it. The question here is narrower and answerable:
holding defense computation fixed, does randomizing \emph{which} composition is applied each round cost
suppression relative to committing to the best member of the same set? The direction was fixed in advance
as $H_1: \bar{\Delta} > 0$, randomization \emph{hurts}, because that is the claim the withdrawn
comparison made and the claim we were asked to substantiate.

\textbf{Three defects in the predecessor, and what each fix costs.} (a) \emph{The arms were not
seed-matched.} The old runner tested \texttt{randomize\_order and rng.random() < 0.5}, which
short-circuits, so the fixed arm never drew that number and the randomized arm consumed one extra draw
per round from the same generator that selects participants. The arms therefore ran on divergent
participant streams and no paired test was licensed. Fixed with a separate \texttt{policy\_rng}, leaving
\texttt{rng} for participants only, which is why every arm here was re-run rather than partly reused.
(b) \emph{A defense-count confound.} Every \texttt{CM}$\to$\texttt{FG} round reduces to FoolsGold alone,
so the old gap conflated randomization with dropping a stage. Every member of every randomized set here
keeps FoolsGold as a genuine per-client upstream, so no round degenerates. (c) \emph{A mixed-$n$ trap.}
Every leg of every contrast below is recomputed per seed from
\texttt{results/}\allowbreak\texttt{randomized\_composition/}\allowbreak\texttt{summary.json} at seeds
42--46; the analyzer prints both legs' $n$ on every row and marks any mismatch.

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{llcccc}
\toprule
arm & condition & def./rd & scaling ASR & pixel ASR & acc.\ (sc./px.) \\
\midrule
1 & \texttt{fixed\_fg\_cm} & 2 & $0.1068 \pm 0.0306$ & $0.0988 \pm 0.0201$ & $0.682/0.664$ \\
1 & \texttt{fixed\_fg\_rfa} & 2 & $0.0510 \pm 0.0096$ & $0.0452 \pm 0.0118$ & $0.499/0.495$ \\
1 & \texttt{fixed\_fg\_tm} & 2 & $0.3843 \pm 0.2594$ & $0.2495 \pm 0.1026$ & $0.694/0.705$ \\
2 & \texttt{rand\_comp\_strong} & 2 & $\mathbf{0.1522 \pm 0.1091}$ & $\mathbf{0.1115 \pm 0.0440}$ & $0.607/0.598$ \\
2 & \texttt{rand\_comp\_all3} & 2 & $0.2317 \pm 0.2780$ & $0.0879 \pm 0.0301$ & $0.623/0.635$ \\
3$^{\dagger}$ & \texttt{temporal\_mix} & 1 & $0.5712 \pm 0.3737$ & $0.6600 \pm 0.1543$ & $\mathbf{0.307}/0.765$ \\
4$^{\dagger}$ & \texttt{pure\_coord\_median} & 1 & $0.5186 \pm 0.1413$ & $0.4425 \pm 0.1290$ & $0.764/0.767$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\noindent All seven conditions, $n=5$ seeds 42--46, population sd. $^{\dagger}$\,\textbf{Compute matching
is claimed for arms 1--2 only}: arms 3 and 4 apply one defense per round and use roughly half the defense
computation, so they are reference points and not comparators. Arm 2's primary set is the \emph{smaller}
one, $\{$FG$\to$CM, FG$\to$RFA$\}$, whose members each suppress both committed attacks alone; FG$\to$TM
is a criterion-FAIL pair whose canonical scaling arm is $0.508 \pm 0.269$, above threshold, so a set
containing it could degrade merely because a non-suppressing member was drawn. That $0.508$ is the frozen
$n{=}3$ reading (seeds 42--44) and is \emph{not} the table's $0.3843$, which is the same arm at $n{=}5$;
the two agree exactly on the three shared seeds by control 3, and the difference is seeds 45--46 alone. Both sets were fixed by
rule in the pre-registration, not by any outcome measured here.

\textbf{Four positive controls, because this project has twice shipped a hook that silently never
fired.} (1) \emph{The hook fired}: every member of every randomized set appears in the stored per-round
policy log across all 15 streams. (2) \emph{The arms differ}: neither randomized arm is bit-identical to
any fixed arm at any seed, the closest approach being max per-seed $|d| = 0.2207$
(\texttt{rand\_comp\_strong}/scaling against \texttt{fixed\_fg\_cm}) and $0.0489$
(\texttt{rand\_comp\_all3}/pixel). (3) \emph{The harness did not change the run}: all eight per-seed
identity checks of the fixed arms against the canonical artifacts return $\max|d| = 0.000\mathrm{e}{+}00$
on their shared seeds, against \texttt{all\_compositions} (seeds 42--44) and \texttt{fg\_cm\_survivor}'s
\texttt{composed} (42--46). (4) \emph{The end-to-end path reproduces a published standalone}:
\texttt{pure\_coord\_median} returns $0.5186$ and $0.4425$ against
\texttt{wave2\_held\_out}'s \texttt{fedavg\_then\_coord\_median} at
$\max|d| = 0.000\mathrm{e}{+}00$, the same $n=5$ seeds. Controls 3 and 4 are what license comparing any
arm here to a number already in print.

\textbf{Control 1 fails as literally pre-registered, and we report it rather than restate it.} The
frozen assertion was a per-stream two-sided $99\%$ binomial interval with no family-wise correction. Three
of the 15 streams carry an excursion (four member-level flags):
\texttt{rand\_comp\_strong} seed 46 draws $36/14$ against $[16,34]$ (tail $P=0.00130$), and
\texttt{rand\_comp\_all3} draws $26$ at seed 45 and $27$ at seed 46 against $[8,25]$
($P=0.00492$, $P=0.00208$). Family-wise, $P(\text{at least one excursion}) = 1 - 0.99^{15} = 0.140$, so
excursions at this rate are expected. We do not treat this as invalidating, and say why: the failure mode
the control targets is a \emph{dead} hook, which yields a member with count $0$ or a $50/0$ log, and every
stream here draws every member. The pre-registered assertion is nonetheless the stricter one and it did
not hold on all streams; that is the honest statement. One further correction of the pre-registration's
own arithmetic: it wrote \texttt{rand\_comp\_all3}'s interval as approximately $8$--$26$, the exact
interval is $[8,25]$, and the exact one is used above.

\textbf{The primary contrast, paired per seed.} \texttt{rand\_comp\_strong} against the best member of
its own set, where ``best'' is fixed by rule as the lower full-artifact mean on that same attack, which
selects \texttt{fixed\_fg\_rfa} on both. On \emph{model scaling} the per-seed gaps are $+0.0352$,
$+0.2899$, $+0.1564$, $+0.0102$, $+0.0143$, giving $\bar{\Delta} = +0.1012$, $5/5$ concordant signs,
one-sided Wilcoxon $p=0.0312$ and paired $t=1.866$ ($p=0.0677$). On the \emph{pixel} backdoor they are
$+0.0816$, $+0.0428$, $+0.1581$, $+0.0133$, $+0.0356$, giving $\bar{\Delta} = +0.0663$, $5/5$ signs,
Wilcoxon $p=0.0312$ and $t=2.603$ ($p=0.0299$). At $n=5$ the minimum attainable Wilcoxon $p$ is
$1/32=0.031$, stated in advance, so only $5/5$ signs can reach $p<0.05$. Post hoc: the randomized arm
exceeds \emph{both} members, not just the better one, in $3$ of $5$ seeds on each attack, so the gap is
not interpolation between the two fixed arms. The secondary contrast, \texttt{rand\_comp\_all3} against
the best of all three arm-1 conditions, runs the same way: $\bar{\Delta} = +0.1806$ with $4/5$ signs on
scaling ($p=0.0625$) and $+0.0427$ with $4/5$ on pixel ($p=0.0625$).

\textbf{The pre-registered outcome: all three admissible outcomes obtained, on three different
objects.} Materiality required \emph{both} $\bar{\Delta} \geq 0.10$ and at least $4/5$ positive signs.
\emph{(i) Material, in the pre-registered direction, on model scaling}: $\bar{\Delta}=+0.1012$ with $5/5$
signs, so we state, scoped to this menu and this attack, that randomizing over non-degenerate
compositions costs suppression at matched defense computation. \emph{(ii) Not material on the pixel
backdoor}: $\bar{\Delta}=+0.0663$ clears the sign leg but not the magnitude leg, and the honest phrasing
at $n=5$ is that no material difference was detected and the smallest effect this design resolves is
$\approx 0.10$. \emph{(iii) The accuracy floor is breached by one arm}: \texttt{temporal\_mix} under model
scaling means $0.307$ clean accuracy against a frozen floor of $0.35$, so that cell is reported as
uninterpretable for ASR rather than compared, and every contrast involving it inherits the exclusion.
This is why arm 3 is a reference point in the table and carries no verdict.

\textbf{Three caveats, and what survives them.} First, $\bar{\Delta}$ clears the $0.10$ threshold by
$0.0012$ and is carried by two of five seeds ($+0.2899$ and $+0.1564$); on the three others the gap is
$\leq 0.036$. A threshold met by $1.2\%$ of itself is met, and it is also the weakest way a threshold can
be met, so we do not describe the scaling result as large. Second, the two tests disagree in opposite
directions across the two attacks: Wilcoxon reaches $p=0.0312$ on both while the $t$-test reaches
$p=0.0299$ on pixel, where materiality \emph{fails}, and only $p=0.0677$ on scaling, where it holds. The
stable part of the result is the sign structure, $10/10$ across both attacks, and not either magnitude.
Third, control 1 fails as literally pre-registered, as recorded above. What survives all three is the
weakest claim consistent with them and the one the design was built to license: at matched defense
computation and with the degeneracy confound removed, randomizing over a set of compositions did not
\emph{help} on either attack and was consistently worse on both, materially so on one. Nothing here
licenses a claim about randomized menus in general, about randomized \emph{internals} such as noise or
subsampling (L3), or about any menu but this one.

\textbf{And the attack is committed in both arms, which is the scope condition that bites hardest on what
this test can be read as answering.} Randomization's theoretical case is against an adversary that
\emph{adapts} to the composition: a menu the adversary cannot predict is a menu it cannot optimize against.
Both attacks here are fixed in advance, identical in every arm, and blind to the draw. So this arm bounds
the \emph{cost} of randomizing under a committed threat and measures none of the benefit randomization is
proposed for, and a favourable outcome would not have shown that benefit either. No adaptive-adversary test
of a randomized menu is run here or anywhere else in this paper: the criterion-aware adversary of
§\ref{sec:fg_rfa_flagship} adapts to a composition that is \emph{fixed} and known to it, which is the
opposite of the setting randomization is meant to deny an adversary, and building an adversary that adapts
to a distribution over menus is a separate experiment we do not run.

\end{document}
